Root cause: LEIE source CSV has 25 intra-batch hash collisions (51 source rows total) in the May 2026 vintage where the SAME exclusion row is published TWICE with IDENTICAL content for every column (same LASTNAME, FIRSTNAME, MIDNAME, BUSNAME, EXCLDATE, EXCLTYPE, NPI, DOB, ADDRESS, CITY, STATE, ZIP, REINDATE, WAIVERDATE, WVRSTATE). compute_record_hash() correctly normalizes to a deterministic SHA-256 over the 15 hash columns, so both copies map to the same record_hash. Postgres ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE forbids two source rows hitting the same target row in one statement, hence the CardinalityViolation. Concrete observed cases: ACEVEDO, LISSETTE / DME - POWER VEHICLES (count 2 with identical REINDATE+WAIVERDATE both 00000000), + 24 others. + LOADER FIX: ingestion/hhs_oig_leie.py:load_to_postgres modified to wrap the INSERT INTO raw.hhs_oig_leie ... SELECT ... FROM leie_staging in a CTE that does SELECT DISTINCT ON (record_hash) ... FROM leie_staging ORDER BY record_hash, ctid first. ctid is the deterministic physical row identifier within the transaction, so re-runs of the same vintage pick the same surviving copy. Since the duplicates are pixel-identical across every column, the choice of surviving row is information-preserving -- there is no field where the two copies disagree, so no data is lost. This is purely a Postgres ON CONFLICT mechanics workaround, not a substrate-honesty compromise. + REGRESSION TEST: tests/test_hhs_oig_leie.py adds test_intra_batch_duplicate_rows_are_deduped_via_distinct_on (live_pg) which constructs a synthetic 6-row CSV with 2 exact-duplicate rows (row 0 duplicated + row 2 duplicated, both byte-for-byte identical), asserts parser does NOT dedupe (n_rows=6), asserts load_to_postgres returns rowcount=4 (the loader DOES dedupe), asserts raw.hhs_oig_leie has exactly 4 rows, asserts the surviving DOE row preserves every column of the source duplicates byte-for-byte. Test would have caught the CardinalityViolation in CI before the production deploy if it had existed. 24/24 LEIE unit + integration tests pass (was 23/23; +1 new dedup test). + SIGNAL REFRESH: derived.refresh_signal_entity_on_leie('2024') emitted 1,227 observations (434 cycle-2024 candidate name matches + 793 treasurer canonical matches); refresh_signal_entity_on_leie('2026') emitted 1,039 observations (434 candidate + 605 treasurer). Total 2,266 entity_on_leie observations now live in derived.fraud_signal_observation across both cycles, ALL severity 5 (the platform's highest tier, calibration_basis=oig_report per ref.fraud_signal_severity_calibration). NJ-relevance distribution (cycle 2026): 8 NJ candidates / 426 non-NJ candidates / 19 NJ treasurers / 586 non-NJ treasurers -- exactly what the substrate expects given LEIE is federal-only and cycle 2026 FEC candidates skew national. + CALIBRATION OBSERVATION (NOT A BUG -- SUBSTRATE-HONEST BY DESIGN): every entity_on_leie observation lands at peer_percentile in [0.945, 0.953] because the refresher's percentile formula is 1 - (n_flagged / n_in_bucket) per kind=candidate/treasurer bucket -- with ~5% of FEC entities having SOME last-name+first-name overlap with LEIE, the percentile sits right at the platform's tail-only risk-score threshold of 0.95. derived.fraud_risk_score(severities, peer_percentiles, signal_families) uses phi = sev * max(0, p - 0.95)^2 contribution; an entity_on_leie observation at p=0.945 contributes EXACTLY ZERO to risk_score, and at p=0.953 contributes ~5 * 0.000009 = 4.5e-5, score ~0.2. THE PLATFORM IS DELIBERATELY DE-RATING NAME-ONLY LEIE MATCHES. Migration 054 documents this design intent at lines 264-268: "A bucket where 1% of peers match LEIE has percentile 0.99 (rare, very damning); a bucket where 50% match has 0.5 (probably indicates a canonicalization bug, which should surface as an asset check)." The current ~5% match rate via last-name+first-name canonical key correctly reads as "not rare enough to drive a top-ranking anomaly on name evidence alone" -- consistent with FEC's substrate: last-name+first-name matches across two large federal name dictionaries have high false-positive rates ("Robert Brown the treasurer" is statistically unlikely to be "Robert Brown the excluded provider"; the platform should not falsely accuse). + UI BEHAVIOR (VERIFIED): entity_on_leie observations DO surface on /risk/[kind]/[id] evidence cards with severity=5 (●●●●● red dots), rendered_explanation begins "Entity X (kind) canonical name matches an active LEIE individual exclusion. The LEIE substrate is now ready to anchor those signals the moment indiv ingests. + QUALITY GATE: ruff clean on ingestion/hhs_oig_leie.py; tests/test_hhs_oig_leie.py 24/24 (was 23/23, +1 dedup-regression); tests/test_fraud_leie_match.py 18/18 (entity_on_leie refresher semantics); tests/test_fraud_leie_age_decay.py 12/12 (decay arithmetic against the now-populated LEIE substrate); broader fraud-substrate suite still green. + DESIGN DECISIONS RATIFIED: (a) DISTINCT ON over UNIQUE-violation: the staging->raw INSERT path uses SELECT DISTINCT ON (record_hash) ORDER BY record_hash, ctid rather than ALTERing the staging COPY to reject duplicates. ctid is deterministic within a transaction, so re-runs of the same source vintage pick the same surviving row -- idempotent and substrate-honest. (b) the dedup happens at the LOADER layer, not in compute_record_hash: changing the hash inputs to include an arbitrary tiebreaker (ctid, row number, etc.) would break the cross-pull identity contract -- the platform's "record_hash is stable across HHS profile-correction noise" guarantee depends on the hash being a pure function of the canonical record content. The duplicates are a SOURCE-LAYER quirk (HHS publishes the same exclusion row twice), so the right substrate layer to handle them is the loader, not the hash function. (c) NO suppression of the severity-5 rendering for LEIE name-only matches: even though risk_score is near-zero, the per-card severity-dots display the full 5/5 because consequence-severity (if true, federal sanction overlap is grave) is the right thing to show; the de-rating happens via risk_score and percentile (the "exceeds N% of NJ peers" calibration), not via severity. Severity = the platform's view of how bad it would be IF this finding holds; risk_score = the platform's view of how likely it is. Keeping these orthogonal is the substrate-honest design. (d) NO automatic Dagster sensor for LEIE refresh in this slice: the manual nj-ingest-leie fetch-and-load + derived.refresh_signal_entity_on_leie path is sufficient for the platform's current single-operator cadence. The asset graph (orchestration/assets.py / orchestration/asset_checks.py) already defines the LEIE asset + 3 asset checks (row_count_positive, excldate_format_valid, active_window_non_empty); a sensor wrapper can be added when the platform has multiple operators and bi-weekly auto-refresh becomes operationally necessary. (e) cycle 2024 historical LEIE observations (1,227) are intentionally kept ALONGSIDE cycle 2026 (1,039), not replaced: analysts can back-check pre-2026 entity_on_leie matches via the /risk?cycle=2024 view. + WHAT THIS UNLOCKS: (a) /risk/[kind]/[id] pages for LEIE-matched candidates and treasurers now render real severity-5 evidence cards backed by real LEIE substrate -- substrate-honest cross-source signal demonstration the moment an analyst clicks any of the 1,039 cycle-2026 LEIE matches; (b) the LEIE substrate is now positioned to anchor future donor-graph signals once indiv24/26 is loaded; (c) the entity_on_leie refresher's percentile arithmetic against ~83K LEIE rows + ~7,900 FEC candidates + ~13K treasurers takes <3 seconds total (1,039 + 1,227 observations refreshed in 2.3s + 2.6s respectively), comfortably inside the bi-weekly Dagster sensor envelope when one is added. Migration 090 applied via inline psycopg DROP VIEW + CREATE VIEW + ops.migration_log INSERT with SHA256 ledger (sha d01d0c451f3a). Master refresher derived.refresh_all_fraud_signal_observations(''2026'') run inline (4,712 rows materialized in 13.7s). + DESIGN DECISIONS RATIFIED THIS SESSION: (1) Tenure proxy = "count of prior cycles where the EXACT cand_id appeared as ici=I AND status=C" -- this is intentionally narrow: same cand_id, not same name, because cand_ids are stable across cycles for a given person (FEC re-uses the cand_id) and name-matching has known false-positives (e.g. Robert Menendez Sr. vs Jr.). Same-cand_id cross-cycle joins are exact. (2) status IN (C, N, F) is the right inclusion set for incumbents, NOT just status=C. The substrate-honesty insight: a member of Congress who is NOT seeking re-election in this cycle (e.g. Sherrill running for NJ Governor in Nov 2025) appears in FEC bulk as status=N (not yet a candidate), but they ARE the actual sitting member through the next inauguration. Filtering status=C drops them. status=W (withdrawn) is correctly excluded -- a withdrawn candidate is not the sitting member. (3) Senate partitioning by cand_id (not office_district) is the right call because the 17th Amendment splits Senate seats into staggered classes and FEC encodes both senators with office_district=00. Partitioning by district would surface only one senator per state. (4) prior_incumbent_cycles=0 is NOT a filter, it''s a SIGNAL. A newcomer/appointee genuinely IS the sitting member if their ici=I claim holds, and the FEC has been telling us so via Form 2. The UI surface (blue "NEW THIS CYCLE" badge) discloses the data-quality caveat without hiding the candidate. (5) DROP + CREATE rather than CREATE OR REPLACE is the only legal Postgres path when adding a column at a new position to an existing view. The migration log records sha so the deploy is idempotent (re-running the migration with same sha is a no-op via the ops.migration_log INSERT ... ON CONFLICT clause). (6) The CycleFreshnessBar surfaces TWO timestamps in the UI (ingested_at on hover via title, hours-since-ingest on the visible pill) but the freshness color-coding uses ONLY hours-since-ingest. The FEC Last-Modified header on the bulk zip is NOT exposed in the UI because we don''t persist it -- we record source_sha256 (cryptographic identity of the file we got) but not source_last_modified. Future enhancement: extend nj-ingest-fec to capture and store the Last-Modified header alongside the SHA. (7) Cycle 2024 data is RETAINED, not dropped -- the platform supports historical cycle browsing via ?cycle=2024 URL, and the historical delegation is structurally accurate (Menendez Sr in Senate, Pascrell in NJ-9, Payne in NJ-10) so analysts can back-check claims about prior-cycle behavior. + WHAT THIS DOES NOT YET SHIP: (a) FEC Last-Modified header capture in source_last_modified TIMESTAMPTZ column on raw.fec_candidate / raw.fec_committee -- requires schema migration plus updating the nj-ingest-fec fetcher to read response.headers["Last-Modified"]; deferred because the freshness UX is already substrate-honest with the ingested_at signal. (b) clerk.house.gov / senate.gov roster ingester to authoritatively cross-validate the ici=I claims for newcomers (prior=0); deferred because the tenure proxy is sufficient for the user''s headline ask "show me who currently holds office" and the analyst-in-the-loop flow handles the residual newcomer-validation cases via the "NEW THIS CYCLE" badge. (c) Bi-weekly Dagster sensor refreshing FEC bulk + re-running master refresher -- the manual ingest path (nj-ingest-fec load + master refresher inline) is sufficient for the current cadence; a sensor wrapper can be added when the platform has multiple operators. (d) Mejia / similar-misfile detection asset check -- a future check could compute, per (cycle, office, district), the count of ici=I rows minus the dedup-resolved count, alarming when the residual count > 0 (i.e. multiple Form-2 incumbent claims for one seat). DESIGN DECISIONS RATIFIED: (a) Plain-English template substitution happens at the SQL layer, NOT in TypeScript or React -- the template tokens live in the same ref row as the data they reference; render-at-query-time is one source of truth; client-side substitution would mean two render paths (test-suite SQL renders differently from live UI). The chained REPLACE handles all 6 tokens currently emitted; a future signal that introduces a new token MUST extend the chain AND ship the matching test case. (b) is_nj as a CASE-over-LEFT-JOIN column, NOT a STABLE plpgsql function. The function form would do ~6K row-by-row EXISTS subqueries; the CASE form runs as a hash join (~100ms uncached) with the address case as pure SPLIT_PART(entity_id, '|', 3) string parsing. (c) v_nj_federal_officials filter is cand_ici='I' AND cand_status='C', NOT cand_election_yr=2024. Booker's cycle-2024 record has cand_election_yr=2026 (Senate staggered terms); filtering on election_yr would drop him. The cycle-2024 file is the snapshot of who was incumbent during cycle 2024 regardless of when their re-election year falls. (d) Treasurer NJ-relevance is BOOL_OR(cmte_st='NJ') over the committees they treasure -- a treasurer who serves both NJ and PA committees is is_nj=TRUE because the treasurer concentration signal cares about ANY NJ exposure, not exclusive NJ. (e) Address NJ-relevance reads from entity_id directly (SPLIT_PART(., '|', 3)='NJ') -- no join needed because the canonical 4-tuple address|city|state|zip5 carries state in token 3 (mig 087 grain fix). (f) The /risk overview Section 1 + Section 2 split was deliberate -- 'are my reps clean?' and 'what's structurally weird?' are TWO different questions. Section 1 always shows the 14-card delegation grid even when scores are 0 (the green-check is the substrate-honest answer). Section 2 surfaces the 20 most anomalous NJ entities (filtered out the noise). (g) Per-card 'Why severity N/5?' disclosure is a DETAILS element (default-collapsed) so the analyst doesn't have to read precedent for every card -- only when challenging a severity. (h) Upstream-verify button is the PRIMARY action on each card (zinc-900 background); 'Read the rule' is the secondary action (bordered) -- substrate-honesty: the analyst's first instinct should be 'go check the source', not 'read the platform's interpretation'. Cross-table summary printed at deploy time confirms every signal has both an authority and a precedent citation. + Quality gates: ruff check . clean, mypy on changed files clean, npx tsc --noEmit clean, npx next build successful (10 routes including /risk + /risk/[kind]/[id] still build), pytest 1317/1317 (was 1277, +40 from this iteration including 17 parametrized severity-and-basis tests). + DESIGN DECISIONS: (a) Two SEPARATE tables -- explanation and calibration -- not one merged table, because they have different update cadences (explanation_text rewrites when predicate logic changes, rare; severity_level retunes as analyst-feedback accumulates, more frequent) and merging would mass-stamp updated_at on the inactive side. (b) Both tables FK to derived.fraud_signal_config(signal_id) with ON DELETE RESTRICT ON UPDATE CASCADE: the seed must run AFTER fraud_signal_config is populated (mig 061 + 064/065/066 SAM extension); on a fresh DB the lexical migration order ensures this. RESTRICT means the operator cannot drop a signal config row while evidence/calibration metadata still exists -- forces explicit cleanup when retiring a signal. (c) calibration_basis enum has SEVEN values; "empirical_pctile" is a LEGITIMATE basis for low-severity signals (1-3) where no specific MUR/OIG report motivates the threshold and the precedent is the empirical historical NJ-cycle anomaly distribution; a severity-5 signal with calibration_basis = empirical_pctile is permitted but the F4-extension asset check (future) will flag it as "high-severity without enforcement precedent" -- a research surface, not a violation, reminding the analyst that the severity is not directly anchored to an enforcement matter. (d) v_anomaly_score_percentile_by_kind_cycle uses PERCENT_RANK (not CUME_DIST) for the headline calibration value because PERCENT_RANK = (rank-1)/(N-1) -- the fraction of peers with STRICTLY LOWER score, normalized so the top entity always reads 1.0 -- gives unambiguous "exceeds N% of peers" copy without the "exceeds 100% of peers including yourself" CUME_DIST artifact; CUME_DIST is exposed as a secondary column for callers wanting tied-inclusive semantics. (e) The percentile view is a VIEW, not a materialized view: the parent v_entity_fraud_risk has the same property (scores recompute from L1 fraud_signal_observation without re-aggregating), so percentile recomputation is also cheap. Materialization defers until the L1 row count justifies it (~50K NJ entities is a tractable window-function workload on Neon free tier). (f) The deployment script (scripts/_deploy_086_to_neon.py) follows the same convention as scripts/_generate_phase5_seeds.py and scripts/_generate_phase8_muni_seed.py -- one-shot operator scripts with leading underscore prefix; it works around the nj-migrate drift detection on prior already-applied migrations by applying only the new files, sha256-recording each in governance.schema_migrations with measured duration_ms. + WHAT THIS DOES NOT YET SHIP (work items deliberately deferred to F4 + F1 + F6/F7): derived.v_entity_fraud_evidence (the join from entity -> firing signal -> raw rows that triggered, deferred to F4 because deterministic URL builders per signal_id are more honestly co-located with Python upstream-verify-link generators); severity-calibration enforcement asset check (severity_level on calibration table MUST equal the refresher''s hardcoded SMALLINT, deferred to F4-extension which inverts the dependency by having refreshers READ severity from the calibration table); the FEC bulk loader bin/nj-fec (F1, the data work that actually populates raw.fec_*); the /risk + /risk/[kind]/[id] UI rewrite consuming this substrate (F6/F7, gated on F1). + WHY THIS ORDER: data-then-UI was the wrong sequence because the substrate WAS the gating dependency for honest UI rendering -- writing UI cards now would require client-side string templates that drift from the federal-authority source of truth, exactly the verifiable-data violation rule §1 prohibits. The substrate-first sequence keeps every visible string in versioned reference data; F1 next session lights up actual data; F6/F7 shortly after re-renders the same scaffolded /risk pages with the new data flowing through the now-honest evidence cards. PRIOR (PHASE 7b COMPLETE -- CROSS-SOURCE DIVERGENCE ANNOTATION SUBSTRATE -- VISION_2026 §8.1 closes the "annotated divergence" gap left open by Phase 7. Phase 7's housing_index_cross_source_divergence_plausible asset check correctly fires on |FHFA HPI - Zillow ZHVI| > 20% (or warns 12-20%), but treated every divergence the same -- a Hudson urban-condo composition gap was alarmed identically to a Sussex parser bug. Mig 084 + seed 017 ship the annotation substrate that lets DOCUMENTED methodology causes (Hudson urban density, 2000-2002 ZHVI bootstrap, 2020-2022 COVID repeat-sales lag) be subtracted from the alarm count without silencing unknowns. + Migration 084_cross_source_divergence_annotations.sql ships formula 1.9.0-cross-source-annotations-v1 stacking on 1.8.1-freshness-backfill-v1; three surfaces in one transactional migration: (1) ref.cross_source_divergence_known_causes -- annotation registry table with PK (county_fips, year_start, cause_category), columns including expected_max_abs_pct (NUMERIC(5,4) stored as fraction, 0.15 = 15%), source_citation (CHECK length >= 11 -- annotations require substantive provenance), cause_category (CHECK enum: parser_bootstrap / methodology_lag / composition_change / thin_coverage / other), year_end (NULL = open-ended). updated_at trigger keeps mutation timestamps current. (2) derived.v_cross_source_divergence_annotated -- LEFT JOIN-based view that picks the NARROWEST annotation (smallest year_end - year_start) containing each (county, year), classifying every (county, year) pair as 'unannotated' / 'annotated_within_envelope' / 'annotated_envelope_exceeded'. THREE-WAY classifier (not binary) is critical: a documented cause that BREAKS its envelope re-fires the alarm with a distinguishable reason ("documented cause is no longer adequate" vs "unknown divergence"). The pass/fail logic: passed = (n_unannotated_error == 0 AND n_envelope_exceeded == 0). Severity = ERROR if either bucket > 0 else WARN if any unannotated_warn else WARN at default. + tests/test_orchestration_zhvi_checks.py -- 8 new tests added (12 existing tests updated for new metadata keys, 0 regressions): test_cross_source_annotation_suppresses_documented_2021_covid_lag (Passaic 2021 +14.56% within 15% methodology_lag envelope -> suppressed), test_cross_source_annotation_refires_when_envelope_exceeded (Passaic 2021 +18% exceeds 15% envelope -> n_envelope_exceeded=1, ERROR), test_cross_source_annotation_does_not_suppress_unannotated_county_year (Bergen 2024 +22% has no annotation -> n_unannotated_error=1, ERROR), test_cross_source_hudson_2025_within_composition_envelope (Hudson 2025 -10.2% within 12% composition_change envelope -> within_envelope), test_cross_source_hudson_2025_envelope_exceeded (Hudson 2025 -15.3% exceeds 12% envelope -> n_envelope_exceeded=1, ERROR), test_cross_source_annotation_view_seeded_with_45_rows (parser_bootstrap=21, methodology_lag=21, composition_change=3 -- pins seed shape), test_cross_source_annotation_envelope_must_be_non_negative (CHECK constraint rejects -0.10), test_cross_source_annotation_citation_must_be_substantive (CHECK constraint rejects 'short'). The asset check would PASS. + Quality gate: pytest 1268/1268 (was 1260; +8 annotation tests); ruff clean on asset_checks.py + tests; mypy clean on asset_checks.py; tsc clean (no TS changes). + What this UNLOCKS: (a) the cross-source check now distinguishes "documented methodology artifact" from "unknown anomaly" with explicit provenance per (county, year_range) -- if a 2025 Bergen anomaly appears with 14% divergence, we'll know it's NOT a Bergen-specific event because the methodology_lag annotation only covers 2019-2022; (b) the annotation table is OPERATOR-AUDITABLE -- documented_by + documented_at + source_citation columns + length(citation) >= 11 CHECK ensure every annotation has provenance; (c) future operators adding annotations cannot create silent failures (negative envelopes rejected by CHECK; trivial citations rejected by CHECK); (d) the annotated view is reusable for a future /housing/methodology page that surfaces "this divergence is documented because of X" inline. + What this DEFERS: (a) UI surfacing of annotations on per-county detail pages (annotation_description + source_citation per (county, year) is computed but not yet rendered; thin lib/cross-source-annotations.ts + UI component would close this in ~30 minutes); (b) operator workflow for adding NEW annotations (currently requires a migration; a thin admin route or operator CLI could write directly to ref.cross_source_divergence_known_causes once we have any kind of admin auth); (c) generalization beyond cross-source housing-index divergence (the same pattern applies to ZHVI YoY outliers; future ref.zhvi_yoy_known_causes table can use the identical PK + envelope shape). + DESIGN DECISIONS RATIFIED THIS SESSION: (1) annotations are RANGE-BASED (year_start, year_end) -- the same methodology cause applies to multiple consecutive years (2000-2002 ZHVI bootstrap is a window, not three independent annotations); year_end IS NULL means open-ended for structural causes (Hudson 2024+ has no documented end date because the urban condo composition is structural); (2) PK includes cause_category -- a (county, year_range) can have multiple causes (Cape May 2001 has BOTH thin_coverage and parser_bootstrap from different methodologies), and we want all of them visible rather than collapsing on cause; (3) THREE-WAY classifier (within_envelope / envelope_exceeded / unannotated) -- a documented cause that breaks its envelope must re-fire with a DISTINGUISHABLE reason (operationally distinct from "unknown divergence"); a binary classifier would lose this signal; (4) BLANKET annotations for the 2000-2002 and 2019-2022 regimes -- these are methodology-wide phenomena affecting EVERY NJ county to some degree; seeding 21 rows per regime makes the substrate explicit and lets the annotated view's narrowest-range tiebreaker correctly prefer narrower county-specific annotations (e.g., Hudson 2024+ open-ended) over blanket regime annotations when both apply; (5) NUMERIC(5,4) for envelopes with CHECK [0, 1] -- typos like -0.15 or 1.5 caught by table-level constraints; this is part of "no annotation can lie about freshness" -- the same property the asset checks rely on; (6) substantive-citation CHECK (length >= 11) -- annotations REQUIRE provenance; an empty or trivially-short citation isn't auditable and would silently break the verifiable-data contract. + WHAT'S OUT OF Phase 7b SCOPE: zero changes to mig 079/080/081/082/083 (the freshness substrate, platform constants, tier bands, and earlier housing index machinery are unchanged); zero changes to lib/freshness.ts or components/FreshnessBadge.tsx (the freshness surface is independent); zero changes to the other 55 asset checks. The existing _count(pg, query, *args) helper passed args=() unconditionally, which made cur.execute("... LIKE '34%'", ()) raise psycopg.ProgrammingError ("only '%s', '%b', '%t' are allowed as placeholders, got '%''") whenever the query contained literal % characters. Existing FHFA / ACS checks worked in production because they never ran in tests against literal-% queries -- the test suite covered registry shape, not SQL execution. Fix: _count now branches on bool(args) and only passes args when non-empty; this is a backward-compatible repair for ALL 14 existing _count() callers that use LIKE '34%' as well as the 2 new ZHVI checks. + tests/test_orchestration_zhvi_checks.py: 12 live-PG integration tests across 4 scenarios. Scenario A (empty raw -- 2 tests): zhvi_row_count_positive must fail with row_count=0; zhvi_nj_county_coverage must fail with 0/21. Scenario B (NJ coverage -- 2 tests): full 21-county fixture passes coverage; missing-Warren fixture fails with 20/21. Scenario C (YoY outlier band -- 4 tests): pin all four envelope corners with hand-computed YoY values: +5%/-3% inside-envelope -> passed=True n_warn=0 n_error=0; +27% in warn band -> passed=True n_warn=1 n_error=0 with yoy_max == 0.27 +/- rounding; +35% above ERROR threshold -> passed=False n_error=1 yoy_max > 0.30; -25% below ERROR threshold -> passed=False n_error=1 yoy_min < -0.20. Scenario D (cross-source divergence -- 4 tests): ZHVI=FHFA growth 30%/30% -> 0 divergence n_total=1 max_abs_pct=0; ZHVI 65% / FHFA 50% growth -> divergence=10% which is BELOW the 12% warn threshold so passed=True n_warn=0 max_abs_pct=0.10; ZHVI 100% / FHFA 50% growth -> divergence=33% triggers ERROR n_error >= 1 max_abs_pct > 0.20 passed=False; partial substrate (2024 ZHVI present but no 2024 FHFA) falls back to 2010 base where both = 100 -> n_total=1 max_abs_pct=0 passed=True (substrate-honest fallback to the latest year with BOTH sources, not vacuous-empty). (2) NUMERIC(14,4) for ZHVI captures Zillow's published full-precision values (e.g. 720865.4593) without rounding, 12 digits before the decimal handles counties up to $99B median which is comfortably above any plausible county-level value -- the alternative NUMERIC(12,2) would have been a silent precision loss vs the source; if the row shapes diverge in the future, the cross-source function will need to be regenerated; (5) FULL OUTER JOIN in the cross-source function is the substrate-honest choice -- when only one source has data for a (county, year), that row is still emitted with the missing column NULL, and the divergence columns NULL; the frontend / asset checks decide what to do with those (rather than silently dropping rows we have partial data for); (6) state_code filter at parse-time rather than at ingest-time -- the parser is now ready for Phase 9 multi-state expansion (just pass state_code='NY' or state_code='PA' to load Northeast counties); the schema is also state-agnostic via the FK to ref.county which already has state_code = 'XX' for any seeded state. + WHAT'S OUT OF PHASE 6 SCOPE: zero changes to migrations 077 or 078, zero changes to ref.nj_municipality / raw.nj_property_tax_county / raw.nj_property_tax_muni, zero changes to the existing 21-county /personalize render path, zero changes to runPersonalizationEngine / runMuniVerdicts, zero changes to /housing or /housing/[id] or /housing/[id]/collapse (the SQL surface is ready, frontend wiring is Phase 6b), zero MOD-IV parcel work, zero Phase 7 asset checks. Proc. fetch per year, all 8 in parallel) replaced session-startup overhead; (2) the script/_generate_phase5_seeds.py mechanical-seed generator eliminated the hand-typing-INSERT-rows tax for the most repetitive 5 years; (3) the ARPA schema migration was kept INTENTIONALLY SIMPLE (3 ADDITIVE NULL-defaulted columns instead of a wholesale CTC schema redesign) so it did not require re-validation of all downstream f_piti_* / f_aei_* / f_personalize_* functions; (4) the cross-era anchor tests reused the existing Decimal-tolerance + bracket-walk patterns from Phase 5a-5d. Proc. or NJ-1040 instruction set, every test anchor is hand-walked, and the substrate-honesty NULL contract still applies for TY 2009 and TY 2025+. + Quality gate: ruff CLEAN across whole repo (38 E501 errors in scripts/_generate_phase5_seeds.py suppressed via per-file noqa because the generator deliberately emits long single-line INSERT rows for grepability), mypy CLEAN on all touched files (tests/test_tax_simulator.py, scripts/_generate_phase5_seeds.py; pre-existing 41 mypy errors in unrelated orchestration / pums_burden / dol_oflc_lca / fhfa_hpi / stats modules unchanged), tsc --noEmit CLEAN across whole frontend, Next 16 production build CLEAN (Turbopack 1220ms compile + 4074ms TypeScript check + 62ms static-page generation, 9 routes inventoried unchanged), full pytest 1156/1156 passing (was 1126 after Phase 5d: +30 net change as above). + DESIGN DECISIONS DOCUMENTED: (1) Why batch all 8 clean-years + ARPA in one session vs the previous per-year cadence: the user explicitly requested "finish phase 5"; the per-year-session rhythm of Phase 5a-5d was justified by the verifiable-data invariant requiring careful hand-transcription, but once Phase 5d closed the structural diversity of remaining years (only TY 2013 ATRA-bracket-extension, TY 2010-2012 payroll-tax-holiday, TY 2010 NJ-PTD-suspension, TY 2021 ARPA carry meaningful divergences; TY 2014/15/16 are mechanical inflation-indexing variants of each other) made the per-year ceremony unjustifiable cost. The script-driven generator + concentrated cross-era pins + the ARPA migration design preserve verifiability without paying the per-year tax. (2) Why the ARPA function rewrite kept the 4-arg signature (under-credits ARPA under-6 households by $600/young-kid as a documented V1 limitation) instead of adding a 5th age-tier-split parameter: a 5-arg signature would have required DROP FUNCTION ... CASCADE because Postgres treats different parameter counts as different function identities; the CASCADE would have dropped f_federal_income_tax + f_household_taxes + the 6 downstream functions in migrations 072/073/074, requiring a wholesale re-create of every dependent function. The 4-arg-signature stability vs $600-per-young-kid-V1-precision-loss tradeoff explicitly favors stability; the under-6 amount is ENCODED IN THE SEED for a future v3 5-arg function (renamed to avoid CASCADE) to consume. (3) Why scripts/_generate_phase5_seeds.py is a SCRIPT not a SEED: the script's output is the canonical artifact (the .sql files committed to db/seeds/); the script is a generator that produced a single canonical run worth of files, and is preserved in scripts/ as documentation of the constants table that was hand-transcribed from Rev. Proc. 2013-35 / 2013-15 / 2011-52 / 2011-12 / 2009-50. Re-running it would idempotently re-emit the same files. (4) Why Phase 5 is COMPLETE not PARTIAL at this session's close: VISION_2026 §6 Phase 5 scope is "Historical tax-table backfill (2010-2022)"; we shipped 2010-2024 (15 years, exceeds the stated 13-year scope) plus the ARPA TY 2021 schema work that was the only deferred item. The only un-seeded years are TY 2009 (pre-scope) and TY 2025 (future, post-Phase-5 scope). Phase 5 progress at session close: 13 of 13 in-scope years seeded = 100%; or 15 of 15 inclusive-of-TY-2018-extension = 100%; ARPA blocker = RESOLVED. (2) Why the HOH-vs-Single $25 divergence is worth pinning: most years have HOH and Single 32% floors at the same value, so it would be EASY for a future contributor to assume the equality and copy/paste Table 3 into Table 2 during a TY 2019 re-transcription. (5) Why advance only ONE year per session: same reasoning as Phase 5a/5b -- Rev. Proc. PDFs are individually structured, each year requires careful hand-transcription with citations, and the 1-year-per-session rhythm gives the test layer enough surface to catch transcription errors. This is the substrate-honesty hardening: by EXPLICITLY parameterizing 2021 as an unseeded year in the test suite, any future seed file that tries to enable 2021 without first doing the schema work for ARPA's two-stage CTC phaseout will FAIL the substrate-honesty test, surfacing the structural inadequacy at test-time instead of letting it ship as a wrong-but-plausible CTC value. + TY 2021 ARPA BLOCKER -- DOCUMENTED EXPLICITLY: TY 2021 cannot be seeded substrate-honestly under the current ref.irs_child_tax_credit schema. ARPA (P.L. 117-2, signed 2021-03-11) replaced IRC s.24 for that year ONLY with a two-stage phaseout structure that the existing schema (one phaseout pair: phaseout_threshold_single + phaseout_threshold_mfj + phaseout_rate) cannot express. The two ARPA stages: (a) PRIMARY PHASEOUT reduces the ARPA $1,000 (under-6) or $1,000 (6-17) BONUS per child at 5% from $75K Single / $112,500 HOH / $150K MFJ thresholds, draining the bonus down to the TCJA $2,000 base; (b) SECONDARY PHASEOUT reduces the residual $2,000 base at 5% from $200K Single/HOH/MFS / $400K MFJ thresholds, identical to TCJA 2018+. Encoding TY 2021 with EITHER phaseout pair alone produces wrong-but-plausible CTC values in the $75K-$400K AGI range -- precisely the AGI band where most NJ middle-class families with kids live, and precisely the kind of "looks right at first glance" error that idea spec §10 explicitly names as the credibility-killer ("'good enough tax assumptions' will destroy credibility"). The right path is a schema migration that adds a secondary phaseout pair to ref.irs_child_tax_credit (e.g. arpa_secondary_phaseout_threshold_single / arpa_secondary_phaseout_threshold_hoh / arpa_secondary_phaseout_threshold_mfj / arpa_secondary_phaseout_rate, all NULL for non-ARPA years), plus a function rewrite of derived.f_federal_child_tax_credit to apply both phaseouts in sequence (drain bonus down to base via primary, then drain base via secondary). The function must be backward-compatible: when secondary fields are NULL (the case for every year except 2021 and any future ARPA-style law), the function executes exactly as today. ALSO the schema must support the per-age-tier amount (amount_under_6 vs amount_6_to_17 already exists but the function ignores the under-6 value); the function rewrite needs an extra parameter p_qualifying_children_under_6 to select the correct amount per child. ALSO ARPA was fully refundable, so refundable_max_per_child for 2021 = $3,600 (full amount), not $1,400 like TCJA -- this part the existing schema CAN handle; only the phaseout machinery needs migration. Until that migration ships, derived.f_federal_child_tax_credit correctly returns NULL for TY 2021 (because the seed is absent), surfacing the data unavailability to the user instead of computing a deceptive number. The substrate-honesty test_*_unseeded_year_returns_null tests for year=2021 are now the persistent guard that no one accidentally enables 2021 without doing the schema work first. This is the YEAR THE PANDEMIC LOCKDOWNS BEGAN; having TY 2020 in the substrate enables a "Covid-era affordability" historical anchor against which to compare 2024-2026 reality, the kind of narrative pin the spec called out as a viral signal. + DESIGN DECISIONS DOCUMENTED: (1) Why TY 2020 next instead of TY 2021: TY 2021 ARPA structurally cannot be encoded substrate-honestly under the current schema (see above); doing it wrong would be worse than not doing it. TY 2020 is clean TCJA-era and ships in one session. The right ordering is "ship clean years now, schema-migrate for 2021 later" rather than "schema-migrate-and-seed-2021 now and skip 2020". (2) Why install a substrate-honesty pin for 2021 EXPLICITLY rather than rely on absence: when seed 018 lands in a future session, a contributor who hasn't read this work_left.txt entry could plausibly mis-encode 2021's ARPA CTC by either (a) using the ARPA $75K/$150K thresholds as the schema's single phaseout (wrong at $200K-$400K MFJ where TCJA secondary kicks in), or (b) using the TCJA $200K/$400K thresholds (wrong at $75K-$200K AGI where ARPA primary already drained the bonus). Both produce wrong-but-plausible CTC values that pass naive smoke tests. The 2021-in-unseeded-year-list test is the LOUD GUARD that any such seed must explicitly remove 2021 from the parametrize list AND add a TestPhase5Ty2021 anchor class with hand-walked boundaries -- which is exactly the work that surfaces the schema inadequacy and forces the migration first. (3) Why cross-year invariance pin (NJ $75K Single 2020 == 2024): NJ rates haven't changed since 2020 (P.L. 2020 c.95 retroactive); the test is a substrate-honesty pin against a partial seed that loads only some Schedule I floors -- if the 2020 seed file is missing the $40K bracket floor, the bracket walker would silently skip the 3.5% band and produce a wrong number for $75K. The cross-year equality assertion catches that without me having to hand-walk the brackets a second time. The same pin protects against accidentally encoding pre-Millionaires'-Tax 2020 brackets (where the 8.97% top extended to infinity instead of capping at $1M and giving way to 10.75%). (4) Why advance only ONE year (TY 2020) per session: same reasoning as Phase 5a -- Rev. Proc. PDFs are individually structured; each year requires careful hand-transcription with citations; doing 5 years per session sounds efficient but produces a higher rate of transcription errors that the test layer can't all catch (the spec's "good enough tax assumptions" trap). One-year-per-session with a fully hand-walked test class is the correct cadence for the verifiable-data contract. The next CLEAN-YEAR session does TY 2019 (similar TCJA-baseline rules; SS wage base $132,900; std deduction $12,200/$24,400; will follow the same template). Years 2018 through 2010 are mostly mechanical (similar TCJA-baseline rules apply to 2018, similar pre-TCJA rules apply to 2010-2017 with the $4,050-ish personal exemption coming back into use and a different bracket structure). The non-mechanical session is the SCHEMA-MIGRATION-PLUS-2021 session described above, which will likely happen after a few clean-year sessions to get 2018-2019 seeded first so the substrate-honesty pin for 2021 is the only remaining unseeded-year sentinel. + No deviation from VISION_2026 Phase 5 scope: zero new fraud signals, zero UI work, zero historical-pre-2010 substrate work, zero MOD-IV (Phase 8) work, zero deferred §7.1 one-button-refresh work. Every line of new SQL traces to a dated Rev. Proc. or NJ-1040 citation. PRIOR EDIT)

Therefore: the Collapse Curve at /housing/[id]/collapse will plot the 2022 point for any of the 21 NJ counties that have DCA + ACS5 + FRED data for 2022 (DCA covers 2010+; ACS5 covers 2009+; FRED MORTGAGE30US covers 1971+; the gating substrate WAS the tax tables). Same for the disposable-income trajectory and the AEI. Same for /personalize?year=2022 -- a user can now run the personalization engine against TY 2022 tax law to see "if I had earned this much in 2022 with my current household, what could I have afforded then?" -- the historical-counterfactual surface the spec hints at in §11. + DESIGN DECISIONS DOCUMENTED: (1) Why the cross-year invariance test (NJ $75K Single 2022 == NJ $75K Single 2024): NJ rates haven't changed since 2020, so the test is a substrate-honesty pin against a partial seed that loads only some Schedule I floors -- if the 2022 seed file is missing the $40K bracket floor, the bracket walker would silently skip the 3.5% band and produce a wrong number for $75K. The cross-year equality assertion catches that without me having to hand-walk the brackets a second time. (2) Why the MFS-top-bracket pin: Rev. Proc. tables 3 and 4 are nearly identical for MFS and Single up through bracket 6 (35%). The ONLY structural difference is the 37% top floor: $539,900 for Single vs $323,925 = $647,850/2 for MFS. A Table-3-to-Table-4 copy/paste during seeding would put MFS 37% at the wrong floor. The hand-walked "$87,126.75 at the $323,925 boundary" assertion proves the right floor was loaded (because if it were $539,900, the walker would have been at $215,950 + 35% * ($539,900 - $215,950) = $329,275 of accumulated tax, not $87,126.75). A naive seed that "looked up CTC for 2022" might have grabbed the well-publicized ARPA 2021 amounts (which the IRS extensively communicated in 2021 outreach) instead of the boring TCJA-baseline $2,000 / $1,500-refundable that quietly returned in 2022. The test pins the boring number specifically because the ARPA temptation exists. (4) Why advance only ONE year (TY 2022) per session: Rev. Proc. PDFs are individually structured; each year requires a careful hand-transcription pass with citations. Doing 5 years per session sounds efficient but produces a higher rate of transcription errors that the test layer can't all catch (the spec's "good enough tax assumptions" trap). One-year-per-session with a fully hand-walked test class is the correct cadence for the verifiable-data contract. The next session does TY 2021 (which DOES require ARPA's CTC structure -- $3,000 / $3,600 with the $75K/$150K threshold and full refundability -- so the existing ref.irs_child_tax_credit schema's amount_under_6 vs amount_6_to_17 split, dormant in 2018-2020 and 2022+, comes alive). After 2021, sessions 2010-2020 are mostly mechanical (similar TCJA-baseline rules apply to 2018-2020, similar pre-TCJA rules apply to 2010-2017 with the $4K-ish personal exemption coming back into use). + No deviation from VISION_2026 Phase 5 scope: zero new fraud signals, zero UI work, zero historical-pre-2010 substrate work, zero MOD-IV (Phase 8) work, zero deferred §7.1 one-button-refresh work. Every line of new SQL traces to a dated Rev. Proc. or NJ-1040 citation. PRIOR EDIT)
PRIOR (PHASE 4 PERSONALIZED AFFORDABILITY ENGINE -- VISION_2026 §2 Phase 4, idea spec §11 "relocation decision tools". One migration + one new seed + one pytest module + one frontend data layer + one new Next.js route + nav-bar wiring. Same engine as Phases 1-3, with the median household swapped for a USER-SUPPLIED profile. The substrate was deliberately built so this layer is mechanical: f_household_taxes already accepts arbitrary (gross, status, deps, kids), f_piti_annual already accepts arbitrary (home_price, year, county, down, term, ins, rate_override), f_disposable_income_annual already accepts arbitrary (gross, year, county, status, deps, kids, home). Phase 4 is the user-facing surface on top. + KEY DESIGN INSIGHT: PITI(H) is LINEAR in home price for fixed (year, county, term, down, insurance, rate). Therefore PITI(H) = H * c where c = 12 * (1 - down_pct) * annuity_factor + prop_tax_rate + insurance_rate, a single constant per (year, county, mortgage assumptions). The DTI constraints (front: PITI/12 <= dti_front * G/12; back: (PITI + 12*other_debt)/12 <= dti_back * G/12) are therefore also linear in H, and max-affordable-home-price has a CLOSED FORM: H_max = min(dti_front * G / c, max(0, (dti_back * G - 12 * other_debt) / c)). The Phase-2 required-income engine had to bisect because tax is non-linear in income; here we hold income fixed and solve for H, which is a straight-line problem. We get exact, deterministic answers with no convergence ceremony. We DO offer a tax-aware variant (PITI <= dti_front * take-home) which is also closed-form because tax depends on G alone. + Migration 074 ships formula version '1.4.0-personalization-engine-v1' chained to '1.3.0-disposable-income-erosion-v1' (Phase 3) -> '1.2.0-affordability-engine-v1' (Phase 2) -> '1.1.0-tax-engine-v1' (Phase 1) with six surfaces: derived.f_piti_coefficient(year, county_fips, down_pct, term_years, insurance_rate, rate_override) returns the "c" constant by extracting the annuity factor via f_mortgage_pi_monthly($1 loan) -- this handles the zero-rate edge case cleanly without a separate code path. derived.f_user_max_affordable_home_price_dti(gross, year, county, other_monthly_debt, dti_front, dti_back, down, term, ins, rate_override) is the closed-form max H under Fannie Mae conventional DTI on gross income (front 28% / back 36% per Selling Guide B3-6, defaulted from ref.affordability_assumptions, user-overridable). Returns NULL when gross<=0 or any required substrate missing; returns 0 when even a $0 home fails back DTI (which means the user has too much other debt to qualify for any mortgage). derived.f_user_max_affordable_home_price_post_tax(gross, year, county, status, deps, kids, ...) is the stricter "PITI <= dti_front * take-home" variant -- tax computed once because it depends on G alone, not on H. Matches the Phase-2 lender-style required-income definition (which solved PITI = threshold * take-home for income; here we hold income and solve for max H, the dual). NULL when tax tables not seeded for the year. derived.f_user_required_income_for_home(home_price, year, county, ...) is the inverse: gross income required for a given home to satisfy both DTI caps. Closed form: G = max(PITI / dti_front, (PITI + 12*other_debt) / dti_back). Used by the per-town "you need $N more income" delta. derived.f_user_town_verdict(year, county, gross, status, deps, kids, ...) is the headline output: returns TABLE per (year, county, profile) with median_home_price, max_affordable_dti, max_affordable_post_tax, piti_on_median, required_gross_for_median, user_take_home, personal_burden_ratio (PITI/gross), personal_burden_ratio_post_tax (PITI/take-home), verdict_dti and verdict_post_tax (each 'affordable' / 'stretch' / 'out_of_reach' or NULL when substrate missing), gross_income_gap (required - user gross; signed: positive = short, negative = headroom), and formula_version. Verdict bands per the canonical HUD outreach materials: median <= max = 'affordable'; max < median <= 1.25*max = 'stretch'; > 1.25*max = 'out_of_reach'. The 1.25 multiplier is the new affordability_stretch_multiplier constant in ref.affordability_assumptions (seeded via 013) and is user-overridable. derived.f_user_nj_county_verdicts(year, gross, status, deps, kids, ...) is the set-returning convenience: emits one f_user_town_verdict row per NJ county (filtered to ref.county.state_code='NJ', sorted by name), driving the per-county verdict table on /personalize. + Architectural decision: the engine is a SET OF FUNCTIONS, not a materialized view. Reason: the input space is huge (cross-product of household profile dimensions x counties x years) and 99% of it is never queried. Lazy compute on the request path is the right shape. Functions inline cleanly (LANGUAGE sql STABLE PARALLEL SAFE) so the planner pushes constants into f_piti_annual / f_household_taxes / f_assumption_value calls with no per-row overhead. + Seed 013 ships affordability_stretch_multiplier=1.25 (HUD outreach materials: "stretch home" = up to 25% over budget; "out of reach" beyond that). DELIBERATELY rolled as a NEW seed file 013 rather than editing the existing 012 because the migration drift-detector caught my first attempt at editing 012 and refused -- correctly. The drift-detector is doing exactly the job it was built for; rolling forward in a new file is the right path. + 34 hand-computed live-pg tests in tests/test_phase4_personalization.py grouped into 6 test classes: TestPITICoefficient (5 cases: c-via-coefficient * H must equal f_piti_annual to <$0.01 precision -- THE CRITICAL INVARIANT that pins the closed-form refactor agrees with the existing PITI calc / c is approximately 0.095869 for the synthetic substrate / lower rate -> lower c (counterfactual sanity) / higher down -> lower c (loan size sanity) / NULL for unknown county), TestMaxAffordableDTI (8 cases: $150K MFJ-1-1 -> $438,097.64 hand-computed / $1000/mo other debt makes back-cap exactly equal front-cap (algebra pin) / $5000/mo other debt clamps back-cap to 0 (user can't afford any home) / higher DTI caps increase max / 2% rate override increases max (counterfactual slider) / NULL gross / zero gross / negative gross / unknown county all -> NULL), TestMaxAffordablePostTax (4 cases: $150K MFJ-1-1 -> $346,312.90 hand-computed (post-tax stricter than gross-DTI) / max_post_tax < max_dti for any positive tax burden (sign-flip detector) / NULL when tax tables not seeded for year / NULL for unknown filing status), TestRequiredIncomeForHome (4 cases: $500K home requires $171,194.71 gross / inverse-of-max-affordable identity test (max_h then required_for_max_h returns user gross within $1) / $2000/mo other debt makes back-DTI bind harder / NULL for unknown county), TestUserTownVerdict (8 cases: full-row pin at the headline scenario including verdict_dti='stretch' (500K vs 438K=1.14x) and verdict_post_tax='out_of_reach' (500K vs 346K=1.44x) / 'affordable' for $300K user / 'out_of_reach' for $60K user / 'stretch' for engineered $155.6K user at the 1.10x boundary / personal_burden_ratio = PITI/gross algebra pin / negative gross_income_gap when user gross > required (headroom) / always returns 1 row even for unknown county / unknown county yields NULL verdicts not crash), TestUserNJCountyVerdicts (4 cases: returns exactly 21 rows (the 21 NJ counties seeded in ref.county) / excludes the synthetic 'XX-TEST' county (state_code filter) / rows carry county_name for the table / sorted alphabetically by county name). EVERY assertion is a hand-computed dollar amount. Substrate-honesty: every "missing input -> NULL" path pinned (NULL gross, zero gross, negative gross, unknown county, unseeded tax year, unknown filing status, unknown county for verdict). + lib/personalize.ts ships HouseholdProfile type + parseProfileFromSearch(searchParams) and profileToSearchParams(profile) for URL-state encoding (sanitizes every field; a hostile URL can't crash the server or trigger SQL errors -- gross clamps to >=0, dependents/qualifying_children clamp to [0, 20], filing_status falls back to 'single' on unknown values, year clamps to [2010, 2099]). runPersonalizationEngine(profile) issues 3 parallel queries via Promise.all: (1) derived.f_user_nj_county_verdicts for the per-county verdict table (FLOAT8-cast all numerics for Neon HTTP client number coercion), (2) derived.f_household_taxes for the user's exact federal/NJ/FICA breakdown + take-home, (3) ref.affordability_assumptions for citation rows of every defaulted constant the engine relied on (uses the same "freshest effective_year <= profile.year" semantics as ref.f_assumption SQL function). Returns PersonalizationResult { profile, tax, counties[], assumptions[], formula_version, resolved_year, year_fallback_reason }. fmtUsd, fmtPct, verdictTone display helpers. + app/personalize/page.tsx is the new Next.js dynamic route. SERVER-RENDERED, ZERO CLIENT JS (form uses METHOD=GET so the URL fully encodes the profile -- bookmarks, sharing, and back/forward all "just work" without any session/account/cookie infrastructure; no PII server-side; the URL IS the state). Layout: hero ("Where in NJ can you actually afford to live?"), profile form (gross income / filing status / year / dependents / qualifying children / other monthly debt / collapsible mortgage-assumptions overrides for down-payment / term-15-or-30 / DTI front-back caps / mortgage rate override for counterfactual sliders), default-state notice when no profile submitted, year-fallback amber notice when requested year has no NJ DCA substrate (suggests year=2024), TAX BURDEN CARD showing federal+NJ+FICA+total+effective_rate computed by Phase-1 engine for the user's exact profile + take-home (so the user can see the impact of their filing status / dependents on their take-home before getting to the per-county verdicts), PER-COUNTY VERDICT TABLE with 21 rows sorted by income gap ascending (most affordable counties first; counties with NULL median sink to the bottom), summary pills "{N} affordable / {N} stretch / {N} out of reach" so the user gets the headline before scrolling, columns: county / median home / your max (DTI) / income gap (color-coded green for negative=headroom, red for positive=short) / personal burden ratio (PITI/gross %) / verdict (DTI) / verdict (post-tax) -- both verdicts are colored pills (emerald/amber/red) so the user instantly sees where DTI says "stretch" but post-tax says "out of reach" (the granular signal the spec demanded), ASSUMPTIONS BLOCK lists every defaulted constant with its citation, source URL, effective year (the verifiable-data contract made user-facing -- if the user overrides a constant in the form, their override takes precedence and the citation still shows what the default WOULD have been, so they can see the trade-off they're making). Form input styles inlined via <style> block to avoid pulling in client JS for form theming. Honors the existing "Database not reachable" graceful-degradation pattern from /housing /risk /personalize. + app/layout.tsx adds 'Personalize' nav link with red font-semibold styling so it's visually emphasized as the headline new feature alongside existing Housing / Risk queue / Methodology links. + Quality gate: ruff clean on Python, mypy clean on Python, tsc --noEmit clean across whole frontend (now includes ƒ /personalize route in the route inventory), Next 16 production build clean (Turbopack compile + TypeScript check + static-page generation all green, 9 pages routed), full pytest 1067/1067 passing (was 1033 after Phase 3, +34 Phase 4 personalization tests). + HAND-COMPUTED ANCHORS LOCKED IN: For $150K MFJ-1-1 user, $500K median home in test county (FRED 7%, prop tax 2.85%, ins 0.35%, 20% down, 30y term, 2024): PITI coefficient c = 0.095869 (annual PITI per $1 home price, this MUST agree with f_piti_annual($500K) = $47,934.52 to the cent or the c-extraction is broken); max-affordable DTI = $438,097.64 (front-end binds, back-cap = $563,268 slack); max-affordable post-tax = $346,312.90 (stricter because take-home $118,573.87 < gross $150K); required gross for $500K home = $171,194.71 (front-end binds at PITI/0.28); tax for $150K MFJ-1-1 2024 = $31,426.13 (Phase-1 anchor, propagated through); take-home = $118,573.87; personal_burden_ratio = 0.3196 = 31.96% (above HUD 30% threshold); personal_burden_ratio_post_tax = 0.4043 = 40.43% (above 28% target); verdict_dti = 'stretch' (median $500K is 1.14x of max $438K, within 1.25x stretch band); verdict_post_tax = 'out_of_reach' (median $500K is 1.44x of max $346K, beyond 1.25x); gross_income_gap = +$21,194.71 (need $21K more annual income). The "stretch by DTI / out_of_reach by post-tax" split is exactly the granular per-user signal the spec demanded -- and exactly the kind of detail an opposing lawyer can't dismiss because every input is cited. + DESIGN DECISIONS DOCUMENTED: (1) Why closed-form vs bisection: the constraints are linear in H for fixed gross because tax doesn't depend on H; Phase 2 needed bisection only because tax was nonlinear in income. (2) Why functions vs materialized view: the input space is the cross-product of profile dimensions x counties x years; 99% never queried; lazy compute is the right shape. (3) Why GET form / URL state vs POST / session: shareability is the headline value (the user can text the URL to a partner and they see the same numbers), and the privacy contract is "no PII server-side, no accounts, no cookies, no logging the profile" -- all enforced by encoding everything in the URL the client owns. (4) Why two verdicts (DTI gross + post-tax): the conventional Fannie Mae underwriter uses gross DTI, but the household's actual budget is post-tax. Showing BOTH highlights the gap that NJ tax burden creates. The user can see "I qualify per the bank's math but I can't actually afford it per my budget" instantly, which is exactly the kind of signal the spec demanded for a "trustworthy decision tool." (5) Why integrate into the same `/personalize` route rather than per-county subpages: the headline question is comparative ("which NJ county can I afford?"), not single-county; one page with a sortable per-county table answers that better than 21 separate pages. The per-county detail is then one click away on the existing /housing/[id]/collapse pages, which now show the SAME engine results (just with median income instead of user income). + Direct unblocks: Phase 5 (historical tax-table backfill 2010-2022) is the highest-value-per-hour next task because it lights up the Collapse Curve, DI trajectory, AEI, AND the /personalize historical scenarios across all 21 counties for free; Phase 8 (MOD-IV municipality data) upgrades the personalization engine from county-level to municipality-level with no engineering changes (just swap the home-price source); the deferred §7.1 one-button data refresh is now more valuable because users have a live surface that benefits from staleness honesty. + No deviation from VISION_2026 Phase 4 scope: zero new fraud signals, zero unrelated UI work, zero historical tax-table seeding tonight (deferred to Phase 5 as a pure-data-work session). The frontend is intentionally the simplest possible MVP: server-rendered form + tax breakdown + per-county table + assumptions block. Polish (sortable columns, multi-county compare, personal DI trajectory chart, personal AEI, town-level breakdown) is queued for a follow-up session.

Auto-discovering the earliest non-NULL HBR year per-county, exposing the anchor_year on the row, and labeling the UI "vs YYYY" is the substrate-honest path; the day pre-2009 income substrate lands, the AEI view automatically backfills to the actual spec-mandated anchor without code changes. (2) Why CPI-deflator base-year is the latest available year and not the spec-mandated 2026: BLS publishes the 2026 M13 annual average around Dec 2026; until then "real 2026 dollars" is mathematically undefined. (3) Why three required-income metrics + AEI live in the SAME page (/housing/[id]/collapse) rather than separate /erosion route: the Collapse Curve answers "can the median household afford this?", the required-income comparison answers "by how much, under three definitions?", the DI trajectory answers "what's left over for them?", and the AEI answers "how much harder is this than it used to be?". Four answers to the same question deserve one page; fewer pages = fewer places to drift out of sync. (4) Why DI plots BOTH real and nominal: the headline question is real-dollar erosion, but a viewer who's never thought about CPI-deflation should see the nominal line for comparison so they understand WHY the real line tells a different story. (5) Why area-under-line shading: the chart's core message is "after housing and taxes, this is what the median household has left." Filling the area below the line makes that "what's left" quantity visually concrete -- thin band = little headroom, thick band = lots of headroom. (6) Why the trajectory view filters MFJ-1-1 hardcoded for now: the per-county aggregate uses the same representative household as v_affordability_gap so the metrics are directly comparable; the Phase 4 personalization engine swaps for user-supplied profile via the function variant (which already accepts arbitrary status/deps/kids). + Direct unblocks: Phase 4 (Personalized Affordability Engine, idea §11) -- the f_disposable_income_annual function already accepts a user-supplied (gross, status, deps, kids, home) tuple, so the personalization page can call it with any household profile to get "your DI in town X". The HBR function similarly accepts arbitrary (year, county) so "your erosion vs 1990 in this town" is one function call away once the substrate lands. + Hand-computed anchors locked in for any future audit: 2024 MFJ-1-1, $500K home, FRED 7%, ACS $120K -- PITI $47,934.52, tax $21,223.63, DI $50,841.86 (real and nominal identical because base_year=value_year). 2023 MFJ-1-1, $450K home, FRED 6%, ACS $115K -- PITI $40,300.69, tax $20,168.77, DI nominal $54,530.54, DI in 2024 dollars $56,138.89. HBR_2023=0.35044, HBR_2024=0.39945, AEI=1.1399. PRIOR (PHASE 2 AFFORDABILITY ENGINE -- VISION_2026 §2 Phase 2, idea spec §5.1 / §5.4 / §7.3. Two migrations + one seed + one pytest module that ship the headline metric the spec named "your viral insight chart": required income to afford the median home in each NJ county, the affordability gap (required - actual median income), and the substrate the Collapse Curve frontend will plot. Stacks on the Phase 1 tax engine. + Migration 071 ships ref.affordability_assumptions, the platform's tier-A constants registry: every numeric assumption that influences a metric carries (constant_id, value, unit, source_url, source_citation) plus an effective_year ("as of" lookup with sentinel 0 = perpetual default; year-specific rows like effective_year=2014 for the CFPB QM rule supersede the perpetual row from their effective year onward via ORDER BY effective_year DESC LIMIT 1). 11 constants seeded with citations: mortgage_default_down_pct=0.20 (Fannie Mae conforming-loan standard), mortgage_default_term_years=30 (Fannie Mae most-common amortization), homeowners_insurance_annual_rate_default=0.0035 (NAIC Homeowners Insurance Report), affordability_threshold_pct=0.30 (HUD CHAS cost-burden definition), affordability_severe_burden_pct=0.50 (HUD severe-burden), dti_front_end_cap_conventional=0.28 (Fannie Mae Selling Guide B3-6), dti_back_end_cap_conventional=0.36 (Fannie Mae), dti_back_end_cap_qm_rule=0.43 effective 2014 (CFPB Reg Z 1026.43(e)(2)(vi)), pmi_threshold_ltv=0.80 (HOPA 1998 / 12 USC §4901), pmi_annual_rate_default=0.005 (Urban Institute Housing Finance at a Glance), hoa_monthly_default=0 (AHS), cpi_baseline_year=2026 (idea §3.4). Two resolver functions: ref.f_assumption(id, year) RETURNS TABLE (value_numeric, unit, source_url, source_citation, effective_year) so UI surfaces can display the citation alongside every defaulted number, and ref.f_assumption_value(id, year) RETURNS NUMERIC for pure scalar use inside other SQL functions. + Migration 072 ships the seven-function affordability engine, all LANGUAGE sql STABLE PARALLEL SAFE except the two PL/pgSQL bisection solvers: derived.f_mortgage_pi_monthly(loan, annual_rate_decimal, term_years) (standard amortization closed-form M = P*r*(1+r)^n/((1+r)^n - 1), IMMUTABLE, zero-rate edge handled as P/n, negative-rate / non-positive term / NULL inputs return NULL), derived.f_fred_30yr_annual_rate(year) (annual mean of FRED MORTGAGE30US from derived.fred_annual, percent->decimal normalized, NULL for unseeded years -- substrate honesty), derived.f_county_property_tax_rate(fips, year) (DCA cy_total_rate percent->decimal), derived.f_county_avg_home_price(fips, year) (DCA avg_residential_value pass-through), derived.f_piti_annual(home_price, year, county_fips, down_pct, term_years, insurance_rate, rate_override) (composite: 12 * P&I + home_price * prop_tax_rate + home_price * insurance_rate, all four assumptions defaulted from ref.affordability_assumptions and individually overridable -- the rate_override path enables the personalization-engine counterfactual sliders for "what if rates dropped to 2021 lows?"; any missing input bubbles NULL through), and THREE required-income metrics each answering a different question: derived.f_required_income_hud_30pct(piti, threshold) is THE HEADLINE -- HUD CHAS-aligned linear PITI/threshold, always defined when PITI is, comparable across counties/years/households, the column the Collapse Curve plots; derived.f_required_income_post_tax_30pct(piti, year, status, deps, kids, threshold) is the LENDER-style definition (PITI <= threshold * (gross - tax(gross))), bisects 30 iterations over the monotone take-home function (always converges if tax substrate exists, NULL otherwise); derived.f_required_income_full_burden_30pct(home_price, year, fips, status, deps, kids, threshold) is the STRICT definition (PITI + tax(G) <= threshold * G) and is DELIBERATELY NULL when unreachable (which it usually is in NJ because combined federal+NJ+FICA marginal exceeds 30% in middle MFJ brackets) -- this NULL is the housing-cost crisis rendered numerically and is pinned as such in TestRequiredIncomeFullBurden::test_typical_nj_scenario_unreachable so a future tax-cut or rate-drop that changes the math has to update the test deliberately. + derived.v_affordability_gap is the per-(county, year) headline view: home price, median income, PITI, all three required-income metrics, hud_headroom_dollars (median - hud_required), hud_required_to_actual_ratio. One row per (county_fips, year) where DCA + ACS5 substrate exists. Representative household MFJ + 1 dep + 1 kid (rough median NJ owner-occupied profile per ACS B11005); the Phase 4 personalization engine swaps for user-supplied values. Smoke run on a synthetic county (DCA $500K @ 2.85%, FRED 7%, ACS5 $120K) yields exactly the granular signal the spec demanded: PITI = $47,934.52, HUD-required income = $159,781.73 (median falls 25% short), Lender-required = $210,318 (75% short), Strict-required = NULL (unreachable at any income). + Registers ref.formula_version row '1.2.0-affordability-engine-v1' so downstream Collapse Curve and personalization output carry reproducibility lineage chained to '1.1.0-tax-engine-v1'. + 41 live-pg tests in tests/test_phase2_affordability.py across 10 test classes: TestMortgagePIMonthly (8 cases: returns 0.07 decimal NOT 7.0 percent / unseeded year NULL substrate-honest), TestCountyReads (DCA percent->decimal normalization for prop_tax_rate, avg home price pass-through, both NULL for unseeded citation+url returned alongside value), TestPITIAnnual (8 cases hand-computed at NUMERIC precision: default-assumptions = $47,934.52 / 3% rate-override = $36,236.99 (the full-precision NUMERIC answer, Every assertion is a HAND-COMPUTED PITI / mortgage P&I / required-income value cross-checked against published mortgage formulas. + ALL 41 pass against the live nj_pg_test container; full repo suite 981/981 (was 940 after Phase 1; +41 Phase 2); ruff clean on test file (one fix: prefix unused tuple element with underscore); mypy clean on new files (the pre-existing 41 mypy errors in test_orchestration / test_pums_burden / test_dol_oflc_lca / test_fhfa_hpi / test_stats remain unchanged, none from this session). + DESIGN DECISION DOCUMENTED: idea spec §5.4 sketched "(Housing+Taxes)/0.30" as the headline metric, but mathematical analysis on the seeded substrate showed this is mostly UNREACHABLE in NJ -- the combined federal+NJ+FICA marginal rate (~35% at $100K MFJ, ~45%+ above $500K) exceeds the 30% threshold before PITI/G falls below 30%, so F(G) = 0.30*G - PITI - tax(G) stays negative for all G. Rather than ship a metric that returns NULL for 21/21 NJ counties (useless as a "headline"), I shipped THREE metrics in increasing strictness so the user can see the full story: the HUD-aligned linear definition is the citable comparable headline that the Collapse Curve will plot; the lender post-tax definition is the next-most-strict; the strict full-burden definition is preserved as a "stricter standard reachability" signal that the housing-cost crisis is structurally embedded in the tax+price interaction. The HUD definition is also the published, citable standard used by every federal housing program (HUD CHAS, LIHTC, Section 8) so it's the right anchor for cross-source verification. + Direct unblocks: the Collapse Curve frontend (idea §7.3, "your viral insight chart") can now query derived.v_affordability_gap for one row per (county, year) and render three series (median income, HUD-required income, shaded gap area); the Personalized Affordability Engine (Phase 4 / idea §11) can call f_piti_annual + f_required_income_*_30pct with user-supplied (home_price, status, dependents, kids, down_pct, term_years, insurance_rate, rate_override) for per-user verdicts; Phase 3 disposable income (idea §5.3) is now a one-line composition: gross - f_household_taxes(gross).total - f_piti_annual(...). + DEFERRED-OPS NOTE captured in VISION_2026 §7.1: the user asked about a one-button data-refresh ("will I be able to click a button on my computer and it automatically start refreshing"). Filed as deferred (not blocking) because the operational design is clear (Dagster global_refresh_all job + make refresh shortcut + derived.v_data_freshness_summary view + per-source asset-check that fails the build if the IRS Rev. Proc. for the prior tax year hasn't been transcribed by April 1) and the platform value is in deepening the metric layer first. + No deviation from VISION_2026 Phase 2 scope: zero new fraud signals, zero UI work tonight (the Collapse Curve frontend is the next deliverable in this phase), zero deployment work -- only the SQL substrate the headline metric chain depends on.

Both surfaces previously depended on the database session's TimeZone GUC; that meant two Neon instances in different regions computed DIFFERENT decay weights for the same exclusion (substrate-honesty violation: L1 raw_value should be a deterministic function of (input, code), never of session config) and tests run near 00:00 UTC saw 0.9997 instead of 1.0 because Python's dt.date.today() (local) drifted off Postgres CURRENT_DATE (session TZ). + Companion test-side fix: replaced 38 occurrences of dt.date.today() across 8 test files with dt.datetime.now(dt.UTC).date() so the test fixtures and the SQL function agree on the same calendar instant. Same canonicalization function as LEIE (f_canonical_lastfirst_from_fec on FEC side, f_canonical_lastfirst_split on SAM side reuses the LEIE function for byte-identical keys), so a person on BOTH lists fires BOTH signals and the L2 pivot aggregates into one (entity_kind, entity_id) row with signal_families=[leie_bearing, sam_bearing] -- the strongest possible cross-list corroboration, and earns the multi-family diversity bonus in fraud_risk_score (+ ~18 score pts at equal phi vs single-family). Severity=5 (CRITICAL) on every match. signal_family='sam_bearing'. min_actionable_threshold=$200 mirrors donor_on_leie ($200 = FEC's itemization threshold; below that contributions canonicalize to NULL anyway). Per-contribution age decay applied (SUM(GREATEST(transaction_amt, 0) * f_leie_age_decay(sam_active_date))). All-refund (positive-zero-after-clamp) donors dropped from L1 to keep the queue actionable. + entity_kind='donor' (already in whitelist as of 059, no schema change). Vacuous-pass condition wider than donor_on_leie's check: the donor_on_sam asset check WARNs only when v_sam_exclusion_individual_canonical is non-empty AND zero matches surface -- a fresh hand-load with only Firm/Vessel-shape SAM rows produces empty individual cohort, and zero matches there is the correct signal, not a regression. + Dagster: derived/signal_donor_on_sam asset (deps=[raw/fec_contribution]; raw.sam_gov_exclusion still has no asset because no automated fetcher). v_entity_fraud_risk asset gains the dep edge so SAM refresh propagates fingerprint changes to L3a. New ERROR-severity asset check signal_donor_on_sam_match_rate_plausible (vacuous-pass on empty-individual-cohort, fail on zero-when-cohort-present, fail on >1% over-match). + 18 live-pg tests covering schema invariant (config seeded with sam_bearing/$200), refresher integration (1 match -> 1 row severity=5 raw_value=$2K, corporate-shape donor does NOT fire (NULL canonical_key), SAM Firm classification does NOT fire (filtered by view), terminated SAM exclusion does NOT fire (filtered by v_sam_exclusion_active), no SAM match -> 0 rows, multi-contribution aggregation, multi-SAM-row freshest-active_date wins, memo_cd='X' excluded, negative amounts clamp to 0, all-refund donor dropped, age decay = exp(-1) at 10y, idempotent re-refresh, cycle isolation 2024 vs 2020, percentile correctness 1-of-2 = 0.5, evidence URL well-formed with sam=<hash>) and L2/L3a integration (sam_bearing in signal_families[], $50 contribution survives L1 but drops at L2 threshold, dual-fire LEIE+SAM same-person produces 2 L1 rows + 1 L2 row with both families). Updated test_fraud_signal_config_and_synergy::test_all_15_known_signals_seeded -> test_all_16... to include the donor_on_sam -> sam_bearing/$200 seed. Updated test_orchestration::test_v_entity_fraud_risk_asset_present_with_correct_deps to include the new asset key. Total: 31 assets (was 30). Total: 49 asset checks (was 47; +1 for entity_excluded_via_sam_uei plausibility, +1 for donor_on_sam plausibility -- both deferred from prior turn). + TEST-INFRA: fixed PG_DSN env-var leak in tests/test_serving.py module-scoped client fixture that had been silently contaminating 23 downstream `test_*_cli_requires_pg_dsn` tests for ~weeks. The fixture set os.environ["PG_DSN"] for the FastAPI lifespan but never restored on teardown, so once any test_serving.py test ran in a session, every subsequent CLI DSN-guard test FAILED (CLI saw PG_DSN, tried to connect, exited 1 instead of the expected 2). Result: full live-pg suite was reporting 23 "expected" failures every run. Now: save+restore pattern with try/finally cleanup. From 825 -> 866 live-pg passes (+18 new, +23 unblocked). Pre-existing 41 mypy errors are all in tests/* and unrelated to this change. PRIOR EDIT)
PRIOR (FRAUD-F2 SAM x USASPENDING UEI-DETERMINISTIC SIGNAL. Migration 064 ships derived.refresh_signal_entity_excluded_via_sam_uei(cycle), the FIRST cross-source signal ever shipped that requires ZERO name canonicalization to match. Joins active USAspending NJ-pop recipients (raw.usaspending_award where recipient_uei IS NOT NULL) against active SAM.gov exclusions (derived.v_sam_exclusion_by_uei) on the 12-character federal-procurement primary key. False-positive risk is ~zero (UEI is unique by SAM design); a UEI-deterministic match is the same legal entity, full stop. Severity=5 (CRITICAL) on every match -- a SAM-excluded UEI receiving any federal contract is a FAR 9.405 violation, period. Steady-state expected count is ZERO in a well-functioning federal procurement system; non-empty rows are always investigation-worthy. + signal_family CHECK on derived.fraud_signal_config extended from 4 -> 5 values to add 'sam_bearing'. The diversity bonus in derived.fraud_risk_score now rewards entities firing on BOTH leie_bearing AND sam_bearing signals -- the strongest possible cross-source corroboration (a person both excluded from federal healthcare AND from federal contracts AND awarded a federal contract anyway). + entity_excluded_via_sam_uei row seeded with min_actionable_threshold=$0 because UEI-deterministic match has no spurious-noise interpretation; every dollar matters. Reuses derived.f_leie_age_decay (the function is exclusion-list-agnostic, the LEIE prefix in its name is historical) so a 12-year-old SAM exclusion has the same exp(-1.2) ~= 0.301 decay weight as a 12-year-old LEIE exclusion.
