PRIOR (FRAUD-F2 SAM.gov LOADER. ingestion/sam_gov_exclusions.py ships a load-only ingester and `nj-ingest-sam-exclusions load <csv>` CLI for the schema established by migration 063. Decision: defer the auto-fetcher (API-key + public-extract paths both have inconsistent auth across clients and the daily exclusions file is small + slow-moving enough that the operational cost of a manual monthly pull is dominated by zero-API-surprise reliability). The loader is the same code regardless of source: an automated fetcher is purely additive when SAM auth is verified. Architecture: HEADER-NAME-BASED parsing (not column-index) -- canonicalizes each SAM header to snake_case ASCII, builds a column-name -> index map, looks up each field by name; tolerates SAM column-order changes and unknown extra columns ('DODAAC' etc.) silently while failing loud on missing required columns (Classification only, in the current contract). Aliases handle SAM's wording variations ("State / Province" vs "State", "Zip Code" vs "Zip", "UEI" vs "Unique Entity Identifier"). Defensive enum coercion: maps "special entity" / "individual exclusion" / "INDIVIDUAL" / "  firm  " variants to the four-value Classification CHECK enum before INSERT. UEI normalized to upper-case + 12-char [A-Z0-9] regex check pre-INSERT. Dates accept ISO YYYY-MM-DD AND MM/DD/YYYY (SAM has shipped both). UPSERT semantics mirror LEIE: TEMP staging -> COPY -> INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (record_hash) DO UPDATE bumps last_seen_at + refreshes vintage_day/source_url/source_sha256 (latest pull's metadata wins; reinstatements detected by last_seen_at lag). record_hash inputs (SHA-256, pipe-joined, upper-case-stripped): classification + name + first/middle/last/suffix + uei + duns + sam_number + exclusion_type_desc + active_date. Address fields deliberately EXCLUDED from the hash because SAM normalizes them aggressively across releases ("STE 200" vs "Suite 200") and we don't want cosmetic drift to spam the hash space. + nj-ingest-sam-exclusions load CSV_PATH --dsn=$PG_DSN --vintage-day=YYYY-MM-DD (default today UTC) --source-url=<sam-landing>. Workflow: operator visits https://sam.gov/data-services/Exclusions/Public%20V2/ -> downloads daily ZIP -> unzips -> runs the load command. + 36 tests across pure-helpers, pure-parser, and live-PG integration: header-canonicalize basics + alias acceptance + missing-required-column rejection + extra-column-silent-drop, classification canonical+variant+case+rejection, UEI happy/empty/malformed paths, date ISO+MM/DD/YYYY+empty+rejection, hash determinism+sensitivity+64-hex-shape, vintage-day shape, parser happy-path + drops-DODAAC + missing-classification-header + empty-classification-field + unknown-classification + all-blank-name-row + empty-file + header-only-file + skips-blank-lines + malformed-UEI rejection, live-PG round-trip + classification canonicalization at COPY boundary + active view filters terminated+Inactive (1 of 4 dropped) + UEI view drops UEI-NULL + individual canonical view filters to Individual (1 of 4) with canonical_key shape + idempotent reload no-duplicates + vintage_day refresh + SAM-side correction inserts new hash keeps old (5 = 4+1 rule). PRIOR EDIT)
PRIOR (FRAUD-F2 SAM.gov SUBSTRATE (schema-only). Migration 063 ships raw.sam_gov_exclusion (one row per stable record_hash, daily-full-replace semantics planned, last_seen_at for reinstatement detection mirroring LEIE 053). Schema covers the SAM.gov Exclusions Public Extract V2 column shape: classification CHECK-enum {Individual / Special Entity Designation / Firm / Vessel}, name + parsed-name components, federal IDs (uei 12-char alphanumeric CHECK, duns, cage, npi), full address quad, exclusion metadata (program / agency / type / active_date / termination_date / record_status / sam_number / cross_reference / additional_comments), provenance (vintage_day / source_url / source_sha256 / ingested_at / last_seen_at). Sanity row-content CHECK requires at least one of (name, last, uei, duns, sam_number) so all-blank parser bugs trip at the boundary; intentionally lax vs LEIE because Vessel exclusions can lack human/firm names. Three indexes: idx_sam_uei (partial, uei IS NOT NULL: serves the deterministic UEI <-> UEI cross-source join against USAspending), idx_sam_individual_last (partial, classification='Individual' AND last IS NOT NULL: serves the name-based individual matching parallel to LEIE), idx_sam_termination_date (active-view filter). + Three derived views: derived.v_sam_exclusion_active filters termination_date in past + record_status='Inactive' (case-insensitive); derived.v_sam_exclusion_by_uei drops UEI-NULL rows for the deterministic-match join surface; derived.v_sam_exclusion_individual_canonical reuses LEIE's f_canonical_lastfirst_split so the LAST|FIRST canonical key is BYTE-IDENTICAL to LEIE's, enabling cross-list (LEIE x SAM x FEC) matching without canonicalization drift. + 21 live-pg tests covering: 4 classifications accepted + 5th rejected, UEI format CHECK (12-char A-Z0-9, rejects lowercase + wrong length), record_hash lowercase-hex CHECK, all-blank-row CHECK + Vessel-with-sam-number-only allowed, active view filtering (past termination drops, NULL keeps, future keeps, record_status='Inactive' drops, case-insensitive 'Active' variants kept), UEI view drops UEI-NULL rows + carries UEI verbatim, individual canonical view includes Individual + excludes Firm/Vessel/Special Entity + skips NULL canonicalization + matches LEIE's canonical function on edge cases (apostrophe, hyphen). DELIBERATELY DEFERRED until SAM fetch path is verified against a real CSV: (a) loader / ingester (no auth path verified), (b) cross-source signal entity_excluded_via_sam_uei (no L1 writer => fraud_signal_config orphan-block would fail), (c) Dagster asset for the table (no compute, would mislead lineage). Total assets: 29 (unchanged). Total asset checks: 47 (unchanged). PRIOR EDIT)
PRIOR (DETECTION-QUALITY SLICE B: LEIE-age decay. Migration 062 ships derived.f_leie_age_decay(excldate) -> exp(-age_years/10), STABLE PARALLEL SAFE, NULL/future-clamp to 1.0. The four LEIE-bearing refreshers (054 entity_on_leie, 058 entity_funded_and_excluded, 059 donor_on_leie, 060 candidate_funded_by_excluded_donors) are CREATE-OR-REPLACE'd to multiply raw_value by the decay weight. 058/059 apply at the per-person aggregate level (one freshest excldate per matched person; equivalent to per-row decay since all rows share the excldate). 060 applies PER-CONTRIBUTION because each candidate receives money from many donors with potentially different excldates: a new matched_donors_with_excldate CTE re-joins LEIE inside 060, then SUM(transaction_amt * f_leie_age_decay(excldate)) per candidate. 054 changes raw_value from binary 1.0 to the decay weight in (0, 1]; min_actionable_threshold=0 keeps all matches. The interaction with 061's threshold filter is the design intent: a 12-year-old $20K contract decays to ~$6024 (below the $10K floor for entity_funded_and_excluded) and drops out of the analyst queue. + 12 new live-pg tests in test_fraud_leie_age_decay.py: decay shape (NULL/today/future/5y/10y/20y/monotonicity), 058 old-excldate scaling, 058 decay-pushes-below-threshold (substrate-honesty: L1 keeps the row, L2 drops it), 059 5y decay, 060 per-donor decay (mixed old+new donors give SUM = 1000*exp(-1) + 1000 = ~$1368), 054 binary->fraction. + Pre-existing 13 LEIE-test failures fixed by changing _seed_leie_individual default excldate from "20200115" to "today" so literal-sum assertions still hold; the 058 multi-LEIE-collapse test was strengthened by setting freshest=today and oldest=2010 so the assertion implicitly verifies multi-LEIE picks the freshest. Total assets: 29. Total asset checks: 47. PRIOR EDIT)
PRIOR (DETECTION-QUALITY SLICE: per-signal min_actionable_threshold + multi-family diversity bonus. Migration 061 ships derived.fraud_signal_config(signal_id PK, signal_family, min_actionable_threshold, comment, created_at, updated_at) -- a static config registry seeded with all 14 known signals classified into 4 families (leie_bearing, workforce, address, structural) and per-signal $-floor thresholds (e.g. donor_on_leie=$200, entity_funded_and_excluded=$10K, donor_employed_by_nj_contractor=$1K, all binary/structural=$0). The L2 view (v_entity_fraud_features) now INNER-JOINs config and applies the per-signal raw_value floor; below-threshold matches stay in L1 (substrate honesty preserved) but drop out of L2/L3a/the analyst queue. The L2 view also exposes a parallel signal_families[] column. A new 3-arg derived.fraud_risk_score(severities, percentiles, families) keeps the same tail-only additive base (phi = sev * max(0, p - 0.95)^2) and adds a family-diversity term: bonus = 0.01 * max(0, n_contributing_families - 1)^2 (only families with at least one signal above the 0.95 percentile threshold count). Two-family entities at equal phi outscore single-family by ~18 score points; three-family entities saturate near 100. The L3a v_entity_fraud_risk view is rewired to use the 3-arg fn and exposes signal_families to the API/UI. + Dagster: derived.fraud_signal_config registered as a fingerprint asset (no upstream; static registry); v_entity_fraud_risk gains a dep edge on it so operator UPDATEs propagate to the L3a fingerprint. + 2 ERROR-severity asset checks: every_signal_id_in_l1_has_a_config_row (orphan-block: a signal_id in L1 without a config row INNER-JOINs out and silently disappears -- this check fails the pipeline) and multiple_families_present (defends against an UPDATE that mass-rewrites the family column and neuters the diversity bonus). + 19 live-pg tests covering schema invariants (4 CHECK constraints, updated_at trigger, all 14 seeded signals with correct family/threshold), L2 threshold-filter behavior (per-signal not per-entity), parallel-array invariant for signal_families, and 3-arg score function (NULL/empty/mismatched-length, 1/2/3-family monotonicity, below-0.95 signals do NOT count as contributing families). Pre-existing test_l2_pivot_aggregates_two_signals_into_one_row updated -- it referenced a non-existent signal_id 'address_clusters' (real id is 'committee_address_clusters'); my migration's strict orphan-blocking surfaced this latent bug. test_live_view_exposes_score_per_entity expected score for entity Y bumped from 95.61 -> 97.34 because Y's 5 signals span 2 families (4 structural + 1 address) and earn the +0.01 diversity term. Total: 29 assets, 47 asset checks.)

================================================================================
PHASE F-UX PLAN (2026-05-08) -- PILLAR 2 FRAUD UX:
STRUCTURAL-ANOMALY HEADLINE + EVIDENCE DRILL-DOWN
================================================================================
STATUS: planned, not started. This is the L4 ("Evidence panel") concretization
of the v3 design proposal below. v3 specifies L1-L5 substrate; this entry
specifies the rendering surface contract for L4 -- what the user actually
sees when they click an entity, and what data substrates the surface requires
that v3 does not yet ship.

Context: scaffolded surfaces today
----------------------------------
- /risk             : minimal table (tier badge, score, kind, families, n_signals);
                      working but no §3.4-style headline promotion.
- /risk/[kind]/[id] : header + 4-col KV grid + 7-col SignalsTable with severity
                      dots, raw value, percentile, peer bucket, and a SINGLE
                      placeholder "link" evidence button per row.
- Production data   : raw.fec_*, raw.leie_*, raw.sam_gov_*, raw.usaspending_*
                      are EMPTY. All 14 fraud_* migrations and v_entity_fraud_risk
                      are present and tested at unit-fixture scale, but no
                      production data flows through them yet.

The "link" placeholder column on /risk/[kind]/[id] is exactly the gap the user
flagged: "make it simple for the user to look and see all the evidence."

Framing -- BINDING
------------------
The user-visible scalar is a STRUCTURAL ANOMALY SCORE (0-1000), defined as the
entity's percentile of anomalousness within its peer bucket, calibrated against
the empirical NJ-cycle distribution. It is NOT P(fraud | evidence). We do not
have labeled enforcement-action data at scale; claiming a calibrated probability
would be statistically dishonest and legally exposed. The substrate-honest
framing -- "this entity exhibits N independent structural patterns historically
associated with FEC enforcement actions, here is the raw row for each one,
verify upstream at fec.gov" -- is what makes the platform legally publishable.

This restates v3's L1-L3a contract. Reaffirmed here so the UI copy does NOT
drift back to "% probability of fraud" language under product pressure.

UX CONTRACT (per .cursor/rules/verifiable-data.mdc, sections 1-5)
-----------------------------------------------------------------

A. /risk overview -- mirrors /housing overview headline pattern (§3.4)

   Hero: top-of-cycle entity rendered as a §3.4-style card --
     - composite score (947) as the largest typography
     - one-line peer calibration ("higher than 99.4% of NJ candidates 2024")
     - count of independent flags + count of contributing families
     - link to full drill-down
   Below: ranked top-N table (existing /risk table, retained).
   Header: <FreshnessBadge variant="compact"> for the FEC pillar (reuses
           lib/freshness.ts substrate; rolls up FEC + LEIE + SAM + USAspending
           into one Pillar-2 freshness signal).

B. /risk/[kind]/[id] -- four sections, progressive disclosure

   Section 1: Headline + family breakdown (mirrors /housing/[id] §3.4 grid)
     - Hero: composite score with peer-bucket calibration label
     - 4-col grid: structural / cross-source / network / behavioral
       (per-family score, per-family flag count)
     - Tier badge (S/A/B/C) demoted to secondary strip

   Section 2: Per-signal evidence cards (THE CORE DELIVERABLE)
     - One <SignalEvidenceCard> per firing signal (NOT a table row)
     - Every card is fully expanded by default. NO hover-only lineage.
       Rule §3 mandates inline visibility, not tooltip easter-eggs.
     - Card layout (top to bottom):
         * family badge + severity dots + score contribution
         * signal_id + human-readable signal name
         * "Why this fired" plain-English block (FROM ref.fraud_signal_human_explanation,
           NOT generated client-side; cites specific 11 CFR clause / FEC enforcement
           matter / FAPIIS criterion that motivated the threshold)
         * threshold from ref.fraud_signal_config (numeric, with min_actionable)
         * formula_version + source_vintage_hash stamps
         * Evidence rows table: 1-N rows of (raw_table, raw_row_pk, field, value,
           verify_upstream_url) -- linking to fec.gov / sam.gov / oig.hhs.gov
           specific record pages (NOT a generic landing page)
         * Source vintage stamp: {zipfile} + Last-Modified + SHA-256 prefix

   Section 3: Cross-source projections (network view)
     - One per cross-source signal (donor_on_LEIE, donor_on_SAM,
       candidate_funded_by_excluded_donors, entity_excluded_via_sam_uei, ...)
     - Renders the (donor) -> (committee) -> (recipient) edges with dollar
       weights and age-decay multiplier visible
     - Each node links to the upstream public record

   Section 4: Methodology footer
     - Composite formula citation -> db/migrations/052_fraud_risk_score.sql
     - Per-family weights -> ref.fraud_signal_config (with vintage)
     - Synergy table -> ref.fraud_signal_synergy (with vintage)
     - Severity calibration -> ref.fraud_signal_severity_calibration (NEW)
     - <FreshnessBadge variant="detail"> per upstream source

DATA SUBSTRATES TO LAND FIRST
-----------------------------

Section 2 above requires four NEW substrates not present in v3 design:

  1. ref.fraud_signal_human_explanation
       PK: signal_id (FK -> ref.fraud_signal_config.signal_id)
       cols: rule_text, citation_authority, citation_url,
             plain_english_template, formula_version, effective_date
       Role: every evidence card's "Why this fired" block reads from here.
             Without this table the cards are buzzwords. With it, every card
             cites a specific 11 CFR clause / FEC MUR / OIG report / FAPIIS
             criterion. Same shape as ref.cross_source_divergence_known_causes.
       Citation discipline: source_url MUST be a federal authority page (fec.gov,
             law.cornell.edu/cfr, oig.hhs.gov, sam.gov), CHECK length >= 11.

  2. ref.fraud_signal_severity_calibration
       PK: signal_id
       cols: severity_level (1-5), calibration_basis (FEC MUR | OIG report |
             CRS analysis | DOJ filing | empirical_pctile),
             precedent_url, precedent_summary, formula_version
       Role: severity dots cannot be vibes. Rule §1 mandates that any constant
             influencing display severity loads from a versioned reference
             table. A signal cannot be assigned severity 5 unless this table
             cites a specific FEC, OIG, or DOJ precedent that turned on the
             same predicate.

  3. derived.v_entity_fraud_evidence
       Joins entity -> firing signal -> raw rows that triggered (up to N).
       Includes deterministic URL builders for upstream verification:
         fec.gov/data/candidate/{cand_id}
         fec.gov/data/committee/{cmte_id}
         fec.gov/data/disbursements/?committee_id={cmte_id}
         oig.hhs.gov/exclusions (search by canonical name + DOB region)
         sam.gov/SAM/exclusion/{record_hash}
       Each row carries formula_version + source_vintage_hash from the raw
       table's ingestion provenance.

  4. derived.v_anomaly_score_percentile_by_kind_cycle
       Empirical CDF of risk_score per (entity_kind, cycle), so the UI's
       "above 99.4% of NJ candidates 2024" calibration label is computed,
       not made up. Refreshed when v_entity_fraud_risk refreshes; carries
       formula_version.

UX GUARANTEES (BINDING -- per .cursor/rules/verifiable-data.mdc)
----------------------------------------------------------------

  G1. No hover-only lineage. Source / vintage / formula are inline on every
      card. Rule §3 ("UI must surface the lineage").
  G2. No silent NULLs. A signal that requires LEIE data, with LEIE substrate
      empty, renders as a slate "no data -- LEIE substrate not yet loaded"
      callout, NOT as "no match found." Rule §5.
  G3. Empty-substrate honest mode. If raw.fec_* is empty, /risk renders the
      empty-state with explicit instructions, NOT zero rows pretending
      to be "no fraud detected." Rule §5.
  G4. Reproducibility footer. Every card shows formula_version +
      source_vintage_hash. Two visits with identical versions produce
      bit-identical evidence. Rule §2.
  G5. External verifiability. Every evidence row links to the upstream
      public source page so the user can verify our claim against the
      original data, NOT against our derived view.
  G6. Severity-precedent invariant. A signal cannot be displayed at
      severity ●●●●● unless ref.fraud_signal_severity_calibration cites
      a specific FEC/OIG/DOJ precedent. Severity is reference data,
      not vibes. Rule §1 + §4.
  G7. No "% probability of fraud" copy anywhere on the UI. Headline copy
      is "Structural anomaly score" with the peer-bucket calibration
      label. Reaffirms v3 framing.

SEQUENCING (gated on data; UI without data is theater)
------------------------------------------------------

  F1. bin/nj-fec bulk loader (cn / cm / indiv from files.fec.gov)
      Lights up 8 of 13 signals. Schema-version pinning via raw-table
      CHECK constraints; idempotent UPSERT on natural keys; emits
      governance.dataset_health 'materialized' signal so the freshness
      badge flips on first load. ~1 day.

  F2. ref.fraud_signal_human_explanation seed (mig + seed)
      One row per signal_id with a hand-authored template citing a
      specific federal authority. ~½ day.

  F3. ref.fraud_signal_severity_calibration seed (mig + seed)
      One row per signal_id citing the precedent that justified the
      severity. ~½ day.

  F4. derived.v_entity_fraud_evidence (mig + view)
      The join + URL-builder substrate Section 2 reads from. ~½ day.

  F5. derived.v_anomaly_score_percentile_by_kind_cycle (mig + view)
      Peer-CDF substrate for the headline calibration label. ~¼ day.

  F6. lib/risk-evidence.ts + components/SignalEvidenceCard.tsx
      Frontend data layer + RSC component. Mirrors lib/freshness.ts and
      components/FreshnessBadge.tsx structure. ~½ day.

  F7. /risk + /risk/[kind]/[id] rewrite
      Headline promotion (§3.4 pattern); Section 2 evidence cards;
      Section 3 cross-source projections; Section 4 methodology footer. ~½ day.

  F8. LEIE + SAM + USAspending ingesters (parallel: each ~½ day)
      Lights up the remaining 5 cross-source signals.

  F9. scripts/deploy_neon_pillar2_substrate.sh
      Mirror of deploy_neon_substrate.sh: applies pillar-2 migrations,
      bulk-loads, post-load asset-check execution, freshness backfill
      via derived.f_backfill_freshness_from_ingested_at(). ~¼ day.

  F10. Production verification. Visit /risk and /risk/{kind}/{id} in
       prod, confirm the dollar-style headline renders, evidence cards
       expand with fec.gov links, freshness badge flips. ~¼ day.

  Total: ~5-6 working sessions. UI work is gated on F1; nothing visible
  improves until raw.fec_* is populated.

================================================================================

TIER 4 DESIGN PROPOSAL v3 (2026-05-04) -- RISK-SCORE FUSION ENGINE
================================================================================
STATUS: design + L1 schema (migration 050) + signal->L1 adapter dispatcher
(migration 051) + L3a scoring function (migration 052) + Read API +
CLI + UI + Dagster wiring (asset + 3 asset checks) shipped. The
analyst queue lands at /fraud, is query-able via
  GET /fec/risk/entities?cycle=2024
  GET /fec/risk/entities/{kind}/{id}
  GET /fec/risk/entities/{kind}/{id}/csv
or as `nj-terminal fec-risk` / `fec-risk-entity`. Dagster auto-refreshes
L1 whenever raw.fec_candidate or raw.fec_committee materializes;
attached asset checks surface (a) empty L1 when cn is loaded, (b)
missing signal_ids from a silently-broken refresher, (c) L3a returning
score=0 for everyone when L1 has data. Triage workflow (L5) and the
unsupervised / supervised score layers (L3b, L3c) are gated on
production analyst-label volume, not engineering. Inverts the v2.A
model (eight flat per-signal lists) to an entity-first risk score with
multi-signal fusion, peer-group normalization, and a transparent
evidence trail.

Why redesign
------------
The user reviewed v2.A and asked for an engine that "surfaces potential fraud,
gives a % prediction, and backs it up with data." Two design pressures
follow from that ask:

  (a) v2.A is a CATALOG of eight lists. There is no entity-level synthesis,
      no fusion across signals, no peer-group context. A committee that
      fires on three signals at the 99th percentile is buried in three
      separate tabs alongside thousands of mild flags.

  (b) "% probability of fraud" requires labels we do not have at scale.
      Real enforcement actions (FEC RAD MURs, DOJ filings) produce on the
      order of dozens of NJ-relevant labels per cycle vs. tens of thousands
      of entities. A naive probability would be a confidently wrong
      number that an opposing lawyer ends the platform with on cross.

Honest framing: the user-facing scalar is a RISK SCORE (0-100), defined as
the entity's percentile of anomalousness within its peer bucket, NOT a
probability of fraud. A real `P(fraud | features)` field appears only after
the L5 triage queue produces enough analyst-confirmed labels to fit and
calibrate (isotonic) a held-out classifier. Until then the API field is
`risk_score_pct`, not `p_fraud`.

Five-layer architecture
-----------------------
L5  Triage workflow      analyst queue (sorted desc by risk_score) ->
                         confirm / reject / escalate; writes weak labels
                         + feature snapshots to governance.fraud_review
L4  Evidence panel       per-entity drill: score decomposition (which
                         signals contributed how many points), per-signal
                         raw rows, 5-NN peer comparison, network sub-graph
                         for relational signals
L3  Scoring engine       L3a rule-based weighted score (ship first;
                         transparent baseline)
                         L3b unsupervised anomaly score (IsolationForest /
                         Mahalanobis per peer bucket); ship second
                         L3c calibrated supervised classifier (only after
                         labels exist); ship last
L2  Entity feature store wide format keyed on entity; one row per
                         (cycle, entity_kind, entity_id) with the L1
                         observations pivoted to a feature vector
L1  Signal layer         long format observations:
                         (cycle, entity_kind, entity_id, signal_id,
                          raw_value, severity, peer_bucket, peer_percentile,
                          evidence_url, materialized_at)
                         Existing 8 v2.A signals slot in unchanged via
                         adapter; Tier-B relational signals are additive.

Entity kinds are first-class: 'committee', 'candidate', 'treasurer',
'address', 'donor_cluster'. Treasurer requires promotion (currently a
string column on cmte) via a canonicalization pass + a new derived.
fec_treasurer table -- without it, treasurer signals cannot have peer
percentiles.

L1 schema (proposed)
--------------------
  CREATE TABLE derived.fraud_signal_observation (
      cycle           CHAR(4)   NOT NULL,
      entity_kind     TEXT      NOT NULL,
      entity_id       TEXT      NOT NULL,
      signal_id       TEXT      NOT NULL,
      raw_value       NUMERIC,
      severity        SMALLINT  NOT NULL,   -- 1..5 ordinal, analyst-curated
      peer_bucket     TEXT      NOT NULL,   -- 'office=H|state=NJ|incumbent=challenger'
      peer_percentile NUMERIC   NOT NULL,   -- 0.0..1.0 within bucket
      evidence_url    TEXT      NOT NULL,
      materialized_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
      PRIMARY KEY (cycle, entity_kind, entity_id, signal_id)
  );

Two non-obvious design choices:

  * peer_percentile, NOT raw value, is what L3 consumes. Senators have more
    donors than House challengers; their address clusters are larger.
    Without peer-group normalization a senator looks "fraudulent" because
    they're big. Default bucket: (office, state, incumbency_class), with
    fallback to (office, state) when bucket size < 30.

  * severity is analyst-curated, not learned. A self-treasurer flag
    (severity 1) is qualitatively weaker evidence than a transfer cycle
    (severity 4). Encodes domain priors until labels arrive; under L3c
    these become Bayesian priors that get updated, not magic numbers.

L3a scoring formula (proposed)
------------------------------
  raw_sum(entity) = SUM_s phi(percentile_s, severity_s)
  phi(p, sev)     = sev * max(0, p - 0.95)^gamma
  score(entity)   = 100 * (1 - exp(-k * raw_sum))
  gamma           = 2
  k               = 50    -- tunable; calibrate against analyst-confirmed cases

Properties:
  * raw_sum = 0  -> score = 0          (no signals fired -> no risk)
  * raw_sum -> +inf -> score -> 100    (extreme stack of high-sev tail signals)
  * monotone in each (severity, percentile) pair
  * smooth (no kinks); percentile rank on score = percentile rank on raw_sum

Why NOT sigmoid: sigmoid(0) = 0.5, so 0 inputs -> score 50. That breaks the
"no signals fired => no risk" invariant. exp(-k*raw_sum) form has the
right zero-input semantics and the same bounded, monotone shape.

Tail-only contribution: a 60th-pct signal is "normal", not "lightly
suspicious". Squaring the tail makes the score insensitive to noise below
threshold and sensitive to extreme outliers. Additive fusion is deliberate:
multiplicative ("AND") fusion explodes when 3+ signals fire and is
indefensible in court. Print the formula on the evidence panel.

k=50 calibration check (sanity, not ground truth):
  * one signal at sev=3, p=0.99: raw_sum = 3*(0.04)^2 = 0.0048
                                  -> score ~ 21
  * five signals at sev=5, p=1.0: raw_sum = 5*5*(0.05)^2 = 0.0625
                                  -> score ~ 96
  * single sev=5 at p=1.0:        raw_sum = 5*(0.05)^2 = 0.0125
                                  -> score ~ 47
Re-tune k once we have an analyst-confirmed reference distribution.

Tier-B signals to add (next high-leverage tranche)
--------------------------------------------------
The v2.A roadmap items 1-12 (lines ~80-200 above) include several of these.
Reframed under the L1 contract:

  * transfer_cycle               money A->B->C->A within receipts graph
                                  (severity 4; entity_kind='committee')
  * inkind_for_pii_chain         in-kind contributions whose ultimate
                                  beneficiary is a different cycle's
                                  candidate (severity 3)
  * donor_employer_cluster       same employer name across N donors all
                                  maxing out to one committee (severity 3)
  * address_reuse_across_cycles  address appears with new committees every
                                  cycle (severity 4; shell churn)
  * treasurer_vendor_candidate_triangle
                                  treasurer also appears as vendor on the
                                  candidate's disbursements (severity 5;
                                  requires loading FEC oppexp files --
                                  not yet in raw.*)

Read API additions
------------------
  GET /fec/risk/entities         sorted queue, filterable by cycle/state/
                                  office/min_score; FecPagedResponse envelope
  GET /fec/risk/entities/{kind}/{id}
                                  full evidence panel payload (score
                                  decomposition + per-signal evidence +
                                  5-NN peers + network sub-graph)
  GET /fec/risk/entities/{kind}/{id}/csv   evidence trail as CSV

CLI parity adds
---------------
  nj-terminal fec-risk           sorted queue, mirror of /fec/risk/entities
  nj-terminal fec-risk-entity    single-entity evidence panel,
                                  --json mirrors GET /fec/risk/entities/{...}

UI changes
----------
  * /fraud landing tab becomes "Risk queue" (replaces "Fraud metrics" as
    default). Sorted desc by risk_score, paginated.
  * Click entity -> evidence panel (the v3 deliverable):
      - score headline + percentile within peer bucket
      - score decomposition stacked bar (which signals contributed)
      - per-signal evidence strip with deep links to underlying rows
      - 5-NN peer comparison (Mahalanobis on L2 features)
      - network sub-graph for relational signals
      - triage actions (confirm / reject / escalate / pending)
  * Existing per-signal flat lists demoted to a "Signal explorer" sub-tab.
    Analysts who want to inspect one signal still can.

L5 triage table (proposed)
--------------------------
  CREATE TABLE governance.fraud_review (
      cycle, entity_kind, entity_id,
      analyst_id            TEXT,
      decision              TEXT,        -- 'confirmed_finding'|'rejected'|
                                          -- 'escalate'|'pending'
      rationale             TEXT,
      decided_at            TIMESTAMPTZ,
      score_at_decision     NUMERIC,
      features_at_decision  JSONB        -- snapshot for label leakage defense
  );

Critical: features_at_decision is the snapshot the analyst saw. When L3c
trains, it fits to features-as-they-were-when-decided. Without this
snapshot, re-materialization between decision and training causes label
leakage and inflated holdout metrics.

Calibration discipline (UI contract)
------------------------------------
  Today's fields:
    risk_score          0..100 composite of L3a + L3b
    risk_percentile     within peer_bucket
    n_signals_fired     count of L1 observations for the entity
    score_decomposition list of (signal_id, contribution_points)

  Forbidden until L3c is live with a published calibration plot:
    p_fraud             P(fraud | features) calibrated on holdout
    p_fraud_ci          95% Brier-style or bootstrap CI

The day L3c ships, risk_score keeps its meaning; p_fraud is added
alongside, with the calibration plot in /docs and a CI on every prediction.
We do NOT retrofit a probability semantic onto the existing score.

Migration path (additive; v2.A survives intact)
-----------------------------------------------
  [DONE] 1. db/migrations/050_fraud_signal_observation.sql -- L1 long-format
            table + L2 wide-pivot view (derived.v_entity_fraud_features) +
            indexes + CHECK constraints (cycle regex, severity [1,5],
            peer_percentile [0,1], entity_kind whitelist, non-empty text
            cols). Schema is forward-compatible: entity_kind whitelist
            includes 'treasurer' and 'address' so the future canonical-
            treasurer / canonical-address tables can write without a
            schema migration. Static + live_pg tests in
            tests/test_migrations_fraud_signal.py
            (constraint rejection + L2 pivot aggregation correctness).

  [DONE] 2. db/migrations/051_fraud_signal_observation_adapter.sql --
            eight per-signal plpgsql refresher functions + a single
            top-level dispatcher
            (derived.refresh_all_fraud_signal_observations(cycle)).
            Each per-signal function does DELETE-then-INSERT on its own
            (cycle, signal_id) slice, so the dispatcher is idempotent
            and partial-state-safe under retries. Continuous signals
            use CUME_DIST() within peer_bucket; binary signals
            (no_pcc, broken_pcc, treasurer_is_candidate) use
            rate-based percentile. Static + live_pg tests in
            tests/test_migrations_fraud_signal_adapter.py exercise
            every signal against an engineered FEC fixture, plus
            idempotence + cycle-isolation invariants.

  [DONE] 2.5. Dagster asset wrapping the L1 dispatcher + asset checks.
            orchestration/assets.py adds two derived assets:

              derived.fraud_signal_observation  -- L1 table
                deps: raw.fec_candidate + raw.fec_committee
                compute: SELECT derived.refresh_all_fraud_signal_observations(cycle);
                         then SELECT signal_id, COUNT(*) ... GROUP BY signal_id
                         to populate per_signal_counts metadata
                automation_condition: eager (cn/cm refresh fans out)
                freshness_policy: FEC_FRESHNESS (matches upstream cadence)

              derived.v_entity_fraud_risk      -- L3a SQL view
                deps: derived.fraud_signal_observation
                compute: _derived_view_fingerprint over
                         (cycle, entity_kind, entity_id, ROUND(risk_score, 2))
                         so an upstream regression yields a different sha256
                automation_condition: eager

            Why raw.fec_candidate + raw.fec_committee but not
            raw.fec_contribution: v2.A's structural signals are cn/cm-only.
            Adding the contribution dep would over-trigger refreshes on the
            ~25M-row asset that the L1 dispatcher does not actually read.

            Why the L3a view skips the L2 intermediate
            (derived.v_entity_fraud_features): L2 is a view-on-view, not a
            materialized asset. Modeling it as a separate asset would add a
            graph node with no compute and confuse the lineage. The dep edge
            L3a -> L1 is truthful: any L1 refresh propagates to L3a's
            fingerprint.

            orchestration/asset_checks.py adds three asset checks. The
            schema CHECK constraints on derived.fraud_signal_observation
            already enforce per-row domains (severity in [1,5],
            peer_percentile in [0,1], evidence_url NOT NULL/empty) at
            INSERT time, so we DELIBERATELY do NOT duplicate those:

              row_count_positive (L1)
                Vacuous-pass when raw.fec_candidate is empty (fresh
                deployment, pre-loader). Fail when cn is loaded but L1
                emitted zero rows -- i.e. the dispatcher silently broke.

              signal_coverage (L1)
                Asserts the DISTINCT signal_id set in L1 equals the
                canonical FRAUD_STRUCTURAL_SIGNAL_IDS tuple
                (the eight v2.A signals). The schema CHECK constrains
                what's there; this check is the only line of defense
                against what's MISSING -- e.g., one of the eight per-
                signal refresher functions returns zero rows because
                its underlying derived.fec_* view regressed to empty
                after a schema drift.

              risk_score_positive_when_l1_present (L3a view)
                Vacuous-pass when L1 is empty. Fails when L1 has rows
                but L3a returns risk_score=0 for every entity -- which
                means either the L3a formula constants drifted (gamma,
                k, percentile floor), the L2 pivot returned only
                below-threshold percentiles, or the view's join broke.

            FRAUD_STRUCTURAL_SIGNAL_IDS lives in orchestration.assets and
            is pinned by test_orchestration::test_fraud_structural_signal_ids_match_dispatcher
            against a hardcoded expected set (so drift in EITHER the
            Python constant OR the SQL dispatcher trips the test).

            Tests:
              tests/test_orchestration.py (4 new unit tests)
                - structural-signal-id constant matches dispatcher (sorted)
                - L1 asset deps == {raw.fec_candidate, raw.fec_committee}
                  + automation_condition declared
                - L3a-view deps == {derived.fraud_signal_observation}
                  + automation_condition declared
                - all 3 asset checks registered in ALL_ASSET_CHECKS

              tests/test_orchestration_fraud_pg.py (7 new live_pg tests)
                Exercises the SQL inside each check against a real DB
                in two scenarios: (A) clean DB -- all three vacuous-pass
                with documented reason values; (B) populated DB
                (engineered anomalies + dispatcher run) -- all three
                pass with the expected positive-state metadata. The
                failure-mode cases (e.g., dispatcher silently dropped a
                signal) are not tested because crafting that DB state
                requires either monkey-patching plpgsql functions or
                seeding raw data that triggers exactly seven of eight
                signals -- both brittle for marginal value over the
                registry test that already pins all three checks.

            Quality gates: ruff + mypy clean; 23/23 orchestration unit
            tests + 7/7 fraud-asset-check live_pg tests + full live_pg
            fraud chain still green.

            Pre-existing test isolation bug (out of scope for this PR):
            21 *_requires_pg_dsn tests in test_terminal_cli.py fail when
            running the full suite with PG_TEST_DSN set, because an
            earlier test sets os.environ["PG_DSN"] without restoring it
            and CliRunner.invoke(env={}) only adds overrides instead of
            clearing inherited env. They pass cleanly in isolation. Same
            issue noted at end of step 6.

  [DONE] 3. db/migrations/052_fraud_risk_score.sql -- L3a scoring
            SQL function:
              derived.fraud_risk_score(severities SMALLINT[],
                                       peer_percentiles NUMERIC[])
              RETURNS NUMERIC(5, 2)
            Pure plpgsql, IMMUTABLE, PARALLEL SAFE. Implements the
            documented formula
              phi(p, sev) = sev * max(0, p - 0.95)^2
              raw_sum     = SUM(phi)
              score       = 100 * (1 - exp(-50 * raw_sum))
            Plus derived.v_entity_fraud_risk: read-surface view that
            composes L2 (v_entity_fraud_features) with the score column
            and preserves every parallel array the evidence panel will
            need (signals_fired, severities, peer_percentiles,
            peer_buckets, raw_values, evidence_urls). ORDER BY
            risk_score DESC = the analyst queue. Tests in
            tests/test_migrations_fraud_risk_score.py: 7 static (file
            exists, function + view declared, gamma=2 + k=50 not
            drifted, Python reference reproduces calibration anchors)
            and 15 live_pg (calibration anchors at 21.34/95.61/46.47;
            below-threshold contribution = 0; mismatched-array RAISE;
            monotonicity in both inputs; 100-clamp; view ordering;
            end-to-end with the step-2 dispatcher).

  [DONE] 4. Read API routes (queue + panel; CSV deferred to step 4.5):
              GET /fec/risk/entities                 -- sorted queue
              GET /fec/risk/entities/{kind}/{id}     -- evidence panel
            Backed by serving/queries_fec_risk.py (whitelisted sort,
            cycle/entity_kind/signal_id/min_score/max_score filters,
            pagination) and serving/routes/fec_risk.py (thin route
            shell, KeyError -> 400, missing entity -> 404). The panel
            pivots the L2 parallel arrays into a list of
            RiskSignalObservation entries and computes per-signal
            score decomposition in Python (phi_contribution +
            score_share_pct) using the same constants as migration
            052 (gamma=2, k=50, percentile_floor=0.95). The queue
            endpoint echoes the effective filters in its response so
            the UI can render "showing entities with risk_score >=
            60 in 2024" without threading params through state.
            Filter by signal_id uses ANY(signals_fired) over the
            ARRAY-AGG'd column the L2 view already exposes -- no
            extra subquery needed. Pydantic models RiskQueueRow,
            RiskQueueResponse, RiskSignalObservation, RiskEntityPanel
            in serving/models.py. Tests in
            tests/test_serving_fec_risk.py: 23 unit (constants/L1
            domain alignment, decomposition math, validators,
            pagination clamps, schema acceptance) plus 14 live_pg
            (sorted-DESC default, entity_kind / signal_id / min_score
            filters, 400 on bad sort / bad entity_kind / inverted
            score range, 404 on unknown entity, panel observations
            sorted by score_share_pct DESC, share_pct sums to 100
            within rounding when any signal exceeds the floor).
            Wired into serving/app.py.

  [DONE] 4.5 GET /fec/risk/entities/{kind}/{id}/csv -- evidence-trail
             CSV (one row per fired signal; entity columns repeated
             on every row so the file is self-contained when an
             analyst pivots / filters by signal in Excel). 18-column
             contract pinned in queries_fec_risk.EVIDENCE_CSV_COLUMNS
             (entity-level: cycle, entity_kind, entity_id, risk_score,
             n_signals_fired, max_severity, max_peer_percentile,
             avg_peer_percentile, primary_peer_bucket,
             last_observation_at; observation-level: signal_id,
             severity, peer_percentile, peer_bucket, raw_value,
             phi_contribution, score_share_pct, evidence_url).
             Routing-order subtlety: the CSV route MUST be declared
             BEFORE the JSON panel route in serving/routes/fec_risk.py
             because the panel uses {entity_id:path} (to support
             addresses with embedded slashes) and ``:path`` greedily
             consumes ``/csv`` as part of entity_id. CLI extension:
             nj-terminal fec-risk-entity gained --csv and -o flags
             (mutually exclusive with --json; same pattern as
             fec-metric --csv). Same-shape output as the HTTP route
             so the CLI is byte-equivalent to ``curl
             /fec/risk/entities/.../csv > x.csv``. Tests:
             tests/test_serving_fec_risk.py +6 (column-contract pin,
             flattener unit test, empty-panel safety, live header +
             rows + quoted comma in entity_id, 404 for unknown
             entity, 400 for unknown kind); tests/test_terminal_cli.py
             +2 (--json/--csv mutual exclusivity, mocked CSV output
             matches column header + observation count + comma
             quoting).

  [DONE] 5. UI: /fraud now lands on the entity-first risk queue tab
            by default; the existing metric-explorer was renamed to
            "Signal explorer" and demoted to second position. Three-

              derived.refresh_all_fraud_signal_observations('2024')`

              100 outlier rank, NOT a probability of fraud) inline so
              the analyst can't miss it.
            * serving/static/fraud/fraud.css: added risk-score-badge
              (4 color bands keyed to the L3a anchors -- high>=80,

  [DONE] 6. CLI: nj-terminal fec-risk + fec-risk-entity (CLI_VERSION
            bumped to 0.32.0). Both commands call queries_fec_risk
            directly (no HTTP hop, same pattern as fec-metrics). The
            queue command (`fec-risk`) takes --cycle, --entity-kind
            (Click choice over VALID_ENTITY_KINDS), --signal-id,
            --min-score / --max-score (FloatRange[0,100], rejected by
            Click before reaching SQL), --sort-by (Click choice over
            RISK_SORT_COLS), --sort-dir, --limit / --offset, --json.
            JSON output is the same RiskQueueResponse shape the HTTP
            layer returns, including the filters echo. The panel
            command (`fec-risk-entity <kind> <id>`) takes positional
            entity_kind + entity_id, --cycle, --json, and renders a
            header line (entity_id, kind, cycle, risk_score,
            n_signals, max_sev, max_pct, peer_bucket) plus a Rich
            table of observations sorted by score_share_pct DESC
            with phi_contribution + share% + evidence_url columns.
            KeyError from the query layer (defense-in-depth whitelist
            failure) -> click.ClickException (exit 1); missing PG_DSN
            -> exit 2; missing entity -> click.ClickException with
            the same wording as the HTTP 404 detail. Added both to
            _TERMINAL_SUBCOMMANDS (the relay invariant test catches
            registration drift). Tests in tests/test_terminal_cli.py:
            14 new (--help works without PG_DSN, missing-PG_DSN guard
            for both, Click choice rejection for entity_kind /
            sort-by / out-of-range min-score, JSON-empty-queue
            envelope shape, KeyError -> ClickException, 404 ->
            ClickException, fake-panel JSON shape, nj relay forwards
            both subcommands).

  [TODO] 7. L5 triage UI + governance.fraud_review table (with
            features_at_decision JSONB snapshot for label-leakage defense).
            Only after L3a is in prod for a few weeks so the queue
            has signal.

  [TODO] 8. L3b unsupervised anomaly score (IsolationForest per
            peer_bucket); behind feature flag until audited on
            known cases.

  [TODO] 9. L3c calibrated classifier (only when label volume justifies
            it); publish calibration plot + holdout Brier/log-loss/AUC.

Steps 1-6 are roughly two weeks of focused work. Steps 7-9 are the
long tail, gated on operator data, not on engineering.

Open decisions PINNED 2026-05-04

  [PINNED yes]   Treasurer is a first-class entity_kind. v3 step 2 uses
                 tres_nm_canonical (regex-normalized name) as the
                 entity_id pending a future ref.fec_treasurer canonical
                 table; cross-state name conflation is a known
                 limitation, documented in migration 051.

  [PINNED my-pick] Peer bucket granularity per signal:
                   - candidate signals: 'office=<>|state=<>'
                     (ICI dropped for v3 step 2; single-cycle 2024
                     data has too few candidates per (office, state, ici)
                     for a stable percentile. Add ICI when 2020+2022
                     cycles are loaded.)
                   - committee signals: 'state=<cmte_st>' (or
                     'office=<linked_cand>|state=<linked_cand>' when
                     the signal is candidate-rooted)
                   - treasurer signals: 'kind=treasurer' (single
                     national bucket; per-state bucketing pending
                     ref.fec_treasurer)
                   - address signals: 'state=<>'
                   - donor_cluster: deferred (no donor signals in v2.A)

                 Continuous signals use CUME_DIST(); binary signals use
                 1 - (n_flagged_in_bucket / n_bucket_population).

  [PINNED yes]   MetricSpec catalog stays as the analyst-facing
                 "Signal explorer" sub-tab; entity-keyed risk score
                 becomes the default landing on /fraud.

The user requested "advanced fraud detection" and (after delivery) called out
that the fraud surface was not visible from the front end. Both shipped:
the metric layer is live, the UI tab renders the eight signals against real
loaded 2024 data, and the housing app cross-links to /fraud.

Status
------
* indiv24.zip ingested: 58,210,004 individual contributions in
  raw.fec_contribution (cycle 2024). Single atomic COPY, ~12 minutes
  end-to-end from cached zip.
* 8 structural fraud signals implemented as derived.fec_* views;
  every one returns positive flags against the real 2024 cycle.
* /fec/metrics + /fec/metrics/_summary + /fec/metrics/{id}[/csv]
  serve catalog, summary counts, paginated rows, and constant-memory

  tab) with a sidebar selector, per-metric description + threshold

* Housing UI / gains a prominent CIVIC INTEGRITY -> cross-link.
* /fec/summary now caches in-process for 5 minutes; first call on
  the 58M-row table is one-shot ~30s, subsequent calls < 5ms.
* Quality gates: ruff + mypy clean; pytest 395/395 (27 new metrics
  tests + the existing 368).

What "advanced fraud detection" looks like in v2.A
---------------------------------------------------
Eight structural signals (computable from cn + cm only -- no
contribution dependency). Each is a derived view + catalog entry +
generic API/UI plumbing; adding a Tier B signal is one view + one
catalog dict entry.

  | Signal id                    | Real-data 2024 hits | What it flags |
  |------------------------------|--------------------:|---------------|
  | treasurer_concentration      | 1,072 | Treasurer name on >= 2 distinct cmtes; >=15 = lead |
  | candidate_no_pcc             | 1,760 | Candidate w/o declared Principal Campaign Committee |
  | candidate_broken_pcc         |   155 | Candidate's cand_pcc not in raw.fec_committee |
  | candidate_multiple_pccs      |    49 | >1 'P'-designated committee for one candidate |
  | committee_address_clusters   |   515 | >=3 cmtes at same canonical street address |
  | committee_name_collisions    |   191 | Same canonical name, different cmte_ids |
  | candidate_namesakes          |   254 | Same canonical name, different cand_ids, same office/state |
  | treasurer_is_candidate       | 1,568 | Self-treasurer; collapses audit chain |

Top-1 examples (DATWYLER 359 cmtes in 43 states; PO BOX 183 Hudson WI
hosting 175 cmtes; SMITH-namesake clusters; etc.) match known
real-world patterns from FEC enforcement actions, indicating the
signals are well-formed and not measuring noise.

Architecture (v2.A delta)
-------------------------
db/migrations/040_fec_metrics_structural.sql
  8 derived.fec_* views. All cycle-scoped. Each row carries a
  severity_score column on a uniform [1, 1000] scale so the UI can
  rank signals consistently. Canonicalization (REGEXP_REPLACE upper +
  whitespace collapse) catches "PO BOX 99" / "P.O. BOX 99" /
  "P O BOX 99" as one address. Views, not materialized tables --
  cn/cm are 10K-21K rows so per-query cost is < 50 ms total.

db/migrations/041_relax_fec_indiv_amt_check.sql
  raw.fec_contribution.transaction_amt CHECK widened from +/-$10M
  to +/-$1B. Adelson's $25M Future Forward contribution (the largest
  individual political donation in US history) hit the old constraint
  on 8/13/2024. Substrate honesty: raw.* mirrors what FEC published;
  anomaly detection lives in derived.*, not in CHECK constraints.

serving/queries_fec_metrics.py
  MetricSpec dataclass + _METRIC_CATALOG dict. get_catalog() returns
  metrics in (tier, id) order. metric_counts() does N small COUNT(*)
  per-view (catalog growth past ~30 will switch to UNION ALL).
  list_metric() and stream_metric() are generic -- they take metric_id
  and apply cycle filter + per-metric sort whitelist + pagination /
  named cursor. Adding a metric is one MetricSpec entry; no API/UI
  changes required.

serving/routes/fec_metrics.py
  Three JSON routes (catalog, summary, paginated rows) plus one
  CSV streaming route. Bad sort_by returns 400 with allowed-column
  hint; unknown metric_id returns 404 with catalog hint. Errors
  surface BEFORE the streaming generator opens, so the client never
  gets a 200 followed by a half-baked CSV body.

serving/models.py
  FraudMetricCatalogEntry / FraudMetricSummary / FraudMetricResult
  Pydantic models. rows is dict[str, Any] (each metric has its own
  shape; the UI is a generic table renderer).

serving/static/fraud/index.html + fraud.css + fraud.js
  Metrics tab now the default landing tab. Two-pane layout: 280px
  signal sidebar with live-count badges + drill-down right pane
  with description, threshold note, controls (cycle/sort/dir),
  Tabulator with columns derived dynamically from row keys, paginator,
  CSV export. URL hash deep-link: /fraud#m=<id>&cycle=<cycle>.

serving/queries_fec.py (get_summary)
  Process-local 5-minute TTL cache. contributions_total uses
  pg_class.reltuples for production-sized tables (instant) with
  exact-COUNT fallback for empty / small / unanalyzed tables.
  First miss with 58M rows: ~30s. Cache hit: <5ms.

serving/static/index.html + styles.css (housing)
  CIVIC INTEGRITY -> cross-link in the top-right nav. Pill style,
  amber accent, links straight to /fraud.

Testing
-------
tests/test_serving_fec_metrics.py (NEW, 27 tests, all green):
  - Schema validation for the 3 new Pydantic models
  - Catalog ordering + lookup
  - Pagination + sort-direction clampers
  - Live integration: synthetic cn/cm with KNOWN anomalies
    (multi-committee treasurer DOE JOHN x3, PO BOX 99 cluster x3,
    SMITH JOHN namesakes, ADAMS self-treasurer, etc.). Confirms
    each signal flags exactly the engineered rows.
  - 404 for unknown metric_id; 400 for unsafe sort_by
  - CSV streaming: header + body + array fields rendered as
    {a,b,c} (Postgres array literal style)

tests/test_serving_fec.py (UPDATED):
  - Added clear_summary_cache() fixture call so cache doesn't leak
    between tests with different synthetic loads.

What is NOT done in v2.A
-------------------------
* TIER B (contribution-level) fraud signals. The indiv data is now
  loaded and ANALYZED. The migration scaffold exists; the planned
  set is below.
* Materialized derived.fec_summary_snapshot (replacement for the
  in-process TTL cache). The cache is fine for one process; for
  multi-worker deployments the right answer is a refresh-on-load
  table.
* Cross-cycle longitudinal metrics (only 2024 loaded). Once 2020
  and 2022 are ingested, the same registry pattern handles trend
  metrics.

NEXT UP -- TIER 4 v2.B (CONTRIBUTION-LEVEL FRAUD METRICS)
=========================================================
With raw.fec_contribution at 58M rows, the following derived views
become possible. Each will follow the v2.A registry pattern: one
view + one MetricSpec entry; no API/UI churn. Plan ordered by
expected real-world signal strength.

  1. donor_round_number_rate
       % of >$200 contributions at exactly $1000/$2000/$2500/$5000
       per committee. Straw-donor networks over-cluster on round
       numbers; legitimate fundraising has a long tail.

  2. donor_velocity_30d
       Donor + cycle pairs with >= N contributions in any rolling
       30-day window. Bundling fingerprint.

  3. same_day_bundling
       (cmte_id, transaction_date, transaction_amount, employer)
       clusters with >= N rows. Classic same-day-same-amount bundle.

  4. donor_state_zip_mismatch
       Reported donor_state vs. ZIP-derived state. Catches forged
       residency declarations (federal donation eligibility hinges
       on residency).

  5. memo_abuse_rate
       Per-committee ratio of memo (MEMO_CD='X') to non-memo rows.
       Memo lines are sub-line itemizations; abnormally high rates
       indicate joint-fundraising allocation games.

  6. self_funded_ratio
       Per-NJ-candidate: % of inbound $$ from a committee linked to
       the same candidate. Detects pure self-funding cycles.

  7. donor_employer_concentration
       Top employers by aggregated contribution to one committee.
       Surfaces concentrated-industry capture (and bundling pools).

  8. nj_out_of_state_money
       Per-NJ-candidate: % of $$ from non-NJ-state donors. Already
       computable via /fec/money-to-nj joins; this metric just
       packages the ratio.

  9. donor_employer_blank_rate_high_dollar
       % of >$200 contributions with empty employer/occupation per
       committee. Required fields per FEC rules; high blank rates
       are a paperwork-fraud signal.

 10. donor_canonical_conflation
       Same canonical (name, city, state, zip) on multiple sub_ids
       within a single cycle, aggregated. Powers the "who's actually
       this person?" view that current donor-search products lack.

 11. donor_velocity_first_filing_cluster
       Per-committee: clusters of new-this-cycle donors all giving
       within their first reported quarter. Smurfing pattern.

 12. cycle_raise_anomalies
       Per-office cohort: z-score of cycle total raised. Outlier
       races worth attention.

NEXT UP -- TIER 4 v3 (LONGITUDINAL + MATERIALIZED)  [SUPERSEDED 2026-05-04]
==================================================
SUPERSEDED by "TIER 4 DESIGN PROPOSAL v3 -- RISK-SCORE FUSION ENGINE" at
the top of this file. The three bullets below remain technical
PREREQUISITES for the redesign (peer-bucket percentiles need multi-cycle
samples; the snapshot/history tables are required for L2 correctness and
analyst-facing trend sparklines), but they are no longer the v3 product
goal -- they are folded into Step 2 of the redesign migration path.

* Ingest 2020 and 2022 cycles (will surface cross-cycle name reuse,
  sleeper-committee resurrection, treasurer career arcs).
* derived.fec_summary_snapshot table refreshed by Dagster asset on
  every raw.fec_* load; replaces the in-process TTL cache and gives
  multi-worker correctness.
* derived.fec_metric_history -- daily snapshot of metric_counts so
  the UI can render trend sparklines per signal.

TIER 4 CHECKPOINT v1.5 (2026-04-29 LATE EVE) -- DEDICATED FRAUD TERMINAL UI
================================================================================
The user requested a SEPARATE front end for the FEC / civic-integrity data
("wire all the fraud data to a separate front end for now complete with
many filters exports and details for me to peruse"). Built and validated.

Architecture
------------
The fraud terminal is a separate HTML+JS bundle that shares the existing
FastAPI app process and Postgres pool with the housing UI. Two endpoints:
  * GET /          -> housing index.html (unchanged)
  * GET /fraud     -> fraud/index.html (new)
Static assets at /static/fraud/{index.html,fraud.js,fraud.css}.

Read API surface (15 routes total)
----------------------------------
JSON list/detail (11 routes, all under /fec/*):
  /fec/summary                      -- cross-table snapshot for the dashboard
  /fec/cycles                       -- distinct cycles (for filter dropdowns)
  /fec/states                       -- distinct cand_office_st values
  /fec/parties                      -- distinct cand_pty_affiliation values
  /fec/offices                      -- distinct cand_office values (H/S/P)
  /fec/candidates                   -- filterable + paginated list
  /fec/candidates/{cand_id}         -- detail + linked PCC committees
  /fec/committees                   -- filterable + paginated list
  /fec/committees/{cmte_id}         -- detail + linked candidate +
                                       25 most recent contributions
  /fec/contributions                -- filterable + paginated list
                                       (against public.v_fec_contribution)
  /fec/money-to-nj                  -- headline view, filterable + paginated

CSV streaming exports (4 routes under /fec/export/*.csv):
  /fec/export/candidates.csv        -- same filter set as JSON
  /fec/export/committees.csv
  /fec/export/contributions.csv
  /fec/export/money-to-nj.csv

UI structure
------------
4 tabs (Candidates, Committees, Contributions, Money -> NJ). Each:
  * Filter bar at top, populated from /fec/cycles + /fec/states +
    /fec/parties + /fec/offices on first paint
  * Tabulator-powered virtualized table with sortable columns,
    fitDataStretch layout, and an "Export CSV" button that wires the
    current filter set to the matching /fec/export/*.csv URL
  * Click-to-drill side detail panel: candidate clicks fetch the
    /fec/candidates/{cand_id} detail (renders linked committees);
    committee clicks fetch /fec/committees/{cmte_id} detail (renders
    linked candidate + 25 most recent contributions); contribution
    rows render the row data inline (no extra fetch)

Architectural choices made today
---------------------------------
* SHARED APP, SEPARATE BUNDLE. One nj-serve process serves both UIs +
  both API surfaces (housing /burden/*, fraud /fec/*). Same DB pool,
  same observability middleware, same lifespan. The two UIs are
  separate HTML+JS bundles only.

* SERVER-SIDE FILTERING + PAGINATION. The UI never holds a full
  result set client-side. limit + offset go to the API; total_count
  comes back in a FecPagedResponse envelope so the UI renders
  pagination controls without a second /count round trip. A Tabulator-
  client-only table would have crippled the contributions tab once
  raw.fec_contribution holds the full ~25M-row indiv24 dataset.

* STREAMING CSV EXPORTS WITH SERVER-SIDE NAMED CURSORS. Each export
  uses conn.cursor(name=...) which streams from Postgres in itersize
  chunks; the FastAPI generator yields one CSV line at a time; the
  client gets Transfer-Encoding: chunked. Memory stays flat regardless
  of result set size. 100k row hard cap for safety; documented in the
  X-Export-Cap-Rows response header so callers can detect truncation.

* SORT WHITELIST = DEFENSE IN DEPTH. Every endpoint has an explicit
  per-table dict of sortable columns. An attacker passing
  sort_by='1; DROP TABLE raw.fec_candidate' silently falls back to
  the first whitelisted column rather than 4xx-leaking that the
  column doesn't exist (or worse, getting a parameter through to
  Identifier()). Pinned by unit test.

* EMPTY-STRING FILTERS ARE NOT PREDICATES. UI dropdowns set value=""
  for "all"; the WHERE-clause builders MUST treat that as "no
  predicate", not as `col = ''` (which would silently return zero
  rows). Pinned by unit test test_build_candidate_where_skips_empty_
  string_filters.

* PYDANTIC RESPONSE MODELS = OPENAPI CONTRACT. Every endpoint declares
  a response_model so /openapi.json carries the full schema. Frozen
  models prevent post-construction mutation (DTO discipline).

* CSS [hidden] OVERRIDE. The detail-panel uses display: flex when
  open; HTML's `hidden` attribute is overridden by that and would
  fail to hide the panel. .detail-panel[hidden] { display: none; }
  restores intent (caught by browser smoke test, not by unit tests).

What's new today
----------------
* serving/models.py                    -- 9 new Pydantic models for FEC
                                          response schemas
* serving/queries_fec.py               -- 600+ line query layer with
                                          filter dataclasses, WHERE-clause
                                          builders, sort whitelists,
                                          server-side cursor streamers
* serving/routes/fec.py                -- 11 JSON endpoints
* serving/routes/fec_export.py         -- 4 CSV streaming endpoints

                                          GET /fraud serves fraud/index.html

                                          unit + live-pg integration)

TIER 4 CHECKPOINT v1 (2026-04-29 EVE) -- FEC BULK INGESTER, END-TO-END
================================================================================
The platform now has its first civic-integrity ingester. FEC bulk data --
the canonical free source for federal candidates, committees, and itemized
individual contributions -- is stateless, idempotent, vintaged, queryable,
and wired into Dagster with five asset checks.

Why FEC first
-------------
FEC is the unique data source that simultaneously satisfies:
  * Stable entity backbone (FECID; 9-char, 18 years of stability) that
    other Tier 4 datasets join into (PACs that hire lobbyists in
    Senate LDA; PACs that get federal contracts in USAspending; donors
    that show up in NJ ELEC + FEC for state/federal stacking).
  * NJ axis is first-class (cand_office_st='NJ' yields 257 candidates
    for the 2024 cycle, validated live 2026-04-29).
  * Bulk-downloadable, no auth, S3-backed (Accept-Ranges: bytes,
    ETag-stable), so conditional GET works.
  * Time series back to 1980 (we ingest current-cycle by default).
  * Dollar amounts in every contribution row -- fraud signal substrate.

Tables
------
raw.fec_candidate    -- Candidate Master (cn{yy}.zip), PK (cycle, cand_id)
raw.fec_committee    -- Committee Master (cm{yy}.zip), PK (cycle, cmte_id)
raw.fec_contribution -- Individual Contribs (indiv{yy}.zip), PK sub_id
                        (large; ~25M rows for a presidential cycle)

Canonical views (public.v_*)
----------------------------
v_fec_candidate                  -- typed pass-through
v_fec_committee                  -- typed pass-through
v_fec_contribution               -- MMDDYYYY -> DATE; is_memo flag
v_fec_nj_candidates              -- cand_office_st='NJ' slice
v_fec_money_to_nj_candidates     -- HEADLINE: contributions to NJ-
                                    candidate committees, joined three
                                    deep (contrib + committee + nj cand)

Architectural choices made today
---------------------------------
* RAW MIRRORS SOURCE BYTES. transaction_dt stays as 8-char MMDDYYYY in
  raw; cooking happens in public.v_fec_contribution. No row drops on
  malformed dates -- we coerce to NULL with COALESCE and CASE so the
  raw -> view path is total.

* SUB_ID IS THE PK ON raw.fec_contribution. Per FEC docs sub_id is
  globally unique. cycle is denormalized into the row (not part of
  PK) for partition-friendly query plans; DELETE WHERE cycle=%s
  before COPY guarantees idempotent re-load.

* QUOTE_CHAR=NONE FOR FEC PARSING. Real FEC bulk contains literal
  double-quote characters in plain text fields ('JOHN "JACK" DOE',
  '"VAL" VALMA PAUL'). Polars's default quote_char='"' aborts on
  these. Setting quote_char=None disables CSV quote interpretation
  entirely, matching FEC's actual unquoted byte format. Pinned with
  test_parse_handles_literal_double_quote_in_name regression test.

* COPY ... FROM STDIN WITH (FORMAT csv, DELIMITER '|', QUOTE E'\b').
  The bell-character QUOTE escape disables Postgres's CSV quote
  interpretation while keeping FORMAT csv's empty-field-as-NULL
  semantics. Matches the FEC byte format byte-for-byte and lets us
  stream the 4.2 GB indiv24.zip directly from a zipfile.open() into
  COPY without ever materializing the contents in Python memory.

* MEMO_CD='X' DOUBLE-COUNT HAZARD HANDLED. The is_memo column in
  v_fec_contribution flags itemized sub-line entries; the headline
  view exposes the flag and downstream summable analytics filter
  WHERE NOT is_memo. COALESCE(memo_cd='X', false) so NULL memo_cd
  is False, not NULL (otherwise WHERE NOT is_memo would drop the row
  -- 3-valued logic trap caught by integration test).

What's new today
----------------
* db/migrations/038_raw_fec.sql        -- 3 raw tables, 7 indexes
* db/migrations/039_fec_canonical_views.sql -- 5 cooked views
* db/seeds/007_release_calendar_fec.sql -- 'raw.fec' release entry
* ingestion/fec.py                     -- fetch+parse+load + Click CLI
                                          (~520 lines, fully typed)
* tests/test_fec.py                    -- 23 tests (parser + integration)
* orchestration/assets.py              -- 3 SDAs (raw.fec_candidate/
                                          committee/contribution),
                                          FEC_FRESHNESS=21d/16d
* orchestration/asset_checks.py        -- 5 new checks
* pyproject.toml                       -- nj-ingest-fec script entry

Validated against real FEC 2024 data (2026-04-29 EVE)
------------------------------------------------------
* cn24.zip (357 KB) -> 9,804 candidates loaded, 257 NJ
  (43 senate + 214 house; Booker, Kim, Andrews, Lautenberg legacy)
* cm24.zip (884 KB) -> 20,941 committees loaded, 454 NJ-domiciled
* PCC linkage works: BOOKER, CORY A. -> CORY BOOKER FOR SENATE
                     KIM, ANDY        -> ANDY KIM FOR NEW JERSEY

  with `nj-ingest-fec load --cycle 2024 --files cn,cm,indiv`.

What's NOT done (deliberately deferred)
---------------------------------------
* Schedules for the FEC SDAs (orchestration/schedules.py). The SDAs
  are registered in ALL_ASSETS so the Dagster daemon will surface
  them; an operator can manually materialize today, and a cron
  schedule is the next iteration.
* Materialized derived tables on top of raw.fec_*. The headline
  v_fec_money_to_nj_candidates is a view; query latency on a 25M-row
  raw.fec_contribution will become noticeable. Materialize when we
  add interactive UI for FEC.
* Donor-entity resolution (DOE, JANE @ ACME CORP -> single donor
  identity). This is the v1.5 step and lives in derived/, not
  ingestion/.
* Loading itcont (4.2 GB) into the live nj DB. Requires ~10-15 min
  of operator time and ~30 GB of free disk; documented but not
  triggered by default.

================================================================================
TIER 4 ROADMAP (v2-v5, BACKBURNER, ORDERED BY VALUE/COST RATIO)
================================================================================
Each entry below is a stub anchor for the next ingester. Implementation
follows the same 8-step pattern as v1 (FEC):
  1. raw migration with PK + indexes
  2. canonical view migration (NJ filter + light typing)
  3. release_calendar seed entry
  4. ingester module under ingestion/ (fetch, parse, load, Click CLI)
  5. Dagster SDA + freshness policy
  6. Asset checks (row count, NJ coverage, referential integrity)
  7. Tests (parser unit + live_pg integration)
  8. CLI script entry in pyproject.toml + README + work_left checkpoint

----------------------------------------
v2: NJ YourMoney state expenditures
----------------------------------------
Source:    https://yourmoney.nj.gov/transparency/expenditures
Why:       NJ-axis state spending visible at vendor level; joins to
           NJ ELEC donations (when v5 lands) for state-level pay-to-play
           inference. Highest-value NJ-specific dataset after FEC.
Format:    CSV download per fiscal year; ~5-10 MB.
Schema:    department, vendor_name, vendor_address, fiscal_year,
           expenditure_amount, fund_type, program_name, ...
Hazards:   Vendor names are non-canonical (use _base.canonical_employer_name).
           Some entries are summary rows -- need to filter by
           transaction_type.
Effort:    ~1 session.

----------------------------------------
v3: SAM.gov exclusions (debarment / suspension)
----------------------------------------
Source:    https://sam.gov/api/prod/exclusions/v4 (free public extract)
           or https://sam.gov/data-services/Exclusions/Public%20V2
Why:       Federally debarred entities. Joins to FEC committees +
           USAspending awards as a fraud-signal flag.
Format:    Daily extract (CSV/JSON), ~50K active records.
Schema:    Name, address, exclusion_type, classification, agency,
           dt_termination, ...
Hazards:   API requires a free SAM.gov account for some endpoints; the
           public extract does not.
Effort:    ~0.5 session.

----------------------------------------
v4: Senate LDA lobbying disclosures
----------------------------------------
Source:    https://lda.senate.gov/api/v1/filings/ (REST; free, no key)
Why:       Direct lobbying ledger; joins to FEC committees + USAspending
           awards via lobbyist_org -> committee linkage.
Format:    JSON paginated; ~75K filings/year.
Schema:    filing_uuid, registrant, client, lobbyists[],
           expenses, registrant_address, ...
Hazards:   API rate-limited (60/min anon); paginate carefully and cache
           ETags for re-runs.
Effort:    ~1 session.

----------------------------------------
v5: NJ ELEC campaign finance
----------------------------------------
Source:    https://www.elec.nj.gov/forcandidates/forcandidatescommitteesreports.htm
           (HTML scrape; no public bulk download)
Why:       State-level campaign finance for NJ; closes the federal
           (FEC) + state (NJ ELEC) loop for full pay-to-play visibility
           at the donor level.
Hazards:   * Site is behind Incapsula bot protection (verified 2026-04-29);
             scraping is fragile and may require browser automation.
           * No bulk download -- need to enumerate candidate IDs and
             fetch per-candidate reports as PDF/HTML.
           * Donor names + amounts are the primary fields; amount
             precision varies by report type.
Effort:    Multi-session; deferred until the simpler bulk sources
           (v2-v4) are in.

----------------------------------------
v6: GLEIF entity backbone (LEI database)
----------------------------------------
Source:    https://www.gleif.org/en/lei-data/gleif-concatenated-file
Why:       Global entity backbone (Legal Entity Identifier). Resolves
           "Goldman Sachs" vs "GS Group" vs "The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc."
           to one canonical entity. Joins everything to a global ID.
Format:    Daily bulk (~150 MB compressed XML/CSV).
Effort:    ~1.5 sessions.

----------------------------------------
v7: USAspending federal contract / grant awards
----------------------------------------
Source:    https://api.usaspending.gov/  (REST; free, no key)
           Bulk:  https://files.usaspending.gov/database_download/
Why:       Where federal money lands -- contracts, grants, loans, IDV
           Joins to FEC (the donor side) for full federal money-flow
           graph: who funds whom, who wins what, who's debarred.
Format:    REST + bulk parquet; bulk is multi-GB per fiscal year.
Effort:    ~2-3 sessions (large dataset; needs careful chunking).


TIER 5 CHECKPOINT v1 (2026-04-29 PM, fifteenth pass) -- BUNDLED WEB UI
================================================================================
The platform now has a consumer surface beyond the JSON API. A single-page
web UI ships inside the `serving` package and is served by the existing
FastAPI app at /. Three views, all driven by the existing read endpoints:

  Trend    -> /pums-burden-county-series  (NEW endpoint, multi-year)
  Compare  -> /pums-burden-county-series  (year-filtered, ranked across
                                           all 21 counties)
  Segments -> /pums-burden-county/{fips}  (existing endpoint, dim filter)

Architectural choices made today
---------------------------------
* ZERO BUILD STEP. No npm, no webpack, no React tooling. Three static
  files (index.html, app.js, styles.css) live in serving/static/ and are
  shipped as package data. Plotly.js comes off CDN (cdn.plot.ly). The
  "build" is `git pull`.

* UI READS THE SAME ENDPOINTS AS ANY API CONSUMER. There is no
  bespoke server-side rendering, no template engine, no second
  serialization layer. If the API breaks, the UI breaks at the same
  contract boundary; if the API stays right, the UI stays right.
  This is the property that lets us treat the API as the only
  product surface to test.

* TWO NEW ENDPOINTS, not refactored existing ones.
    GET /pums-burden-county-series  -- All-years overall-segment time
      series; the read surface that makes "Trend" and "Compare" cheap.
      Filters: ?tenure, ?county_fips, ?product, ?include_suppressed.
    GET /counties                   -- ref.county dropdown source for
      the UI; pulled live so the seed is the only source of truth.

* /pums-burden-county-series PURPOSEFULLY DOES NOT WRAP THE YOY VIEW.
  The yoy view drops the earliest year (no prior_year). The trend
  chart wants every year; client-side delta math is trivial. We
  reserve the YoY view for the future variance-corrected
  derived.pums_burden_yoy table (BBG-LIKE-A in next-up).

What's new today
----------------
* serving/queries.py        +list_pums_burden_county_series, +list_counties
* serving/models.py         +PumsBurdenCountySeriesRow, +CountyRef
* serving/routes/...        +pums_burden_county_series.py, +counties.py
* serving/app.py            +StaticFiles mount at /static, GET / -> index.html
* serving/static/           +index.html, +app.js, +styles.css
* tests/test_serving.py     +9 new tests (counties, series, static UI)
* pyproject.toml            +[tool.setuptools.package-data] for wheel inclusion

Quality gates
-------------
303 tests passing (was 286), ruff clean, mypy clean (now 15 source
files in serving/), 12 v0 endpoints registered, /docs renders Swagger
correctly, /static/app.js + /static/styles.css served with right MIME.
End-to-end smoke: GET / -> 200 text/html with the bundled index;
GET /counties -> 21 NJ counties; the UI boots, populates dropdowns,
and gracefully reports empty data when the derived table is not
materialized in the target DB (instead of rendering broken charts).

Tier 5 progress: 20% -> 40%.

How to use it
-------------
  export PG_DSN=postgresql://postgres:ci@localhost:5432/nj
  nj-serve            # uvicorn on :8000
  open http://localhost:8000           # bundled UI
  open http://localhost:8000/docs      # Swagger

The UI works against any DB with the platform schema. Empty data
renders friendly status messages rather than broken chart frames;
once you re-materialize derived.pums_burden_county_segmented all
three views light up.
