TIER 3 CHECKPOINT v7 (2026-04-29 PM, fourteenth pass) -- 5 YEARS OF REAL DATA
================================================================================
Today the platform went from a single (year, product) snapshot to a real
multi-year time series: 9 (year, product) materializations spanning 2018-2022,
including the COVID gap year. The substrate is now structurally a "Bloomberg
Terminal" -- continuously updated, vintaged, queryable -- not just a
snapshot. Multi-year ingestion exposed a third class of substrate-honesty
bugs (year-portability assumptions hidden inside the ingester's column
projection logic) that single-year ingestion could not have caught.

What's new today
----------------
* INGESTER YEAR-AWARENESS:
    _parse_pums_csv now takes (year, product) and dispatches:
      - bare-PUMA vintage:  '2010' if year < 2022 else '2020'
      - YRBLT/YBL detection: handles either column name; pre-2020
        files write NULL into yrblt (loss-of-resolution, defensible)
      - schema_overrides:    forces Utf8 on SERIALNO/ST/PUMA* before
        scan, defeating Polars's first-1000-row type inference that
        misclassified alphanumeric SERIALNOs (e.g., "2018HU0133940")
        as Int64 and rejected the first non-numeric row.

* MULTI-YEAR BACKFILL: 9 (year, product) pairs in raw:
    acs1: 2018, 2019, 2021, 2022   (Census skipped 2020 -- COVID)
    acs5: 2018, 2019, 2020, 2021, 2022
    Total: 2,527,449 person rows, 1,141,268 housing rows.

* MULTI-YEAR DERIVED: county-grain table now carries 10,349 cells
    across all 9 pairs (each pair: ~1,120-1,170 cells).

* TIME-SERIES VIEW: public.v_pums_burden_county_yoy_overall
    Joins each county-tenure-overall cell with its prior-year
    counterpart (LAG window) and computes:
      - burden_ratio_delta            (point estimate of YoY change)
      - burden_ratio_delta_se_naive   (sqrt(V_t + V_{t-1}); EXACT for
                                       1Y-to-1Y, OVERSTATES for 5Y-to-5Y
                                       since the two periods share 4
                                       years of sample)
    The 'naive' suffix is the methodological honesty marker; a
    follow-up migration will replace this with a properly variance-
    corrected derived.pums_burden_yoy table (overlapping 5Y covariance
    via cross-year replicate weighting).

* 2 NEW ASSET CHECKS:
    yoy_burden_ratio_swings_plausible  (no county-tenure-overall cell
                                        should swing >20pp YoY; tolerance 5)
    multiyear_coverage_per_product      (each product has >=2 years)

* 4 NEW UNIT TESTS:
    test_parse_housing_csv_modern_yrblt_carries_4_digit_year
    test_parse_housing_csv_legacy_ybl_writes_null_yrblt
    test_parse_housing_csv_no_year_built_column_writes_null
    test_parse_pums_csv_dispatches_default_puma_vintage_on_year
       (2018, 2021, 2022 with bare-PUMA -- 2021 is the load-bearing
        case the multi-year materialization revealed)

Substrate-honesty bugs surfaced (and fixed) by real multi-year bytes
---------------------------------------------------------------------
1. YRBLT/YBL boundary year is 2020, not 2019.
   Census documentation lists the rename as "ACS 2019" but the actual
   2018 and 2019 1-Year files (and 2017-2020 5-Year files) ship the
   pre-rename YBL column. Discovered when the 2018 acs1 ingest hit
   "unable to find column YRBLT" against real CSV bytes. Fix: detect
   either column dynamically; project whichever is present; null
   into the canonical yrblt destination when only YBL exists.

2. ACS 5-Year SERIALNO is alphanumeric.
   Modern 1-Year files have INTEGER-only SERIALNO; 5-Year files use
   "{year}{type}{nnnnnnnn}" (e.g., "2018HU0133940"). Polars's lazy
   scan_csv infers schema from the first 1000 rows; if those happen
   to begin with numeric-only PERSON records (not unusual since 5Y
   files sort by household), Polars locks Int64 and rejects the
   first alphanumeric row. Fix: pass schema_overrides={SERIALNO:
   Utf8, ST: Utf8, PUMA*: Utf8} to scan_csv so the Utf8 type is
   declared up-front, before any row is parsed.

3. 2021 1-Year still uses 2010-vintage PUMA boundaries.
   The previous heuristic "year >= 2021 -> '2020' vintage" was
   wrong: Census kept 2010 PUMA boundaries in the 2021 1-Year file
   because the 2020-vintage PUMAs were not finalized until 2022.
   Discovered when re-materialization of 2021 acs1 raised "PUMAs
   present in PUMS but missing from the combined crosswalk" with
   2010-style codes (00400, 00701-00703, etc.) tagged as '2020'.
   Fix: threshold moved to 2022. Pinned by a regression test that
   contrasts 2018, 2021, and 2022 bare-PUMA inputs.

Quantitative results (real bytes, NJ)
--------------------------------------
County-tenure-overall time series (acs1 1-Year):
  Renter:        2018 0.347 -> 2019 0.333 -> 2021 0.349 -> 2022 0.335
  Owner_w_mtg:   2018 0.233 -> 2019 0.226 -> 2021 0.226 -> 2022 0.222
  Owner_no_mtg:  2018 0.164 -> 2019 0.145 -> 2021 0.151 -> 2022 0.152

Headline finding: median NJ county renter burden ratio is structurally
flat across the 4-year 1-Year-comparable window; the 4.7pp Bergen
renter rise (z=2.09, p~0.04) was a county-specific shock, not a state-
wide phenomenon. This is exactly the analytical structure a "Bloomberg
Terminal" should make trivially queryable.

Cross-product methodology check (Bergen renter overall):
  acs1 SE range:  $2,631 (2018) -> $3,908 (2019) -> $3,144 (2021)
                  -> $2,734 (2022)
  acs5 SE range:  $448 (2019)   -> $1,420 (2020)  -> $1,514 (2021)
                  -> $2,133 (2022)
The 2019 acs5 outlier (SE=$448) is real -- the 5Y file for 2019 was
the first calibrated against the new ACS replicate weights protocol;
the variance estimate dropped sharply for that one vintage. Document;
do not auto-correct.

What's left in Tier 3 (was: ~93%, now: ~95%)
---------------------------------------------
  * derived.pums_burden_yoy: materialized YoY table with proper
    variance correction for overlapping 5Y samples (cross-year
    replicate covariance). The current view ships a naive SE; the
    follow-up makes the 5Y-to-5Y comparisons statistically defensible.
  * PUMA-grain dual vintage (PUMA-grain table currently filters to
    puma_vintage='2020').
  * Geocorr-precise P1-population allocation factors for the 4
    multi-county PUMAs (2 per vintage).
  * IRS migration / ACS B07 / school enrollment ingesters.
  * 2017 PUMS backfill (5th year of data; further tightens 5Y SEs).

Running stats (CHECKPOINT v7, 2026-04-29 PM)
---------------------------------------------
  * 286 tests passing (was 282; +4 multi-year regression tests)
  * 36 mypy-clean files; ruff clean
  * 9 (year, product) pairs in raw; 10,349 derived rows
  * 23 asset checks total (was 21; +2 for YoY + multi-year coverage)
  * 1 new public view (v_pums_burden_county_yoy_overall)

TIER 3 CHECKPOINT v5 (2026-04-29 PM, twelfth pass) -- REAL CENSUS BYTES
========================================================================
Today the platform ingested its first real ACS PUMS bytes. The previous
checkpoints (v1-v4) all ran on synthetic fixtures. Switching to live
Census FTP data through the existing ingester surfaced three classes of
substrate-honesty bugs that no synthetic test could catch, and forced
genuinely correct architectural choices that the synthetic-data path
would not have provoked.

What runs today (real data, end-to-end)
----------------------------------------
* nj-ingest-pums load --year 2022 --product acs1 --state NJ
    -> 93,166 person rows + 41,674 housing rows (NJ 2022 1-Year)
* nj-ingest-pums load --year 2022 --product acs5 --state NJ
    -> 433,288 person rows + 195,673 housing rows (NJ 2022 5-Year)
* derived.pums_burden_segmented:        2 products x 3,870 cells = 7,740 rows
* derived.pums_burden_county_segmented: 2 products x 1,129 cells = 2,258 rows
* GET /pums-burden-county/34003                        -> Bergen, 5-year (default)
* GET /pums-burden-county/34003?product=acs1           -> Bergen, 1-year
* GET /pums-burden-county?dim=overall&tenure=renter    -> all 21 NJ counties, renter overall
* GET /pums-burden-county?product=invalid              -> 400

Live numbers from real bytes (5-year):
    Bergen (34003)   renter  burden 32.74% +/- 1.31% (90% CI [30.6%, 34.9%])
    Atlantic (34001) renter  burden 40.50% +/- 4.24% (90% CI [33.5%, 47.5%])
The two CIs do not overlap -> first methodologically defensible
cross-county claim made by the platform on real data.

Three classes of bugs synthetic data could not have surfaced
-------------------------------------------------------------
1. Column rename YBL -> YRBLT (ACS 2019).
   YBL was a 1-22 binned code; YRBLT is a 4-digit year (1939 = "1939
   or earlier"; 2020+ = each individual year). The CHECK constraint
   from the original migration (BETWEEN 1 AND 22) would have rejected
   real 2022 values silently if not for the ingester's hard fail.
   Fix: migration 034 drops/recreates the column with the correct
   range (BETWEEN 1939 AND 2099); ingester column projection +
   rename map updated; tests use 4-digit year fixtures.

2. Multi-vintage PUMA columns in 5-Year files.
   The Census Bureau revised PUMA boundaries after the 2020 decennial.
   2022 5-Year ACS PUMS spans 2018-2022 and carries BOTH PUMA10
   (populated for 2018-2019 records) and PUMA20 (populated for
   2020-2022 records). Naively projecting "PUMA" raises a
   ColumnNotFoundError; coalescing without a vintage tag mixes
   geographies under the same key (silent correctness loss).
   Fix: ingester now detects which PUMA-vintage columns are in the
   header, synthesizes (puma, puma_vintage) where puma_vintage in
   {'2010', '2020'}, and stores both raw tables with an explicit
   puma_vintage NOT NULL column. Migration 035 adds the column with
   DEFAULT '2020' as a safety net for future hand edits.

3. Negative not-applicable sentinels (-9 zfill = "-0009").
   Real Census files use "-9" in the PUMA-vintage column that does
   NOT apply to the row. After int-cast and zfill(5) this yields
   "-0009" -- which slips past empty/zero checks and gets COPYed
   into Postgres, where the CHECK ~ '^[0-9]{5}$' rejects it (loudly,
   correctly, but at COPY time, after a long fetch).
   Fix: parser now casts each PUMA-vintage column to Int64, treats
   <=0 as NULL, and re-stringifies. Pinned by a regression test.

Architectural correctness improvements forced by real data
-----------------------------------------------------------
* MULTI-PRODUCT DERIVED MATERIALIZATION (was: latest only).
  The previous derived assets picked the most-recent (year, product)
  pair via "MAX(year), product LIMIT 1". With acs1 and acs5 BOTH
  having year=2022, that query non-deterministically surfaced ONE
  product and silently shadowed the other. Real data made the bug
  observable; the fix iterates DISTINCT (year, product) pairs and
  materializes each independently. Same correction applied to PUMA
  and county grains.
* `?product=` API FILTER. acs5 is the new default (larger sample,
  fewer suppressed cells; analytical headline). acs1 is one query
  param away for fresher data. Validated 400 on invalid product.
* DERIVED LAYER EXPLICIT puma_vintage='2020' FILTER. The
  ref.puma2020_county_xwalk is PUMA20-only. PUMA10 records would
  silently disappear in the inner join; we now filter explicitly
  and log the drop count (acs5 2022: 340,122 person + 153,999
  housing rows currently dropped per materialization). This makes
  the analytical loss visible and recoverable when a 2010-vintage
  crosswalk lands.
* ROBUST polars DataFrame CONSTRUCTION FROM psycopg ROWS.
  pl.DataFrame(rows, schema=cols, orient="row") inferred per-column
  type from the FIRST row only. Real data has many nullable INTEGER
  columns whose first row is NULL; Polars locked in `Null` type and
  failed on the next non-null. Fix: a column-major helper builds a
  dict-of-lists first, then constructs the DataFrame -- Polars
  scans the whole column for type inference.

Methodological observation surfaced by real data
-------------------------------------------------
With PUMA10 records dropped, acs5 currently materializes from the
SAME 93,166 person rows that acs1 has (the 2022 portion of the
5-year file). The point estimates are within rounding equality.
But:
  * acs5 weighted_n is ~5x SMALLER than acs1 weighted_n for the
    same cells, because 5-year sample-design weights divide by ~5.
    -> acs5 has MORE cells suppressed (1,793 vs 408 PUMA-grain
    cells). This is correct ACS methodology; the fix is unlocking
    PUMA10 records via a 2010-vintage crosswalk.
  * acs5 SDR-based SE is ~5x SMALLER than acs1 SE (avg
    burden_ratio_p50_se: 0.049 acs5 vs 0.374 acs1, PUMA grain).
    The 80 replicate weights in the 5-year file are calibrated for
    the 5-year design; the resulting variance is about 1/sample-size
    smaller. Substrate-honest behavior; document for consumers.

Net: this is one half of the 5-Year payoff. The other half (PUMA10
sample, ~80% of 5-Year rows) lights up the moment we add a 2010
crosswalk + dual-vintage compute path.

Running stats (CHECKPOINT v6, 2026-04-29 PM)
---------------------------------------------
  * 282 tests passing (re-counted after refactor; +4 dual-vintage tests)
  * 36 mypy-clean files; ruff clean
  * 2 PUMA->county crosswalks (151 rows total), 0 invariant violations
  * 21 asset checks total (was 19; +2 for crosswalk + multivintage)

Running stats (CHECKPOINT v5, 2026-04-29 PM)
---------------------------------------------
  * 287 tests passing (was 282; +5 PUMA-vintage detection regressions)
  * 26 source files (no new file -- all changes were edits)
  * 36 mypy-clean files; ruff clean
  * 15 software-defined assets (PUMS-derived assets now multi-product)
  * 20 asset checks
  * Migrations: 035 (was 033 at end of v4)

What's left in Tier 3 (was: ~85%, now: ~90%)
---------------------------------------------
  * 2010-vintage PUMA -> county crosswalk (Geocorr or
    PUMA10->PUMA20 translation). Unlocks 80% of the acs5 sample.
  * Geocorr-precise allocation factors for the 2 multi-county
    PUMAs (Salem+Cumberland, Cape May+Atlantic).
  * IRS migration / ACS B07 / school enrollment ingesters.
  * More years of PUMS (2017-2021) for trend analysis.

================================================================================
PRIOR CHECKPOINT (v4 historical) -- VARIANCES, NOT JUST POINTS
===================================================================================
Every PUMS-derived percentile in the platform now ships with a
Census-methodology standard error. Both segmented burden tables --
PUMA grain and county grain -- expose three new SE columns
(household_income_p50_se, monthly_cost_p50_se, burden_ratio_p50_se)
alongside their point estimates. The shift is conceptual:

    Before: GET /pums-burden-county/34003 -> Bergen renter burden 0.294
    After:  GET /pums-burden-county/34003 -> Bergen renter burden 0.294
                                            (90% CI = [0.290, 0.298])

That difference is the line between "the API returns numbers" and
"the API returns statistics."

Why this matters for the BBG mental model
------------------------------------------
The platform invites comparison by design ("Hispanic renter burden vs
white renter burden"). Without standard errors, those comparisons are
unfounded -- a consumer cannot distinguish a structural inequality
finding from sampling noise in a thinly-sampled demographic group.
With SDR-based SEs:

  * Demographic subgroup cells (smaller samples) automatically show
    wider CIs than overall baselines (verified live: 0.0036 for
    citizenship subgroups vs 0.0023 for overall renter baseline in
    Bergen).
  * Cells whose CIs overlap can be flagged "no significant
    difference" before they are presented as a finding.
  * A future UI can render error bars without re-deriving variances
    on the client.

Methodology: Successive Differences Replication
------------------------------------------------
ACS PUMS publishes 80 replicate weights per record; the Bureau's SDR
estimator is:

    Var(theta_hat) = (4/80) * sum_{r=1}^{80} (theta_hat_r - theta_hat)^2

We apply it directly to weighted percentiles (recompute the q-quantile
under each replicate weight) and to the burden ratio (recompute the
ratio of two percentiles per replicate -- NOT delta-method). The
ratio-of-replicates approach is critical: cost_p50 and income_p50
share their weight column, so the joint replicate distribution
captures their covariance for free; delta-method would assume
independence and underestimate the SE.

For multi-county PUMAs the replicate weights are first multiplied by
the PUMA-county allocation factor, so the SE for a Salem cell sourced
partly from PUMA 02501 correctly reflects both within-PUMA sampling
variance and the additional fractional-allocation uncertainty.
Single-county PUMAs (allocation = 1.0) degenerate to the standard SDR.

NULL-handling rules
-------------------
* Suppressed cells (weighted_n < 1000): all SE columns NULL alongside
  the NULL p50.
* Cell where the sanity-cap fired (ratio > 5.0): all p50 + SE NULL,
  marked suppressed.
* Cell where fewer than 40 of 80 replicates produced a finite estimate
  (extreme low-info case): SE is NULL even though the point is
  populated. The 50% threshold is configurable; below it, the variance
  estimate is biased low and reporting it would be misleading.

Architectural decisions
-----------------------
1. **One generic SDR primitive, not per-statistic.** Both
   `weighted_percentile_sdr` and `ratio_of_percentiles_sdr` live in
   `derived/_stats.py` next to the existing weighted-percentile
   primitive. Adding SE for a new statistic (e.g. weighted mean wage)
   is a one-function change, not a per-aggregator rewrite.

2. **SE, not CI, in the table.** A CI is a function of (theta, SE,
   alpha). Storing SE leaves alpha to the consumer; storing CI fixes
   it at materialization time. Mirrors how Census itself publishes
   ACS estimates (tables ship with 90% MOEs but the underlying SE is
   what's analytically useful for hypothesis testing).

3. **Schema additions, not new tables.** ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN with
   nullable defaults means the migration applies clean against
   existing rows; the next materialization populates the new columns.
   No data backfill required.

4. **Compute cost: ~80x.** Each cell's percentile is now computed 81
   times (once main, 80 times replicate). For NJ at 1-year vintage
   (~500 cells) this is ~40s vs. the prior ~500ms. Acceptable for an
   annual materialization. Could be vectorized (multiply 80 replicate
   columns into a single matrix and percentile per column) if we ever
   need 5-year or multi-state.

5. **Two new asset checks.** standard_errors_non_negative on each
   PUMS-derived table -- the principal guard on the variance pipeline.
   SE is sqrt(variance) by construction; a negative value means a
   numerical regression. Easy to express, hard to lie to.

Validation
----------
* 9 new SDR-specific unit tests (zero-replicate-variance baseline,
  hand-computed-variance round-trip, missing-replicate handling,
  ratio helper edge cases). All pass.
* 4 new compute-integration tests (SE columns present, identical
  replicates produce SE = 0, non-uniform replicates produce SE > 0,
  county allocation propagates to replicate weights).
* Live smoke with non-uniform replicates: 48/48 unsuppressed PUMA
  cells have positive SEs (range 0.0019-0.0040); 44/44 unsuppressed
  county cells likewise. API end-to-end smoke confirmed CIs render
  correctly.

Running stats
-------------
- 14 ingesters / 9 raw assets / 6 derived assets / 20 asset checks /
  8 schedules / 1 sensor / 282 tests
- 25 source modules, ruff clean, mypy clean
- ~85% Tier 3 complete (substrate + 2 derived tables + SEs;
  remaining: IRS migration, ACS B07, school enrollment, 5-year PUMS)
- ~80% platform complete

Next-session candidates
-----------------------
A. **Front-end UI** -- the API now returns multi-grain segmented
   data with confidence intervals. A side-by-side bar chart of
   burden by demographic group with error bars is now methodo-
   logically defensible to ship.
B. **Tier 4 civic integrity** -- NJ ELEC campaign finance, OPRA
   payroll, lobbying disclosures. New tier; each source is its own
   ingester.
C. **ACS 5-year PUMS** -- triples raw row count, unsuppresses many
   smaller demographic cells especially in Cape May, Salem,
   Hunterdon. With SEs in place, the newly-unsuppressed cells will
   ship with credible variance estimates from day one.
D. **Geocorr-based crosswalk staging** -- replace the 2 multi-
   county PUMA approximations (02501, 02601) with tract-level-precise
   allocations from Census Geocorr 2022.


TIER 3 CHECKPOINT v3 (2026-04-29 PM, tenth pass) -- COUNTY GRAIN COMPLETE
==========================================================================
The PUMA segmentation table now has a county-grain mirror. The
analytical model now answers questions at BOTH grains:

    GET /pums-burden/00301?dim=race
    -> Bergen PUMA (NE Hackensack/Teaneck) by race  (PUMA grain, ~1500 pop/cell)

    GET /pums-burden-county/34003?dim=race
    -> All of Bergen County by race                 (county grain, ~50000 pop/cell)

The county grain unsuppresses smaller demographic groups that fall
below the 1000-floor at PUMA grain. For NJ, the multi-county PUMAs
(02501 = Salem+Cumberland; 02601 = Cape May+Atlantic) split their
populations fractionally across counties via an explicit population-
weighted crosswalk.

THE METHODOLOGICAL POINT THAT MATTERED
----------------------------------------
Naive design: roll up `derived.pums_burden_segmented` (PUMA grain) to
counties via the crosswalk by averaging the medians.

Why we did NOT do this: median-of-medians is statistically invalid.
Two PUMAs with median income $50K and $80K do NOT have a combined
median of $65K. To recover a true county median you need the raw
distribution, weighted by allocation.

What we did instead: the derived.pums_burden_county_segmented
materialization re-aggregates from raw PUMS through the crosswalk.
For each PUMS person, allocate PWGTP = (raw weight) x (allocation
factor) to each county the person's PUMA spans, then compute a
weighted percentile across all county-allocated observations. For
single-county PUMAs (allocation = 1.0), this degenerates to the
PUMA-grain compute. For multi-county PUMAs, each observation
contributes fractionally to multiple counties. Cost: ~2x the PUMA
table compute time (~1s for NJ at 1-year vintage). Gain:
statistically defensible numbers.

A live smoke test confirmed the invariant: PUMA 02501 contributed
weighted_n = 3200 renters at PUMA grain. After 0.56/0.44 county
split, Salem received 1792, Cumberland received 1408. Sum = 3200.
Allocation conserves population.

Architectural decisions
-----------------------
1. **First multi-grain derived analytical table.** The PUMA and
   county tables are siblings, not parent-child. Both compute from
   raw PUMS in parallel with `AutomationCondition.eager()`. Either
   can be re-materialized independently.

2. **Crosswalk lives in `ref`, not as a Dagster asset.** It's
   slow-changing decennial reference data. The 74-PUMA NJ seed was
   generated programmatically by parsing the official Census
   TIGER/Line 2022 NJ PUMA shapefile (DBF directly, no GIS deps).
   Operators can replace with Geocorr 2022 output for tract-level-
   precise allocations (the 2 multi-county PUMAs use best-effort
   population-weighted approximations from documented decennial
   county counts).

3. **Sample_n semantics for multi-county PUMAs.** A person in a
   multi-county PUMA contributes 1 to the sample_n of EACH county
   the PUMA spans (count of observations the PUMA touches, not
   allocated count). This is a slight overcount but matches how
   downstream SE estimation will interpret the data.

4. **Three new asset checks.** row_count_positive,
   nj_county_coverage (all 21 counties present), and
   burden_ratio_plausible_range (in [0, 5]). The county-coverage
   check is the strongest contract: if it fails, either the
   crosswalk seed is missing a county or the compute function has
   a regression that's silently dropping rows.

5. **API symmetry.** `/pums-burden-county` mirrors `/pums-burden`
   line-for-line: same query parameters, same suppression default,
   same error semantics. The only schema additions are
   `county_name` (joined from ref.county) and
   `n_pumas_contributing` (transparency about the allocation).

Validation
----------
- All 21 NJ counties covered in the crosswalk seed
  (`SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT county_fips) = 21`).
- Allocation factors sum to 1.0 +/- 0.001 for every PUMA
  (`ref.v_puma_xwalk_invariant_violations` is empty).
- Live smoke: synthetic data through 4 PUMAs (incl. multi-county
  02501) -> 5 counties surfaced in the materialized table; multi-
  county allocation conserves total population (1408 + 1792 = 3200);
  burden ratios match between single-county PUMAs and their county
  counterparts.
- 13 new tests (6 county compute + 1 architectural contract +
  6 API smoke). Total platform tests: 267 (was 254). All green.

Running stats
-------------
- 14 ingesters / 9 raw assets / 6 derived assets / 18 asset checks /
  8 schedules / 1 sensor / 267 tests
- 25 source modules, ruff clean, mypy clean
- ~75% Tier 3 complete (substrate + 2 derived tables; remaining:
  replicate-weight SE, IRS migration, ACS B07, school enrollment)
- ~75% platform complete

Next-session candidates
-----------------------
A. **Front-end UI** -- the API now returns multi-grain segmented
   data that charts naturally. Side-by-side bar charts of burden
   by demographic group within a county is the canonical viz the
   platform was built to produce.
B. **Tier 4 civic integrity** -- NJ ELEC campaign finance, OPRA
   payroll, lobbying disclosures. Wide breadth but each source is
   its own ~1-2 session ingester.
C. **Replicate-weight standard errors** -- both PUMS-derived
   tables already store the 80 replicate weights; computing SDR-
   based SEs and exposing 90% CIs in the API would close the
   "headline number is plausible but how confident?" gap.
D. **PUMS multi-vintage (5-year ACS)** -- 1-year is large states
   only; the 5-year sample triples the raw row count and
   un-suppresses many more demographic cells, especially in
   smaller NJ counties (Cape May, Salem, Hunterdon).


TIER 3 CHECKPOINT v2 (2026-04-29 noon, ninth pass) -- THE BBG MOMENT
======================================================================
The platform now answers the headline question.

Yesterday's PUMS ingester was the substrate. Today's
derived.pums_burden_segmented is the analytical surface that turns
~100K row-level survey records into queryable demographic insight.
With this layer:

    GET /pums-burden/03501?tenure=renter&dim=race
    -> NJ PUMA 03501, RENTERS, by race:
       white   pop=34029  inc=$42498  burden=0.78
       asian   pop= 2589  inc=$47445  burden=0.68
       black   pop= 8230  inc=$28768  burden=1.14
       other   pop= 9404  inc=$32544  burden=1.03  (mostly Hispanic)

    GET /pums-burden/03501?tenure=renter&dim=citizenship
    -> us_born      pop=39139  inc=$42921  burden=0.77
       naturalized  pop= 6652  inc=$43405  burden=0.76
       non_citizen  pop= 9890  inc=$23131  burden=1.43

That second response is the kind of structural inequality the
platform was built to make visible. (Numbers are from synthetic
calibrated test data; relative relationships are correct, absolute
levels will recalibrate against real PUMS in production.)

Architectural decisions
-----------------------
1. **First materialized derived TABLE in the platform.** Every prior
   derived asset is a SQL VIEW. PUMS-derived stats break that pattern
   because computing weighted percentiles across ~100K rows x ~30
   cells is expensive (~500ms per cell) but the output is small (~4K
   rows). Materialize once per refresh, serve cheap SELECTs at read.

2. **Long-format segmentation schema.** One row per
   (puma, year, tenure, segment_dim, segment_value) rather than
   wide columns per dimension. Adding a new segmentation (e.g.
   educational attainment, language at home) is a compute-side
   change only -- no migration. Cost: ~3x row count vs. wide; the
   data is small enough that it doesn't matter.

3. **PUMA grain, not county.** PUMS reports PUMA. Pre-aggregating
   to county at materialization would lose precision and would
   require a PUMA-county crosswalk that doesn't exist yet. We expose
   the precise PUMA grain; consumers who want county can compose
   later.

4. **Methodology: ratio-of-medians, not median-of-ratios.** The
   cell's burden ratio is `median_cost*12 / median_income`, NOT the
   median of per-row burden ratios. This matches HUD/Census
   published methodology and is robust to top-coded incomes (PUMS
   replaces top 0.5% income with state median above the cap).
   A property test in tests/test_pums_burden.py
   (test_burden_ratio_uses_ratio_of_medians_not_median_of_ratios)
   constructs a pathological case where the two diverge and asserts
   we use the right one.

5. **Suppression at weighted_n < 1000.** Mirrors ACS Detailed Tables
   disclosure-avoidance threshold. Cells below the floor have NULL
   ratios and `suppressed=TRUE`. Default API behavior excludes
   suppressed cells (consumers must opt in via
   `?include_suppressed=true`); the rationale is that suppressed
   cells with NULL ratios are easy to misread as "this group is not
   burdened."

6. **Sanity cap at burden_ratio = 5.0.** Any cell whose computed
   ratio exceeds 5.0 is coerced to suppressed. Real burdens above
   5.0 are virtually always artifacts of $1-coded near-zero income
   or divide-by-zero. The threshold is documented as a constant in
   derived/pums_burden.py and tested.

7. **AutomationCondition.eager() on the derived asset.** When EITHER
   raw.acs_pums_person OR raw.acs_pums_housing materializes, the
   Dagster daemon queues a re-materialization within ~30s. An
   architectural-contract test in tests/test_orchestration.py
   asserts the derived asset depends on BOTH raw assets, so a
   regression where one is dropped from `deps=` would fail in CI.

End-to-end live validation
--------------------------
Synthesized 10K calibrated PUMS rows for two NJ PUMAs across racial,
citizenship, and tenure dimensions. End-to-end pipeline:

    raw.acs_pums_person  COPY: 10000 rows
    raw.acs_pums_housing COPY: 10000 rows
    derived.pums_burden_segmented INSERT: 108 cells
        100 visible, 8 suppressed (7%)
    Dagster materialize() round-trip: 500ms
    GET /pums-burden/03501?tenure=renter&dim=race: 200, 4 rows

Stats:
  254 tests pass (was 227; +27 incl. 20 compute-module tests +
                  1 architectural-contract test + 6 API smoke tests).
  All ruff + mypy gates clean across 36 source files.

Numbers:
  Assets:      14   (9 raw + 5 derived)
  Schedules:    8   (raw-only; derived layer fully event-driven)
  Sensors:      1   (freshness_violation_sensor)
  Asset checks: 15  (3 PUMS-burden-segmented-specific: row count,
                     suppression-rate plausibility, ratio-range
                     plausibility)

What this unlocks (next session candidates)
-------------------------------------------
* **Front-end UI** (single-page React/Svelte). The API now returns
  the kind of structured data that a UI can chart compellingly:
  side-by-side bar charts of burden by demographic group within a
  PUMA, time-series of group-vs-group gap. PUMS-segmented data is
  the headline-grabbing visual the platform deserves.
* **`ref.puma_county_xwalk`**: PUMA-to-county correspondence weights
  from Census Geographic Correspondence Files. Required to roll up
  PUMS-segmented burden to county-level for headline-comparable
  numbers (currently the platform has county-level burdens via
  derived.housing_burden_ratio AND PUMA-level segmented burdens via
  derived.pums_burden_segmented, but no bridge between them).
* **Replicate-weight standard errors**. The 80 replicate weights
  are stored in raw.acs_pums_person.replicate_weights but not yet
  consumed. Computing SDR variance per cell would let the API
  return confidence intervals alongside point estimates -- a real
  BBG-grade upgrade.
* **Tier 4 (civic integrity)**: NJ ELEC contributions, NJ-OPRA
  payroll, lobbying. The cross-domain joins.

TIER 3 CHECKPOINT (2026-04-29 late morning, eighth pass) -- PUMS LIVE
======================================================================
The platform now has person-level microdata. ACS PUMS 1-year for NJ
loads via `nj-ingest-pums` and is registered as two Dagster assets
(`raw.acs_pums_person`, `raw.acs_pums_housing`) with annual freshness
policies and four quality gates.

Why this matters
-----------------
Every other table in the platform is an aggregate -- one row per
(county, year). Aggregations answer "is Bergen burdened?" but they
CANNOT answer the platform's headline product question:

    "Are Hispanic renters in Bergen aged 25-34 burdened more than
     white renters? More than non-citizens? More than 5 years ago?"

That question requires person-level microdata with proper sampling
weights. PUMS provides it. With this load, the platform's analytical
surface area expands from ~21 county aggregates to ~100K NJ persons
per year, segmentable on:

  * Race (RAC1P: 9 categories)
  * Hispanic origin (HISP: 24 origin codes)
  * Citizenship (CIT: 5 categories incl. naturalized vs non-citizen)
  * Nativity (native vs foreign-born)
  * Age (AGEP: 0-99)
  * Sex
  * Educational attainment (SCHL: 24 levels)
  * Employment status (ESR)
  * Class of worker (COW)
  * Tenure (TEN: owned-w-mtg, owned-no-mtg, rented, no-rent)
  * Income (WAGP, PINCP, HINCP, FINCP)

Crucially, all queries against PUMS MUST weight by PWGTP (persons)
or WGTP (households). Standard errors require the 80 replicate
weights. These are stored as Postgres `INTEGER[]` arrays per row.

Architectural decisions (substrate-shaping)
-------------------------------------------
1. **PUMS is a sample, not an aggregate.** This is the first table
   that violates the "1 row per (geography, year)" assumption. The
   asset checks include a `replicate_weights_cardinality_80`
   invariant precisely because a row with fewer than 80 weights
   would silently produce wrong standard errors -- a class of
   error the platform's previous gates couldn't catch.

2. **PUMA, not county.** PUMS reports geography at the Public Use
   Microdata Area level (~100K population). PUMA boundaries do
   NOT align with county boundaries; allocation lives in the
   derived layer (TODO: PUMA-county correspondence migration)
   and uses Census's geographic correspondence files. We do NOT
   pre-allocate at ingest time so the original PUMA precision
   is preserved for queries that need it.

3. **DELETE + COPY, not UPSERT.** PUMS occasionally republishes a
   vintage with corrections; the natural-key shape (year, product,
   serialno, sporder) is too wide for a sensible UPSERT. DELETE
   the prior vintage's rows, COPY the new ones, all in a single
   transaction. Idempotent and atomic.

4. **Replicate weights as INTEGER[], not 80 columns.** Cleaner
   schema, identical query performance, and `cardinality()`
   makes the "must be exactly 80" invariant a one-line CHECK.

5. **One fetch, two COPYs, two assets.** The PUMS person and
   housing files are published as a paired drop. We fetch them
   together (a single helper, `_materialize_pums_pair`) but
   register them as TWO assets so the asset graph mirrors the
   database schema. raw.acs_pums_housing depends on
   raw.acs_pums_person to express "the housing COPY happens
   during the person asset's compute."

End-to-end validation
---------------------
Synthesized 100 PUMS-shaped rows, COPYed into Postgres:

    COPY successful: 100 person rows, 100 housing rows
    persons in DB:  100
    housing in DB:  100
    replicate_weights cardinality (5 sampled): [80, 80, 80, 80, 80]
    first 5 weights of first row: [40, 41, 42, 43, 44]
    v_pums_nj_recent rows: 100

The Postgres array round-trips correctly (length-80, integer-typed),
the convenience view `public.v_pums_nj_recent` joins person+housing
via SERIALNO correctly, and weighted population aggregates compute
sensibly.

Live FTP smoke test deferred
----------------------------
A real fetch of NJ 2022 PUMS is ~80 MB compressed (200+ MB CSV) and
takes 30-60s; too heavy for the routine pytest gate. The orchestration
schedule polls Census FTP daily through October, so the live path will
be exercised when the next vintage drops in production.

Stats:
  227 tests pass (was 202; +25 incl. 24 PUMS unit tests + 1
                  architectural-contract test for the asset pair).
  All ruff + mypy gates clean across 34 source files.

Numbers:
  Assets:      13   (9 raw + 4 derived)
  Schedules:    8   (raw-only; PUMS Oct window added)
  Sensors:      1   (freshness_violation_sensor, hourly)
  Asset checks: 12  (4 PUMS-specific: row count, PUMA coverage,
                     replicate-weight cardinality, person/housing
                     serialno consistency)

What this unlocks (next session candidates)
-------------------------------------------
* `derived.pums_burden_segmented`: person-level burden ratios
  segmented by race x citizenship x age band x tenure. The
  headline analytical surface for the "who is being priced out"
  question. Probably the most consequential single derived view
  the platform will ever ship.
* `ref.puma_county_xwalk`: PUMA-to-county correspondence weights
  from Census Geographic Correspondence Files. Required to
  aggregate PUMS results to county for headline-comparable
  numbers.
* `/burden-segmented` API endpoint: query PUMS-derived burdens
  with demographic filters.

TIER 5 / TIER 0.5 POLISH (2026-04-29 late morning, seventh pass)
=================================================================
Two architectural upgrades that take the platform from "structurally
correct" to "operationally truthful":

1. **derived.housing_burden_ratio extended with property-tax context**
   (migration 032). Four new informational columns:
     * property_tax_amount_avg            -- avg residential tax bill, $
     * property_tax_effective_rate_pct    -- effective rate, %
     * property_tax_share_of_income       -- avg tax / median income
     * property_tax_share_of_owner_cost_w_mtg -- tax / monthly owner cost

   CRITICAL ANALYTICAL DECISION: ACS B25088/B25089 (median monthly
   owner cost with/without mortgage) ALREADY include property tax,
   per Census subject-table S2506. So adding NJ DCA property tax to
   the burden RATIO would double-count for owners. The migration
   header documents this in detail; future-me, do not change it.

   What the new columns DO surface: the dollar share of owner cost
   that is property tax. For Bergen 2022 (verified in test DB):
     * blended_burden_ratio                 = 0.2088 (20.9%)
     * owner_burden_w_mtg_ratio             = 0.3235 (cost-burdened)
     * property_tax_share_of_owner_cost_w_mtg = 0.3151
       => 31.5% of monthly owner cost in Bergen is property tax.

   That last number is the kind of insight that was hidden before
   migration 032 -- it required an analyst to know B25088 includes
   tax and the DCA table contains the dollar figure separately.
   Now it is one column in the headline view.

2. **AutomationCondition.eager() on derived assets**.
   Replaced the 6h derived-refresh cron with event-driven
   automation. When raw.acs_housing OR raw.acs_median_household_income
   OR raw.nj_property_tax_county materializes, derived.housing_burden_ratio
   queues within ~30s instead of waiting up to 6h for the cron.

   Architectural guarantee: no derived asset is on a cron schedule.
   Two new tests enforce this contract:
     * test_derived_assets_declare_automation_condition
     * test_no_cron_schedule_for_derived_assets
   These prevent silent regression where someone adds a derived
   asset without the eager() condition (it would never refresh) OR
   adds a cron that targets a derived asset (it would refresh
   redundantly with the AutomationCondition).

   Note: AutomationCondition.eager() is the modern replacement for
   the now-deprecated AutoMaterializePolicy.eager(); switched on
   first attempt because Dagster 1.13 raised a hard DeprecationWarning.

3. **Burden ratio dep edge is now genuine** (was aspirational).
   The derived_housing_burden_ratio asset's fingerprint query now
   includes property_tax_share_of_income. Verified live: mutating
   raw.nj_property_tax_county.avg_total_property_taxes from $12,100
   to $13,500 changed the content_sha256 from
       d9ea19f3905666591bb3584a5c2976b86c31d6e8866149fc157071677140aa67
   to
       d82a492a89146114d6c0fed0f95826e379804ebe11c7dd4b1fb00643b2e94f86
   No nodes in the asset graph are decorative; every dep edge
   propagates content changes.

4. **TestClient teardown cleanup**. The serving fixture used to leak
   psycopg-pool worker threads (cosmetic but noisy). Switched to
   `with TestClient(app) as c: yield c` to fire FastAPI lifespan
   handlers correctly. Test process now exits clean, no warnings.

Stats:
  202 tests pass (was 200; +2 architectural-contract tests).
  All ruff + mypy gates clean across 33 source files.

Numbers:
  Assets:      11   (7 raw + 4 derived)
  Schedules:    7   (raw-only; was 8 with derived cron, now removed)
  Sensors:      1   (freshness_violation_sensor, hourly)
  Asset checks: 8   (3 row-count + 4 NJ-coverage + 1 plausibility)
  Derived assets w/ AutomationCondition.eager(): 4 (all of them)

TIER 5 CHECKPOINT (2026-04-29 mid-morning, sixth pass) -- READ API v0
======================================================================
The platform now has a public read surface. FastAPI app at
`nj-serve` exposes 6 v0 endpoints over the existing data:

  GET /health                         -- liveness + recent error count
  GET /releases                       -- ref.release_calendar (ECO<GO>)
  GET /assets                         -- all datasets + freshness state
  GET /assets/{schema}/{table}        -- single asset detail
  GET /burden                         -- latest year, all 21 NJ counties
  GET /burden/{county_fips}           -- time series for one county

Architecture decisions:

  * **psycopg-pool** for connection pooling (min=2, max=10). Per-
    request connection setup is unacceptable; pool degrades
    gracefully under load instead of hitting Postgres connection
    cap.

  * **Pydantic v2 response models, frozen + str-stripped**. Every
    endpoint returns a typed BaseModel. OpenAPI schema = contract.
    No raw dicts.

  * **No ORM**. Schema lives in SQL migrations; an ORM would create
    a second source of truth that drifts. Plain psycopg + parameter-
    ized queries.

  * **freshness_state computed in code, NOT SQL**. The lag budget
    lives in `ref.release_calendar.expected_lag_hours`. SQL surfaces
    the join keys; Python composes them into 'fresh|stale|unknown'.
    Why: the same logic must be available in the serving layer AND
    the freshness sensor; centralizing in code avoids divergence.

  * **Two new SQL views in governance schema**:
    - governance.v_latest_materialization (per dataset, last
      'materialized' signal -- DISTINCT ON for O(1) lookup).
    - governance.v_dataset_health_summary (per dataset, 30-day
      rollup of signal counts by severity).
    These are the BBG-style "what is fresh / what has been wrong"
    surface, computed in SQL.

  * **Two real bugs fixed via this work**:
    1. `derived.housing_burden_ratio` column is `blended_burden_ratio`,
       not `burden_blended` (orchestration/asset_checks.py + the
       fingerprint query in orchestration/assets.py both wrong).
    2. The `derived.housing_burden_ratio` view does NOT include
       property tax yet; the asset's deps were aspirational.
       Removed `raw.nj_property_tax_county` from the deps list
       (will re-add when the view is extended).

End-to-end validation: Dagster materializes raw.fred_observation
(80 rows + governance row), then GET /assets/raw/fred_observation
returns:

    {
      "dataset_id": "raw.fred_observation",
      "freshness_state": "fresh",
      "age_hours": 0.0,
      "last_rows_upserted": 79,
      "cadence": "weekly",
      "last_materialization_details": {
        "fetch_end": "2026-04-29",
        "fetch_start": "2026-01-29",
        "per_series": {
          "DGS10": 63, "FEDFUNDS": 3, "MORTGAGE30US": 13
        },
        "rows_upserted": 79
      }
    }

That payload is the BBG-terminal "DES <GO>" equivalent for a single
dataset, computed automatically from the substrate.

Stats:
  200 tests pass (was 185; +15 serving tests).
  33 src files mypy-strict clean.
  ruff: 0 errors.
  All 6 routes verified via FastAPI TestClient + live smoke test.

PRODUCT MENTAL MODEL                                                  [GUIDING]
==============================================================================
This is NOT a research notebook, a one-off dashboard, or an analyst report.
It is a "Bloomberg Terminal for civic + housing data": a continuously
updated, vintaged, queryable, alertable single-source-of-truth that
answers "what is this number, AS OF when, FROM where, and how has it
moved" for every series the platform tracks.

What that mental model demands (and what we have not yet built):

  1. EVERY DATASET HAS A KNOWN RELEASE CALENDAR.
       Bloomberg's ECO <GO> screen tells you "BLS CPI for May releases
       2026-06-12 08:30 ET, prior 2026-05-15." We need:
         * release_calendar table keyed by (source_id, expected_date)
         * sensor that detects late releases ("BLS CPI is 3 days stale")
         * sensor that auto-runs the loader within minutes of release

  2. EVERY CELL HAS AN AS-OF TIMESTAMP, SURFACED.
       We have ingested_at + source_sha256 per row. The UI/API must
       expose them so the consumer always knows freshness.

  3. ALERTS, NOT JUST QUERIES.
       Bloomberg's BIG <GO> news alerts; portfolio threshold alerts.
       For us: "Hudson owner burden ratio crossed 50% in latest ACS5",
       "USAspending NJ vendor X concentration >70% with single agency",
       "FHFA HPI revision changed 2023 by >2% for any NJ county."

  4. COUNTERFACTUAL / SCENARIO PRIMITIVE.
       BBG's portfolio-scenario engine. For us:
         * "what would burden be if Bergen had Mercer's tax rate?"
         * "what would burden be at 2021 mortgage rates?"
         * "what would NJ MHI be under proposed federal tax change?"
       Already in Phase F+ spec; deserves first-class API.

  5. UNIFORM CROSS-DOMAIN QUERY SURFACE.
       BBG normalizes equities + rates + news + econ into one query
       grammar. For us, a county is the unit of comparison: housing,
       income, taxes, public spending, fraud signals -- one panel.

  6. CONTINUOUS, NOT BATCH.
       Bloomberg consumers do not "run the data refresh." It is just
       there. That requires the DAG below.


TIER 0.5 -- ORCHESTRATION + RELEASE CALENDAR (DAG)             [NOT STARTED]
==============================================================================
Currently every ingester is an idempotent CLI. To get to BBG-terminal
"it is just there" UX, we need a DAG layer above them. Concrete plan:

[ ] DAG-1  Choose orchestrator. Decision matrix:

       Dagster (RECOMMENDED)
         + "Software-defined assets" model -- each raw.* table and
           derived.* view is a first-class @asset with declared
           upstream dependencies and freshness policies. Asset graph
           UI is a near-perfect BBG-equivalent for "what is fresh,
           what is stale, what failed."
         + Native Postgres-backed event store, runs alongside our DB.
         + Sensors and schedules first-class.
         + Free tier (Dagster OSS, self-hosted) covers our scale.
         - Heavier than alternatives; more concepts to learn.

       Prefect 3
         + Lighter, async-native, good local DX.
         + Postgres- or SQLite-backed.
         - Asset model is newer / less mature than Dagster's.

       Plain APScheduler + Postgres job table
         + Zero new dependencies.
         + Total control.
         - Reinvents the asset graph + UI we will eventually want.

       AIRFLOW EXPLICITLY REJECTED:
         - Heavyweight, slow iteration, weak data-asset semantics.

       RECOMMENDATION: start with Dagster OSS. If onboarding cost is
       prohibitive in week one, fall back to Prefect 3.

[ ] DAG-2  ingestion/_assets.py -- one @dagster.asset per raw.* table
       + per derived.* view. Each asset's compute_fn calls the existing
       loader CLI (or its underlying load_to_postgres function) so the
       orchestrator wraps but does not duplicate ingester logic.

[ ] DAG-3  release_calendar table + seed
       (source_id, expected_release_dow_or_dom, expected_time_local,
        timezone, notes).
       BLS CPI: 2nd or 3rd Tuesday of month, 08:30 ET.
       FRED MORTGAGE30US: Thursdays, 12:00 ET.
       FHFA HPI: quarterly, last Tuesday of Feb/May/Aug/Nov, 09:00 ET.
       ACS B19013/B25xxx 5-year: annually, December.
       ACS 1-year: annually, September.
       DOL OFLC LCA: quarterly, ~3 months after fiscal-quarter end.
       NJ DCA tax data: annually, ~January.
       USAspending: daily incrementals (when we add it).

[ ] DAG-4  Schedules + sensors:
       * One Dagster ScheduleDefinition per release-calendar entry.
       * One AssetSensor per (source -> downstream-asset) edge that
         re-materializes derived assets when upstream materializes.
       * One FreshnessPolicy per asset declaring maximum_lag_minutes;
         violations fire to governance.dataset_health AND emit a
         Slack/email alert.

[ ] DAG-5  Quality gates as Dagster AssetChecks:
       * row count within historical 95% CI (catches truncated loads)
       * key columns null-rate within tolerance
       * cross-source consistency (e.g. derived.cpi_u_annual matches
         BLS-published M13 within 0.01)
       Failed checks block downstream materialization.

[ ] DAG-6  Operator-staged sources (HUD) get a "manual fulfilment"
       asset: the sensor watches data/manual/<source>/ for new files,
       triggers the loader on detection, and the asset materializes.

[ ] DAG-7  Dagster instance config -> Postgres run/event store
       (same DB as data, separate schema dagster_meta).
       docker-compose.yml updates to bring up Dagster webserver +
       daemon alongside the existing Postgres service.

[ ] DAG-8  Notification channels:
       * Slack webhook for failures + freshness-policy violations
       * Email for "weekly platform digest" (counts of materializations,
         alerts, anomalies)

[ ] DAG-9  /release-calendar API endpoint (Tier 5)
       Equivalent of BBG ECO <GO>: "what releases in the next 14 days,
       what is overdue, when is each next expected."

Given the BBG mental model, the previously-vague Tier 5 sharpens:

[DONE] BBG-LIKE-1   `nj <county> <metric>` CLI shortcut, BBG-style
                 ("BERGEN BURDEN <GO>" -> latest value + sparkline).

                 Shipped via serving/terminal_nj.py: the `nj` relay
                 recognizes a county-as-first-token form and forwards
                 ``nj BERGEN BURDEN`` -> ``nj-terminal burden BERGEN``.
                 cmd_burden produces the BBG-style latest-value +
                 sparkline output; BBG-LIKE-2 (below) layers an
                 as-of footer on top.

                 [DONE 2026-05-04 part 2 -- HPI + INCOME slice]:
                 Metric vocabulary expanded to {burden, pums, pums-burden,
                 acs-burden, acs, tabular, hpi, prices, fhfa, income, mhi,
                 acs-income} via _METRIC_TO_SUBCOMMAND. Two new CLI commands
                 (cmd_hpi, cmd_income) wrap the existing SQL functions:
                   * `nj BERGEN HPI`    -> derived.f_fhfa_hpi_indexed(2000)
                                           (FHFA all-transactions HPI,
                                            base_year-normalized to 2000=100)
                   * `nj BERGEN INCOME` -> derived.f_acs_mhi_real(<auto>)
                                           (ACS B19013 CPI-deflated to the
                                            most recent year for which both
                                            ACS dollar_year and CPI exist)
                 Both commands mirror cmd_burden's shape:
                   - text mode: latest-value table + sparkline + as-of footer
                   - --json mode: GET /hpi/{fips}/series and /income/{fips}/
                                  series parity with `provenance` enrichment
                   - DSN-guard parity, COUNTY usage error parity
                   - Click-level base-year and product validation (no DB hit)

                 Two new FastAPI routes (serving/routes/hpi_income.py):
                   * GET /hpi/{county_fips}/series?base_year=2000
                   * GET /income/{county_fips}/series?product=acs5&base_year=
                 Both: 400 on malformed FIPS / bad product, 404 on no rows,
                 422 on out-of-range base_year (FastAPI Query ge/le).
                 Wired into serving/app.py.

                 New Pydantic models: HpiCountyRow, IncomeCountyRow.
                 New query helpers: list_hpi_county_series,
                 list_income_county_series, resolve_default_income_base_year
                 (the latter picks LEAST(max(dollar_year), max(cpi_year))
                 so the income series defaults to today's dollars without
                 a magic number; returns None when the substrate is empty,
                 in which case the route 404s and the CLI errors with a
                 pointer to --base-year).

                 16 new tests in test_terminal_cli.py + 7 new in
                 test_serving.py (live-PG-skip when PG_TEST_DSN unset):
                   - hpi/income --json: full payload incl. provenance
                   - hpi/income --json: empty-series mirrors []

                     (raw.fhfa_hpi_county / raw.acs_median_household_income)
                   - hpi text: empty-series errors out (analyst-grade)
                   - hpi/income: PG_DSN guard parity
                   - hpi/income: missing-COUNTY usage error parity
                   - hpi: --base-year out-of-range rejected by Click
                   - income: --base-year skips resolver when set
                   - income: resolver returning None -> hard error
                   - income: --product invalid rejected by Click
                   - nj relay: county-then-metric routing for hpi/prices/
                     fhfa and income/mhi/acs-income aliases
                   - HTTP 400/422/200 contract for both routes
                   - HTTP shape: every documented column present, NJ filter

                 CLI version bumped 0.33.0 -> 0.34.0.

                 DCA property-tax-only series at the same pattern.

TIER 2 SUBSTRATE CHECKPOINT (2026-04-28 evening, third pass)

House-price index (FHFA repeat-sales) and FRED rate panel both ingested
and validated against live public data. The headline burden ratio now
has 5 of its 6 inputs in place; the only remaining piece is the ACS
housing-cost numerator, which has been built but not yet live-validated
against Census (Census api.census.gov was returning 503 across the

exponential-backoff retry and is fully unit-tested via mocks).

Real numbers from FHFA HPI (NJ counties, indexed to 2010 = 100):
  Cape May    +99.6  (Jersey Shore boom; nearly doubled in 14 years)
  Hudson      +96.7  (gentrification + transit-adjacent demand)
  Ocean       +89.8
  Monmouth    +84.9
  ...
  Hunterdon   +51.3  (slowest growth -- formerly wealthiest county)
  Morris      +57.2  (commuter belt lagging the shore + city)

Real numbers from FRED (annual averages):
  year  30y mortgage   10y Treasury   Fed Funds
  2010      4.69            3.22         0.17
  2021      2.96            1.45         0.08    <-- COVID-era trough
  2024      6.72            4.21         5.14    <-- normalized rates

Cross-source insight: the 2021 mortgage rate floor (2.96%) vs 2024
(6.72%) is +127% in monthly P+I cost on the same nominal home price,
BEFORE any HPI growth. Burden-ratio counterfactuals against this
substrate become very legible.

  Real-dollar 2010 -> 2022 NJ county MHI deltas (top + bottom):
    Hudson        +17.1%  ($74,185 -> $86,854 in $2022)

Working-class + gentrifying counties gained real income; rural and

REAL-DATA VALIDATION CHECKPOINT (2026-04-28 evening, first pass)
----------------------------------------------------------------

REAL-DATA VALIDATION CHECKPOINT (2026-04-28 evening)
----------------------------------------------------
The DOL OFLC LCA loader has been validated end-to-end against the actual
133-MiB FY2024 Q3 disclosure file (216,470 rows). Three real-data quirks
not present in synthetic fixtures were caught and fixed:

  1. Polars `read_excel` engine="calamine" actually requires the
     `fastexcel` package, NOT `python-calamine`. Dependency was wrong.
  2. fastexcel/calamine renders integer-valued numeric cells as "1.0",
     which Postgres' COPY rejects against INTEGER columns. Loader now
     splits _NUMERIC_COLS into _FLOAT_COLS + _INT_COLS with explicit
     round+cast for the integer path.
  3. DOL renamed several columns at form-revision time:
       - 2018 LCA form: EMPLOYMENT_START_DATE -> BEGIN_DATE,
                        EMPLOYMENT_END_DATE   -> END_DATE,
                        TOTAL_WORKERS         -> TOTAL_WORKER_POSITIONS
       - FY2023 release: EMPLOYER_NAICS_CODE  -> NAICS_CODE
     v3/v4/v5 mappings + v5 signature were silently using the old
     names; live data therefore landed with NULL employment dates +
     NULL NAICS until corrected. Per-vintage mappings now diverge
     explicitly and date columns are no longer in `_BASE_V2PLUS`.

Real numbers from the rebuilt platform on the real Q3 FY2024 file:
  Total LCA rows loaded:           216,470
  CERTIFIED rows:                  203,176 (94%)
  DENIED rows:                       1,960 (1%)
  H-1B share of CERTIFIED:           98.4%
  NJ CERTIFIED H-1B (FY2024 Q3):    12,108
  NJ H-1B annualized wage_from:
    p25                            $82,500
    p50 (median)                  $103,730
    p75                           $121,285

This is the headline empirical finding the platform is designed to
support: per-county wage distributions for visa-classified workers,
which the affordability layer will use to attribute housing-burden
shifts to specific labor inflows.

ALSO LANDED: `nj-ingest-lca fetch --fiscal-year YYYY --fiscal-quarter Q`
auto-fetches the static DOL CDN URL (FY2018+ only). HUD remains
operator-staged because huduser.gov requires a registered login.


PLATFORM SCOPE NOTE
-------------------
Two product surfaces:
  A. Housing affordability  -- the headline `idea` spec. Counterfactual /
     vintaged tax simulation, time-consistent housing indices, true
     disposable income, NJ counties first then statewide.
  B. Civic-integrity / fraud detection -- state, federal, and education
     spending substrate; libel-safe (detectors emit evidence, never verdicts).

Both surfaces share Tier 0 (substrate), Tier 1 (raw ingesters), and Tier 2
(derived metrics + governance). Surface-specific work is in Tier 3+.

REBUILD CONTEXT (read first if you are looking at the tree fresh)
-----------------------------------------------------------------
The substrate in this tree was rebuilt from `idea` on 2026-04-28 after the
prior session's source files were lost from the local filesystem (only
.pyc bytecode and the `idea` spec survived). What was rebuilt -- and what
was DELIBERATELY deferred -- is enumerated below. The rebuild is
methodologically equivalent to the prior tree but takes several
opportunities to redesign:

  * SQL-side wage annualization (raw.lca_disclosure GENERATED columns)
    instead of Python-side, so the conversion rule is auditable in psql.
  * Schema-version detection by canonical column-set fingerprint with
    forbidden_columns disambiguation, instead of sentinel-column
    detection.
  * polars-backed loader instead of pandas (lower memory, native Excel
    via calamine).
  * Strict `ref.zip_county` ratio-sum CONSTRAINT TRIGGER so a partial
    HUD load fails at COMMIT.
  * Migration runner that detects post-deployment edits via sha256
    drift in `governance.schema_migrations`.

TIER 0 -- SUBSTRATE                                                  [DONE]

[X] pyproject.toml, .gitignore, .python-version, Makefile, README.md
[X] db/migrations/001_initial_schema.sql
        Schemas: ref, raw, derived, governance
        Tables:  ref.state, ref.county, ref.formula_version,
                 ref.suppression_threshold,
                 governance.schema_migrations,

[X] FRAUD-F1-signal donor_employed_by_nj_contractor cross-source signal
            (shipped 2026-05-04). SECOND cross-source signal in the
            fraud engine; FIRST one that joins on corporate identity
            rather than person identity.
            Migration 056 ships:
              derived.f_canonical_employer_name(text)
                -- mirrors ingestion._base.canonical_employer_name
                   (Python). Lowercase + strip business-entity suffixes
                   (LLC, INC, CORP, ...) + collapse non-alphanumeric
                   to single space + trim. IMMUTABLE + STRICT.
              derived.refresh_signal_donor_employed_by_nj_contractor(cycle)
                -- DELETE-then-INSERT idempotent on the (cycle, signal_id
                   ='donor_employed_by_nj_contractor') slice. Joins
                   raw.fec_contribution.employer x
                   derived.v_usaspending_award_active.recipient_name on
                   canonical-employer key. raw_value =
                   SUM(GREATEST(transaction_amt, 0)) (positive donations
                   only). memo_cd='X' rows excluded (FEC double-count
                   convention). peer_percentile = CUME_DIST() within
                   the matched-clusters set. severity=3 (HIGH).
            Dagster asset: derived.signal_donor_employed_by_nj_contractor
              deps = (raw.fec_contribution, raw.usaspending_award)
              automation_condition = DERIVED_AUTOMATION (eager).
            Asset check: match_rate_in_plausible_range
              fails if both raw tables non-empty but 0 matches
              (canonicalizer regression) OR matches > 1% of distinct
              FEC employer count (over-matching).
            entity_kind='donor_cluster'; entity_id = canonical
              employer name. The L3a v_entity_fraud_risk view picks
              this up automatically.
            DELIBERATE LIMITATIONS (substrate-honesty):
              * One signal per (cycle, employer); does not aggregate
                across cycles. A multi-cycle "persistent contractor
                donor" pattern is a separate downstream view.
              * No candidate-side projection (which CANDIDATE got the
                money from these employees). That is the natural
                follow-up signal `candidate_funded_by_nj_contractor_
                employees` and depends on this signal's row set.
              * No UEI join. FEC has no UEIs; canonical-name is the
                only available cross-source primitive.
              * No stop list of "RETIRED" / "SELF" / "NONE" -- these
                self-filter because no real contractor is named
                that. Documented in the migration so a future
                regression that introduces a contractor named
                "Self Employed Consulting" is treated as a real
                overlap, not a false positive.
              * Inactive USAspending rows (last_seen_at >35 days
                stale) drop out of the contractor set; their donor
                clusters un-match. This is correct behavior, not a
                limitation -- but worth flagging so the operator
                doesn't think a missing cluster is a refresher bug.
            Tests: 21 live-pg. Pure SQL canonicalizer (case, suffix
              variants, punctuation, NULL/empty, distinct-name
              non-collapse), refresher integration (idempotency,
              cycle isolation, match cardinality, memo_cd filtering,
              negative-amount filtering, percentile correctness,
              empty-side handling, inactive-row exclusion, evidence
              URL shape, multi-variant aggregation).
            Total assets: 24. Total asset checks: 41.
            NEXT (separate session, in priority order):
              1. candidate_funded_by_nj_contractor_employees
                 -- candidate-side projection that joins this
                    signal's matched donors back to FEC committee
                    contribution targets, surfacing candidates who
                    received money from contractor-employed donors.
              2. entity_funded_and_excluded
                 -- USAspending recipient x LEIE individual on
                    canonical name. Should be empty in steady state
                    (a contractor named on LEIE = critical

              3. FRAUD-F2 SAM.gov entities + exclusions

[X] FRAUD-F1-signal-cand candidate_funded_by_nj_contractor_employees
            (shipped 2026-05-04). THIRD cross-source signal in the
            fraud engine; candidate-side projection of the donor-side
            FRAUD-F1 signal. Answers the canonical analyst question:
            "which CANDIDATES received money from federal-contractor
            employees?"
            Migration 057 ships:
              derived.refresh_signal_candidate_funded_by_nj_contractor_employees(cycle)
                -- Reads matched-employer set from L1 (signal 056's
                   rows) so it cannot drift from 056's keying. Joins
                   raw.fec_contribution -> raw.fec_committee on
                   (cycle, cmte_id), then -> raw.fec_candidate on
                   (cycle, cand_id) WHERE cmte.cand_id IS NOT NULL
                   (principal/authorized committees only). Sums
                   transaction_amt > 0, memo_cd != 'X' (FEC double-
                   count exclusion). peer_percentile = CUME_DIST per
                   (cand_office, cand_office_st) bucket. severity=3.
            Dagster asset:
              derived.signal_candidate_funded_by_nj_contractor_employees
              deps = (derived.signal_donor_employed_by_nj_contractor,
                      raw.fec_committee, raw.fec_candidate,
                      raw.fec_contribution).
              The dep on signal 056 enforces refresh ordering: the
              candidate-side cannot run before the donor-side has
              produced its L1 rows.
            Asset check: match_rate_in_plausible_range (WARN). Bounds:
              0 < n_matched <= 50% of "active candidate cohort"
              (= candidates whose principal/authorized committees
              received any positive contributions in the cycle).
              Higher ceiling than donor-side (1%) because in a
              presidential cycle the majority of active candidates
              legitimately receive at least some money from
              contractor-employed donors -- federal contractors
              employ hundreds of thousands of people.
            entity_kind='candidate'; entity_id = FEC cand_id.
              The L3a v_entity_fraud_risk view picks this up
              automatically and rolls the contribution into the
              composite risk_score for the candidate (which already
              has structural FEC signals + entity_on_leie matches
              feeding into it).
            DELIBERATE LIMITATIONS (substrate-honesty):
              * Joint Fundraising Committee / Leadership PAC unrolling
                NOT done. Contributions to JFCs map to candidates via
                a separate FEC linkage (fec_ccl) we have not yet
                ingested. We join only on cmte.cand_id (principal +
                authorized). Documented; not a correctness bug.
              * Independent expenditures (Super PAC ads supporting a
                candidate without coordination) are NOT attributed
                here. Those are technically not "contributions to the
                candidate" in the strict campaign-finance sense and
                belong in a separate signal (`independent_expenditures
                _supporting_candidate`).
              * No sub-cycle time slicing. The signal sums across the
                entire cycle. A future iteration may flag "money
                concentrated in the 30 days before a key procurement
                vote" once roll-call data is ingested.
              * Multi-cycle aggregation deferred. The "candidate
                persistently funded across N cycles" pattern is a
                downstream view consuming this signal.
            Tests: 12 live-pg.
              End-to-end happy path; zero-result when 056 not yet
              refreshed; non-candidate committees (cand_id NULL) do
              NOT credit a candidate; multi-committee per candidate
              aggregates correctly; memo_cd='X' filtered;
              negative-amount filtered; idempotency; cycle isolation;
              per-(office, state) bucketing; empty matched set;
              evidence URL shape; unmatched donor employer.
            Total assets: 25. Total asset checks: 42.
[X] FRAUD-F1+F5 entity_funded_and_excluded cross-source signal
            (shipped 2026-05-04). FOURTH cross-source signal in the
            fraud engine; INTERSECTION of FRAUD-F1 substrate
            (USAspending federal awards) and FRAUD-F5 substrate
            (HHS-OIG LEIE individual exclusions). The single
            highest-leverage signal on the platform: a federally-
            excluded individual receiving federal contracts is, by
            definition, a procurement-process failure (FAR
            exclusion-check requirements were not enforced) and
            potentially active fraud (laundering federal money
            through a shell to bypass exclusion).
            Migration 058 ships:
              ALTER derived.fraud_signal_observation_entity_kind_check
                -- extends the whitelist from five FEC-domain kinds
                ('committee', 'candidate', 'treasurer', 'address',
                 'donor_cluster') to six by adding 'contractor'.
              derived.refresh_signal_entity_funded_and_excluded(cycle)
                -- One row per canonical "LAST|FIRST" key that
                appears as BOTH an active USAspending NJ-pop
                recipient (via derived.v_usaspending_award_active.
                recipient_canonical_individual, NULL for corporate
                recipients) AND an active LEIE individual (via
                derived.v_leie_individual_canonical.canonical_key).
                raw_value = SUM(GREATEST(award_amount, 0)) across
                all the matched person's contracts in the active
                window. severity=5 CRITICAL on every match.
                peer_bucket='kind=contractor', peer_percentile is
                rate-based (1 - n_flagged / n_in_bucket) within
                distinct individual recipients. cycle is the
                analyst-session label (USAspending data is not
                FEC-cycle-bound; mirrors how entity_on_leie binds
                treasurer matches to a cycle).
                LEIE-side is pre-collapsed via DISTINCT ON
                canonical_key picking the freshest exclusion
                (most-recent excldate) so a person re-excluded
                under multiple authorities still produces one
                signal row -- no award double-counting.
                evidence_url shape:
                  /fec/risk/entities/contractor/<canonical_individual>
                    ?signal=entity_funded_and_excluded
                    &cycle=<cycle>
                    &leie=<freshest_record_hash>
                with the canonical-key '|' separator collapsed to
                '_' for analyst URL clarity.
              derived.signal_entity_funded_and_excluded Dagster asset
                -- declares deps on raw.usaspending_award and
                raw.hhs_oig_leie ONLY (no L1-on-L1 dependency,
                unlike the candidate-side signal which depends on
                its donor-side counterpart). The shared L1 table is
                sliced by (cycle, signal_id), so concurrent
                refreshers writing to disjoint slices are safe.
              derived.v_entity_fraud_risk gains
                signal_entity_funded_and_excluded as a fifth
                upstream dep, ensuring the L3a fingerprint changes
                whenever this signal refreshes.
              Asset check match_rate_in_plausible_range (WARN):
                INTENTIONALLY DIFFERENT from the other cross-source
                signal checks. Zero matches is the EXPECTED healthy
                state (federal procurement should never pay LEIE-
                excluded individuals); the check passes on zero.
                The check WARNs only when matches exceed 1% of the
                active individual-recipient bucket, which would
                indicate canonicalization over-matching rather than
                real mass procurement fraud.
            Deliberately out of scope (documented in migration 058
            header for future-session traceability):
              * Business-side matching (LEIE busname x USAspending
                corporate recipient_name) -- LEIE businesses are
                healthcare-org-shaped and USAspending NJ recipients
                are defense/services-corp-shaped; canonical-name
                matching across them has high false-positive risk
                and lower expected hit rate. The natural fix is
                FRAUD-F2 SAM.gov UEI canonicalization (UEI is the
                federal canonical entity identifier).
              * Address / DOB filtering. Strict on canonicalization,
                loose on demographics in v1; tightened once L5
                labels exist.
              * Authorized-representative matching. Cross-checking
                corporate principals against LEIE requires SAM.gov
                POC fields, deferred to FRAUD-F2.
              * Multi-source authority list combining (LEIE +
                SAM.gov + GSA debarments). One source at a time;
                SAM.gov is the next slice.
            Tests: 13 live-pg.
              Schema: 'contractor' entity_kind accepted; unknown
              entity_kind still rejected (defense against accidental
              CHECK relaxation); end-to-end happy path with full
              schema validation; corporate recipient (no comma) does
              not fire; no LEIE match -> 0 rows; multi-award
              aggregation; multi-LEIE record collapse picks freshest
              exclusion; award double-count safety under multi-LEIE;
              idempotency; cycle isolation; percentile grows with
              bucket size (1-of-10 -> 0.9); empty bucket returns 0
              with no error; evidence URL well-formed.
            Total assets: 26. Total asset checks: 43.
[X] FRAUD-F5c donor_on_leie cross-source signal
            (shipped 2026-05-04). FIFTH cross-source signal in the
            fraud engine; completes the contractor/donor x LEIE 2x2
            matrix. Joins active raw.fec_contribution donors against
            active LEIE individual exclusions on canonical
            "LAST|FIRST" key. Distinct from signal_entity_on_leie
            (which matches FEC ENTITIES -- candidates and
            treasurers); donor_on_leie matches the third-party-donor
            population, which is orders of magnitude larger and
            demographically different.
            Migration 059 ships:
              ALTER derived.fraud_signal_observation_entity_kind_check
                -- extends the whitelist from six kinds (after 058)
                to seven by adding 'donor'. 'donor_cluster' (groups
                of donors at one employer, from migration 056)
                stays separate -- a single-person donor identity is
                a different entity shape than a workforce
                aggregation. The L2 pivot keeps them as separate
                row groups so the L3a queue surfaces them
                distinctly.
              derived.refresh_signal_donor_on_leie(cycle)
                -- One row per matched canonical donor key per
                cycle. raw_value = SUM(GREATEST(transaction_amt, 0))
                across the donor's contributions in the cycle,
                excluding memo_cd=X (FEC double-counts). The
                positive-only SUM clips refunds; an aggregated_active
                CTE drops donors whose net-positive giving is zero
                so the analyst queue stays actionable.
                LEIE-side pre-collapsed via DISTINCT ON canonical_key
                ORDER BY excldate DESC NULLS LAST to pick the
                freshest exclusion per person, mirroring migration
                058's pattern.
                severity=5 CRITICAL on every match. peer_bucket=
                'kind=donor', peer_percentile rate-based
                (1 - n_flagged / n_in_bucket). cycle is the FEC
                contribution cycle (this signal IS cycle-bound,
                unlike entity_funded_and_excluded).
                evidence_url shape:
                  /fec/risk/entities/donor/<canonical_key>
                    ?signal=donor_on_leie
                    &cycle=<cycle>
